Only one preview tab exists; clicking again focuses it. Opening it closes the enlarged-view window, and clicking the dock image focuses the tab. You can subsequently move or merge the tab like other tabs, but it never becomes the editing document. Its context menu only offers Close and Move to the other pane; it is not reopened at startup. Closing all document tabs also closes it. Drag the divider between Preview and Tool options to resize their heights; the split height persists across launches.

Reopening at the next launch

Tabs open when you quit are reopened at the next launch, including the split, their order, the selected tab of each pane and the focused pane. Missing or unreadable files, never-saved new documents and the preview tab are skipped.

Timeline

In the timeline at the bottom of the screen, rows are layers, columns are frames and each cell is a cel. Tags appear in the strip at the top. The frame and layer you click become the current frame and layer shown on the canvas. For selecting see Selecting in the timeline; for moving and copying see Editing the timeline.

Layer rows

Layer rows have ① an eye (visibility) and ② a lock icon and the name. If opacity isn't 100%, ③ a percentage follows the name. Groups collapse and expand with a ④ triangle, and their ⑤ parts appear indented below (with their size next to the name).

Alt+click an eye to hide every other layer and keep only that one, or Alt+click a lock to lock every other layer (clicking a group keeps its parts; clicking a part keeps its group too). Alt+click again when that is already the state to make all layers visible or unlocked again. Each is one undo step.

Layers with effects get an fx badge; click it to expand or collapse the effect rows below (Layer effects). Groups using auto layout get a diamond badge that opens the auto layout settings, and clicking a group's or part's skin chip opens the skin manager. The canvas pivot's representative layer (see Pivot) has a small crown before its name.

You can't draw on a locked layer, but you can draw on a hidden one. A hidden normal layer appears semi-transparently on the canvas only while editing and doesn't appear in the preview or exports.

Frame columns and durations

Each frame column header shows ⑥ its number and duration (ms), and each cel shows ⑦ a thumbnail of the editable art without effects.

Double-click a frame number or choose Duration… from the header's right-click menu to open the frame duration dialog: 1–60000 ms (quick picks 50, 80, 100, 120, 150, 200). With several frames selected, all change together. Frames in a new project are 100 ms, and newly added frames take the duration of the frame before them.

A normal layer's cel thumbnails crop to the painted area across all frames (within the canvas), using a fixed scale and position so movement is visible. They show editable art without layer effects or effect padding. Only cels whose effects generate an image despite having no painted pixels (such as text) display their effects. Group rows combine visible parts in position (without part or group effects) and crop to the union of part bounds; part rows use the part's bounds. Empty normal layers use the whole canvas. While drawing, the old crop remains until the stroke ends. Empty cels in other skins do not display first-skin art.

Tag strip, canvas size chip and playback marker

  • ⑧ The tag strip shows groups of frames as colored brackets (Tags).
  • The canvas chip in the top-left corner (left of the frame-number row) has two parts. Click the icon on the left to open the same canvas size mode as Edit > Canvas size… (Ctrl+Alt+C). Click the Canvas W×H text, which shows the current canvas (frame) size (such as Canvas 64×48, updated as soon as the size changes), to switch to the pivot tool with the canvas pivot (sheet and Unity export pivot) as its target; the text gets a highlight border while it is. Click a layer name or select another layer to edit layer pivots again. The icon and the text each have their own tooltip.
  • While the preview plays, the frame column being shown gets a ⑬ green outline.
  • When effects take a while to compute, thumbnails of frames being recomputed get a gray veil. On heavy images, dragging an effect box's range moves only the box, and the canvas updates when you release.

Right-click menus

There are no separate tool buttons; everything is in the right-click menu. The top of the menu shows ⑭ what's selected (e.g. "Frames 1–3 selected").

Where you right-clickMain menu items
⑮ Frame headerCreate tag, Empty frame after (Alt+N), Duplicate frame (Alt+D), Delete, Move forward/back, Duration…, Copy frames (with links), Copy frames — images only (no links), Cut/Paste frames, Reset pivot to default, Use a separate pivot for this frame only, Export cell name…
⑯ CelClear cels, Copy image from previous frame (one empty cel), Copy cels (with links), Copy cels — images only (no links), Cut cels, Copy cels (as image with effects), Copy frame N as shown, Paste cels, Link cels / Unlink cels, Add effect…, Create tag, Empty frame after, Duplicate frame, Duration…, pivot and export cell name
⑰ Layer nameRename, Opacity… (0–255), Effects…, Expand/Collapse effect rows, Show/hide, Lock/unlock, New layer/group/part, Duplicate, Move up/down, Merge down (when the layer below is a normal layer), Delete, Use as canvas pivot representative (or Stop using as canvas pivot representative if it already is; one undo step). Normal layers also have Add skin (turns into a group); groups have Add part, Expand/Collapse, Auto layout and Skin; parts have Part size, Position, Skin for this part and Group skin (Parts chapter).
⑱ Empty area (such as the top-left cell)New layer (Shift+N), New group, New part (Shift+P), Empty frame at end, Copy last frame to end
TagTag properties…, Play this tag, Delete (Tags)
Effect rowEffect settings…, change the range (To all frames, This frame only, From this frame to end, From start to this frame, Extend to here), Turn off/on, Delete, Apply effect permanently, Expand canvas… (grow the canvas to fit that effect's result; see Effect settings), Copy/Paste effects (Layer effects)

The pivot and cell name items at the end of the frame header and cel menus apply to the right-clicked frame, not the whole selection. The pivot items (Reset pivot to default, Use a separate pivot for this frame only) apply to the current pivot target (the current layer, or the canvas pivot after you click the canvas chip's text). Items that don't apply are grayed out.

Adding frames and layers

  • After the last frame: ⑨ plus in the header adds a blank frame, ⑩ copy below duplicates the last frame. Press and release for one; drag right and release to add as many as the covered slots (move left to reduce, minimum one). A pale blue numbered preview appears while dragging and the view autoscrolls at the right edge. Esc cancels; releasing commits one undo step and selects the last new frame. Copies of linked cels join their source link groups.
  • Row below the last layer: the whole name cell is a ⑪ plus button = add an empty layer at the bottom; the whole frame area is one ⑫ copy button (icon every 10 frames) = add a copy of the bottom layer at the bottom (a group comes with its parts).

Drag ⑲ the Add frame button to the right to preview how many numbered frames will be appended when you release.

Drag the Add blank frame button in the header several slots right to preview the new frames and their numbers in blue; release to append that many blank frames. The Copy last frame button in a cel row works the same way, repeatedly copying the last frame.

An orange diamond at the top left of a cel means that layer has its own pivot in that frame (for a part, its position too). Part cels always show it; normal layers and groups show it only on the current layer's row. A diamond on a frame header appears only while you edit the canvas pivot (after clicking the canvas chip's text), on frames where the canvas pivot is set separately. See Pivot for setting pivots per frame.

Compact view and scrolling

Switch off ⑳ frame previews to make ㉑ the cels narrower and lower, with only a white dot where art exists.

Toggle frame previews with the image button at the right end of the timeline header (on initially; persists across launches). Off makes frame columns and layer rows compact: a white dot marks a cel with art, empty cels stay blank (group dots mean any part has art). Headers show numbers only; linked cels keep the colored strip but not the chain icon. Selection, dragging and the next-frame column still work. Drag the boundary between layer names and frame cels to resize the names between 140 and 600 px (or half the timeline); double-click resets it to 270 px. The width is remembered.

The horizontal scrollbar sits above the grid, right below the timeline title bar, and spans only the frame columns (so it stays clear of the add-layer row and copy buttons at the bottom). The wheel scrolls layer rows up and down; Shift+wheel or Ctrl+wheel scrolls frame columns sideways. Dragging a selection or a move to an edge scrolls automatically. Timeline cells can't be zoomed.

Selecting in the timeline

In the timeline you select one kind of thing at a time (frames, layers, cels or effects). What you click becomes the current frame and layer, and the selected kind decides what Delete, copy and paste do.

How to select

Click a ① frame header to select that frame, a layer name for that layer, or a ② cel for that cell; the selection gets a blue border. Click an effect in an expanded effect row to select that effect.

ActionResult
DragSelects everything the rectangle touches.
Shift+clickExtends the selection to a range up to the clicked item.
Ctrl (macOS ⌘)+clickAdds or removes one item at a time, even when not adjacent. Pressing inside selected cels with Ctrl held and dragging copies them instead (see Editing the timeline).
Click an empty areaClears the selection.
Click the eye, lock, collapse triangle or fxOnly does the icon's own job; doesn't select.

The ③ status bar shows what's selected and what Delete will do.

Keyboard

After clicking the timeline, ←→ go to the adjacent frame and ↑↓ to the adjacent layer (stopping at the first and last). Moving left/right with frames selected, or up/down with layers selected, keeps that kind; moving the other way switches to cel selection. Shift+arrow extends the selection.

F2 = rename the current layer, Delete = delete what is selected (also Backspace on macOS; if the canvas has a selection, only its pixels are cleared), Esc = cancel a drag. For frame navigation shortcuts see Timeline shortcuts.

How multiple selections look

④ The clicked anchor has a dark border; ⑤ the neighboring and ⑥ separately selected cels have pale blue backgrounds.

With multiple cels selected, the clicked anchor has a dark blue border; others have a pale blue fill and a faint border around each contiguous block. Ctrl/⌘+click adds or removes individual cels even when separated, and touching cells share one block border. Copy and cut cels do not work on disjoint selections assembled with Ctrl/⌘+click; the status bar explains why.

Linked cels

Cel links make several cels of a layer share one image. Editing one cel changes every linked cel, so use links to edit a drawing that repeats across frames in one go.

Each cel in ① the link group has a chain; the same-color strip continues under adjacent linked cels.

Linking cels

Right-click ② chain-marked cels to use ③ Link cels or Unlink cels.

Right-click selected cels and choose Link cels to share one image per layer, even across disjoint frames and rows. The dark-bordered clicked cel is the source if it has art; if already linked the others join its group. If empty, an already-linked selected group is preferred, then the first selected cel with art. Painting, moving, transforming, filtering or resizing the canvas/layer in one cel updates all linked cels in one undo step. A chain at the cel's lower right and a continuous colored strip under adjacent linked frames show the group. Groups take the first unused color in that row in order (blue, orange, green, pink, yellow, purple, cyan); joining an existing group uses its color. Links and colors are saved in .chickxel; older app versions open them as ordinary cels. AI tools link_cels, unlink_cels and set_link_color manage links and colors, while get_document reports each layer's frames and colors.

Unlinking and what breaks a link

Unlink cels gives each selected cel its own image copy. Delete on a linked cel clears the shared image but leaves the link and color. Timeline paste detaches the pasted cel from its old link, and a linked copy joins the group it was copied from (see Editing the timeline); Copy image from previous frame detaches only that cel. Moving or copying a block of cels by dragging keeps links (the original group within the same row); for unlinked copies, copy with Ctrl+Shift+C and paste. When unlinking a cel whose linked image was showing an animated effect's held end after its range, the currently visible end is baked into its art (enabled effects only, clipped to cel size, ignoring layer visibility/opacity) so it stays visually unchanged. Cels inside an active effect range or with the held-end effect disabled keep an ordinary art copy; an effect reporting movement continues to place the image at its final position, so that movement is not baked and other end-state effects may disappear. Detaching the range's last cel also preserves the remaining linked cels that would lose that end-state; if their appearances differ they split into separate cels. The operation is one undo step. The status bar and AI unlink_cels report both the number unlinked and the number of held appearances baked.

Links on group rows

Frames linked on ④ the group row show matching linked frames and colors in ⑤ both part rows.

Linking multiple frames on a group row creates a matching-color link group in each unlocked part (those frames leave any earlier independent part links). When both group and part rows are selected, group linking takes priority; each part uses its clicked cel or first visible painted cel as source. The group row shows the chain and strip only when every part links the same frames; changing or unlinking a single part removes that group-row indicator for the affected frames. Unlink on the group row unlinks every part at those frames, and the three AI link tools accept group layers. A new empty skin copies the first skin's linked frames and colors but no art; a cloned skin copies both the source skin's art and its links. Part-only skins and skins made by converting a normal layer also copy link structure; subsequent link edits are independent per skin.

Editing the timeline

Delete, reorder, move and copy the frames, layers, cels and effects selected in the timeline. Every edit is a single undo step.

To do thisHow
DeleteSelect, then Delete. Frames → delete frames, layers → delete layers (groups include their parts), cels → clear the image only, effects → delete the effects. You can't delete every frame or layer.
ReorderDrag an already selected frame header or layer name. Other columns/rows move aside to make room; Esc cancels. Parts move only within the same group.
Move or copy a block of cels① Select several cells, then click inside and drag to move; hold Ctrl (or Alt) when dropping to copy. Pressing inside selected cels with Ctrl held starts a copy drag right away (copy cursor), even with a single cel selected; releasing without dragging adds or removes that cel like Ctrl+click. Both moves and copies keep links, as with linked copies below. Cels can't move to rows of a different size or type.
Copy and pasteCtrl+C or Ctrl+X, then Ctrl+V (or ② copy, cut, paste in the right-click menu). Cels overwrite the same shape with the clicked cell as top-left, frames are pasted as new frames after the selected frame, and layers as new layers above the original. Copies keep cel links; to copy only the images without links, use Ctrl+Shift+C (see Linked copies and images-only copies below). Cels picked one by one with Ctrl+click across gaps can't be copied or cut.
Bring in the previous frame's imageRight-click an ③ empty cel → ④ "Copy image from previous frame"
Change duration or nameDouble-click a frame number = frame duration; double-click a layer name or F2 = rename in place (Enter confirms, Esc cancels)

New layers created by copying get their trailing number increased by 1 (item_1 → item_2, tile_009 → tile_010, or _1 if there's no number). Duplicate in the layer menu follows the same rule. Every edit is a single undo step.

Linked copies and images-only copies

Copying or cutting cels or frames in the timeline carries their link groups along. Paste into the same row (same layer and skin) and the pasted cels join the original group with the same color (blue stays blue, orange stays orange), so editing one changes them all. Paste into another row or document, or after the original group is gone (for example after a cut), and cels from the same group form a new group. The new group keeps the original color unless another group in that row already uses it, in which case it takes the first unused color. A single cel from a group is pasted without a link. If the paste target is a linked cel of another group, only that cel leaves its group. Dragging a block of cels to move or copy it follows the same rules. Every paste is one undo step.

Copy images only (no links) (Ctrl+Shift+C) copies only the images, without link groups, so pasted cels stand alone. Besides the Edit menu, it's in the cel right-click menu as Copy cels — images only, no links and in the frame header right-click menu as Copy frames — images only, no links. When the timeline doesn't have focus (for example on the canvas), it works like a regular copy.

Copying cels as an image

Right-click ⑤ a cel to see ⑥ commands to copy the selected cels with effects, or the entire frame, as an image.

In a cel's right-click menu, Copy cels as image (with effects) copies only the selected cels at the clicked frame (all parts for a group row), composited as visible with effects, opacity and outfit, excluding hidden layers, into a canvas-sized clipboard image. Copy frame N as image copies the entire composited frame, like Copy image in Preview. Paste into another app or the canvas; this clears the timeline-cel clipboard. A notification appears if no visible image exists.

Groups and Add skin

Hold Shift while dragging a layer name to move it into or out of a group instead of reordering. Right-click a normal layer and choose Add skin (turns into a group) to turn it into a group with a new skin. What each drop spot does and how the art and pivot change are covered in Moving layers into and out of groups.

Tags

A tag is a named frame range that groups an animation (for example idle or run). Tags set the preview playback range, the GIF range, and export cell names and file splits.

Creating a tag

① Select a range of frames and choose ② Create tag from the right-click menu. In the dialog, set the ③ name, ④ frame range, ⑤ playback direction and loop, and ⑥ color.

FieldMeaning (initial value)
③ NameThe tag name; it can't be empty or match another tag. Names such as idle_down, walk_left, run, jump and fall are suggested (initially the first unused one).
④ FramesStart and end frame (inclusive); the end can't come before the start (initially the selected range).
⑤ DirectionForward, Reverse or Ping-pong (bounces back from the end, playing each end frame once) (initially Forward).
⑤ LoopWhen off, the preview plays the tag once and stops (initially on).
⑥ ColorThe tag strip color. Click the swatch or type a HEX value (initially blue, orange, green, red, purple, teal, yellow and gray in turn).

Editing and playing tags

  • Hover near either inner end of a ⑦ tag box until the cursor becomes ↔, then drag to change the range.
  • Right-click a tag for the ⑧ menu with Tag properties… (the same dialog as when creating; double-clicking the tag also opens it), Play this tag and Delete.
  • Clicking a tag selects it (white outline), and the preview plays that tag in its direction (a tag with Loop off plays once and stops). Clicking the frame area outside tags (headers, cels, effect rows or empty space on the tag strip) deselects it, and the preview loops all frames again.

Tag names are used for By tag cell names and per-tag files in Export, and for the range in GIF export. Tags are saved in the .chickxel file.

Layer effects

Layer effects are non-destructive: they are applied only when the layer is displayed, without changing cel pixels. Stack as many as you like on each layer — from looks such as outlines and shadows to animated touches such as hit flashes, shakes and explosions — and turn them off, change or remove them at any time. They apply automatically to anything you draw later, and every effect is drawn per pixel (no blur or anti-aliasing).

Use them to put an outline or shadow around a whole character at once, to add a flash or blink on just a few frames (such as the moment of a hit), to make a recolored version of the same drawing (Palette swap), or to put text on the image (Text). When you need to edit the result as pixels, apply the effect permanently.

Basic workflow

  1. In the timeline, right-click a layer name and choose ① Effects…. To add an effect to only some frames, select those cels and choose Add effect… from the right-click menu (the dialog opens with that frame range — see Effect rows in the timeline).
  2. On the Effects tab on the left of the effects dialog, double-click an effect or drag it into the effect list in the middle.
  3. Watch the before/after preview on the right while you change values in the settings, and drag in the frame strip below the preview to set the frames the effect applies to.
  4. Click OK to apply. The layer shows ② fx on the right of its name, and each effect gets an effect row under it in the timeline.

Pages about layer effects

Layer effects are covered on the pages below. If you are new to them, read this page and the effects dialog first and look up the rest when you need it.

PageWhat it covers
Effects dialogLayout of the dialog where you add and edit effects, preview, extending frames, deleting frames in the dialog
Built-in effectsWhat the 23 official effects and the Text effect do, with their main settings
Effect colors and clippingSolid or gradient color settings, clipping to the effect above
Effect range and common settingsEffect range, speed and repeat, moving the pivot along, keeping the end look, effect padding
Effect rows in the timelineAdding effects in the timeline, dragging ranges, moving and copying effects
Effect presetsSaving several effects with their values and inserting them elsewhere in one go
Script effects · Script APIUsing and editing effects written in JavaScript, allowing them to run, the rules for writing scripts
Effect MakerMaking, previewing, checking and publishing new script effects
Asset Manager · Publish onlineGetting effects from the Store, exchanging files, publishing your own effects

Order of effects

Effects are applied from the top of the effect list down, and each effect receives the image with the effects above it already applied. So Outline → Shadow casts the shadow of the outlined shape (left), while Shadow → Outline also draws the outline around the shadow (right). Change the order with the Up and Down buttons in the effects dialog or by dragging effect names in the timeline.

  • Each effect has an on/off checkbox, so you can turn it off for a while without deleting it. A turned-off effect stays in the list and isn't drawn.
  • Effects that draw after all other effects, such as Afterimage, are drawn last regardless of their place in the list.
  • To make an effect draw from only the pixels drawn by the effect above it, turn on clipping (for example, a gradient on the outline only).

Layers, parts and groups

Normal layers and parts apply effects to their own cels; groups apply them to the combined shape of their parts. For example, a group outline is drawn as one line around the whole character, while outlines on each part also draw lines where the parts meet.

Applying permanently

Apply effect permanently bakes the effect into the cel pixels and removes it from the list; you can then edit the result like any drawing. Enabled effects above the selected one are baked too, so the result looks exactly as before. Use the Apply permanently button in the effects dialog or the right-click menu of a timeline effect box; while baking, a Baking effects progress overlay (percentage) appears.

  • Group effects and effects on locked layers can't be applied permanently (for a group, apply them to the parts).
  • Duplicating a layer also copies its effects, and Merge down bakes the upper layer's effects.

Where effects show

  • The canvas shows effects when Show effects on canvas is enabled; preview, frame sheets and part atlases apply effects too. Normal, group and part thumbnails in the timeline show editable art without effects (except text-effect cels with no painted pixels). The selected part view shows its own and its group's effects when enabled, or its plain source cel when disabled.
  • The part atlas includes only the part's own effects; spreading effects such as outlines or shadows make the sprite wider by that amount (the pivot is shifted so it points at the same spot).

Effects this version doesn't know

Effects this version doesn't know (for example, ones added in a newer Chickxel) are not thrown away. Opening such a project shows the notice "Unknown effect (type ID) on layer "…" is not drawn; it is kept as is when you save.", and the effect list shows a dimmed Unknown effect · type ID row (its checkbox is locked). Selecting it only explains that it can't be drawn or edited and Apply permanently is off, but you can still reorder or delete it. Its timeline effect row is gray.

Effects dialog

The effects dialog is where you add effects to one layer, reorder them, change their settings and ranges, and preview the result. Nothing reaches the project until you click OK, and everything you changed is a single undo step.

To open it: right-click a layer name and choose Effects…; select cels and choose Add effect… from the right-click menu (Add range is filled in with those frames); double-click an effect box in the timeline; or click Open in Effects… in the effect group of the tool options in the right dock.

PartWhat it does
⑥ Effects tabEvery effect you add one at a time, in one grid: the official effects the app installs by default (Outline, Shadow and so on) → Text → received script effects (Store or asset files) → script effects you made. Each tile previews the effect on the current image (animated effects play), and effects that came in as assets show the author's name and profile picture under the tile (a person icon when there is no picture). Effects with a description (official effects, Text and script effects that declare one) have a question mark at the top right of the tile; click it for a small window with the effect's name and description (click it again or outside to close). The code mark of non-official script effects sits left of the question mark. Double-click to add at the end of the list, or drag into the effect list to add at that position. Open Store at the bottom opens the Store of the Asset Manager filtered to effects; new effects are made in the Effect Maker.
⑦ Presets tabShows only effect presets (several effects with their values). Insert them the same way as on the Effects tab.
⑧ Add rangeThe effect range for newly added effects: All frames or a start–end frame. Change the range of effects already in the list in the ⑫ frame strip or the timeline.
⑨ Effect listThis layer's effects in the order they are applied. Checkbox = on; the buttons below delete, move up, move down and apply permanently. A script effect that can't draw gets a warning icon at the end of its row.
⑩ SettingsSettings of the selected effect: numbers as a slider and number field, on/off settings as labeled checkboxes, choices as lists and colors as swatches. Below the effect's own settings are common rows such as Apply to, Speed, Repeat, Later effects and Cels after the range. Hover the i marks for explanations. The Common settings (⑬, ⑭) and Settings groups share one fixed-height scroll area, so the window keeps its height when you add or select effects and longer settings scroll inside that area. On a short screen the area opens smaller so the OK and Cancel buttons stay on screen (other OK/Cancel dialogs also never open larger than the screen; when they overflow, only their content scrolls).
⑪ Before/after previewThe current frame before and after effects, with play/pause. Click the preview to open the enlarged view, which has play/pause, previous/next frame, the frame number and the Selected effect range only and Loop end to start checkboxes (they change together with the same checkboxes in the dialog); Space = play/pause, ←/→ = previous/next frame, Esc = close.
⑫ Frame stripClick a cell to preview that frame, or drag to set the selected effect's range. Dragging the end of the range past the last cell adds frames, like ⑭ Extend frames.
⑬ Expand canvas…The effects dialog doesn't grow the canvas by itself on OK or Apply permanently; effect results that go past the canvas are cut off (the parts to be cut are dimmed in the ⑪ before/after preview). To grow it, click this button at the top of the common settings. It measures, on every frame, the result up to the effect selected in the list (the last effect of the group when a group head is selected) with the effects as edited in the dialog, then opens a small window to choose the growth mode (minimal / keep aspect / keep center) and see the new size. While you choose, the before/after preview shows that canvas. Click Expand and On OK W×H → W'×H' appears next to the button with an X (don't expand) button. The canvas grows on OK or Apply permanently, together with the effects and extended frames as one undo step; cancelling the effects dialog changes nothing.
⑭ Extend framesAdds frames to the effect's range to create animation frames (for example, a 12-frame explosion on a 4-frame drawing). Choose how to fill them: with original frames, same ratio, first frame, last frame or repeat. The added copies are linked cels of their originals, so editing one original changes its copies (unlink a cel to paint it separately).

The same Expand canvas… is also in the timeline effect row's right-click menu (below Apply effect permanently) and in the effect group of Tool options in the right dock (the button next to Open in Effects…). It measures that effect's result (all frames, including the effects before it but not those after), then the same small window as ⑬ shows the growth mode (the value is shared) and the new size; here, clicking Expand grows the canvas right away as one undo step (⑬ inside the effects dialog grows it on OK). If the result already fits, the button is disabled.

⑮ Save Current Effects as Preset at the top of the ⑦ Presets tab saves the whole current effect list (values and clipping included) as an effect preset.

Pressing Enter while typing a setting doesn't close the effects dialog. Changes apply only when you click OK at the bottom, and everything you changed in the dialog is a single undo step. Cancel or closing the window restores the original.

When effects take a while

On large images where effects take a while to compute, cells being redrawn in the ⑫ frame strip get a gray veil, and the "after" side of the ⑪ preview shows a veil and a loading icon until it is ready. Playback waits until the next frame is ready. While you drag a number field (such as the frame count) or the frame strip, the previous image stays under the veil and everything is redrawn at once when you release (light images update while you drag). Clicking ⑬ Expand canvas… before all frames are drawn shows a Measuring effect bounds progress overlay that closes when it finishes.

Deleting frames in the effects dialog

The dashed trash cell after the last cell of the ⑫ frame strip is the ㉑ delete cell. Grab it and drag left: the cells from there to the last cell get a red veil and border, and releasing marks those frames for deletion. The first frame can't be deleted.

  • ㉒ The trash cell shows how many frames you deleted last, in red. Click the trash cell without dragging to bring back only that last deletion (earlier deletions can't be restored).
  • While frames are marked for deletion, the preview and frame strip show the order after deletion, and effects whose range lies only in the deleted frames appear turned off (they are removed when you click OK).
  • If you then drag a range end past the last cell or raise the frame count with ⑭ Extend frames, the deleted frames are discarded (no longer restorable) and new frames are added after the remaining ones.
  • The frames are removed from the project when you click OK or Apply permanently. This is one undo step together with the effect changes, and effect ranges and tags shrink just as when you delete frames in the timeline. Cancel deletes nothing.

Built-in effects

The effects on the Effects tab from the start are the 23 official effects and the Text effect. Official effects are script effects that the app installs by default (the Asset Manager shows their source as Installed with the app); on first run they are installed in the background and appear in the effects dialog shortly after. They update automatically when the app contains a newer version, and deleted official effects come back with Restore default effects in the Asset Manager. Built-in effects in older projects and presets open as the official script effects with the same look and are saved as script effects.

Setting names in the tables are the names shown in the effects dialog, and number ranges are the values you can pick. Every color setting can be a solid color or a gradient (see Effect colors and clipping). A setting named Seed has a number field and a dice button instead of a slider; each click gives another random look, and a look you like stays fixed at that seed value. In the effects dialog, the question mark at the top right of an effect tile also shows that effect's description.

Effects that change the look

These effects look the same on every frame.

EffectWhat it doesMain settings
OutlineDraws a line around the image (outside) or on its edge pixels (inside). Shade from neighbors colors each line pixel like the touching sprite pixel, made darker or lighter by Shade % (dark blue next to blue). Hide original erases the sprite and keeps only the line (inside: only the edge ring), and effects clipped to this outline apply to the line only.Color, Thickness 1–3, 8 directions (incl. corners), Inside (edge pixels), Hide original (outline only), Color mode (Fixed color / Shade from neighbors), Shade % −100 to 100
ShadowCasts a copy of the image's shape behind it, offset by X and Y. With dither the shadow is a dot pattern instead of semi-transparent, for a pixel-art look.Color (semi-transparent black by default), X/Y −16 to 16 (point picker), Dither (None (semi-transparent) / 25% / 50% (checkerboard) / 75%)
Edge light · shadeAdds depth by painting the edge facing the light with the light color and the opposite edge with the shade color.Light direction (8 directions), Range 1–16, Light color, Shade color
Color overlayTints visible pixels with a color or gradient (Amount 100% = silhouette). With the gradient mode Whole sprite, one color (over time), the whole image passes through the stop colors over the effect range.Color, Amount 0–100, Blend (Overlay / Multiply / Screen)
Inner glowMakes a glowing edge by blending a color inward from the edges.Color, Thickness 1–8, Strength 0–100, Weaker toward the inside (stepped), Blend (Overlay / Multiply / Screen; Screen by default)
Palette swapSwaps chosen colors for other colors (palette swap) — for example a version with blue clothes instead of red, without changing the cels.Source → target (color pair list, up to 64 pairs), Tolerance 0–255
Color adjustmentChanges colors with grayscale, sepia, hue shift, brightness, contrast or saturation.Style (Grayscale / Sepia / Hue shift / Brightness/contrast / Saturation), Amount 0–100, Hue −180 to 180, Brightness and Contrast −100 to 100, Saturation −100 to 100
ReflectionFlips the image and reflects it below (floor, water), above (ceiling) or to the left or right (wall), fading gradually.Direction, Gap 0–32, Height (width for sides) % 1–100, Start and End opacity, Fade with dithering (pattern instead of semi-transparency), Tint, Ripple 0–3
TextDraws text over the image in one color (solid or gradient) with the bundled pixel fonts (Galmuri and others) or fonts you added; anything outside the image is cut off. On an empty layer it works like a text layer (drawn even without cels); Merge down or applying it permanently turns it into pixels. The Text tool (T) creates a new layer with this effect. The effect list shows it as "Text · first line · font".Text (multi-line, up to 1000 characters), font, bold/italic, size, color, X/Y position, box width/height (0 = auto; with a width, lines wrap at the box width), horizontal/vertical scale, letter and line spacing, alignment, smooth (vector fonts), Typewriter (one letter at a time) (letters appear from the first one over the effect range — empty on the first frame, complete on the last, following speed and repeat)

Animated effects

These effects change over their effect range. A longer range makes them slower and a shorter one faster, and most of them are tuned with the common Speed (Fast start / Linear / Slow start / Slow start and end) and Repeat (Once / Loop / Ping-pong, 1–8 times) rows (see Effect range and common settings). Blink and Afterimage are timed in frames, so they have no speed or repeat. Shake and Bob report how far they move the image, so the center (pivot) of later effects can follow them.

EffectWhat it doesMain settings
ExplodePixels shatter and fly outward as they disappear.Fly distance X/Y 0–128, Center (Center / Pivot / Bottom center / Custom (X, Y)), Random variation X/Y, Gravity −64 to 64, Fragment size 1–4, Smaller toward the end, Vanish and Vanish start, Flash frames and Flash color, Keep original (a copy flies off), Seed
Light sweepA band of light sweeps across the image from one side to the other (the glint on coins or buttons).Angle 0–359 (dial), Width 1–32, Strength, Color, Softness 0–2
Squash & stretchWobbles horizontally and vertically like jelly, then returns to its original shape.Horizontal and Vertical strength 0–50, Bounce 1–3, Anchor (Bottom center (feet fixed) / Center / Pivot), Upward first (tall → flat)
Hit flashThe image flashes a single color (white, red and so on), as if hit.Color, Strength 0–100, Count 1–8, Shape (Solid (on, then off) / Fade out gradually)
BlinkRepeatedly shows and hides (for example to show invincibility frames).Interval (By frame count / By count in range), Visible and Hidden frames 1–16, Count 1–16, Hide style (Fully transparent / Translucent / Dither 50%), Opacity
ShakeThe whole image jitters by whole pixels.Direction (Horizontal / Vertical / Both), Strength 1–32, Count 1–16, Decay (weaker toward the end), Regular (sine), Seed
BobGently floats up and down (or left and right) — floating items, idle motion. Start and end connect seamlessly.Height 1–32, Count 1–8, Direction (Up/down / Left/right)
Appear / disappearThe image appears or disappears by opacity, dither, wipe or pixel scatter.Disappear (off = appear), Style (Opacity / Dither (4×4 Bayer) / Wipe / Pixel scatter), Wipe from (Top / Bottom / Left / Right), Edge 0–2, Seed
Fade in · outGradually raises or lowers the opacity of the whole image over the effect range.Fade out (off = fade in)
MeltPixels drip down, each column at a different speed.Distance 1–128, Irregularity 0–100, Drip (leaves trails), Vanish, Seed
RotateRotates the image around an anchor. It uses RotSprite, so pixel lines break less and no new colors or translucency appear.Angle −1440 to 1440 (negative = counterclockwise, 360 = full turn; dial), Pivot (Bottom center (feet fixed) / Center / Pivot)
AfterimageLeaves fading copies of previous frames behind for a sense of fast motion. It is drawn after all other effects.Count 1–8, Spacing (frames) 1–8, Opacity (nearest afterimage), Tint, Tint color
Color cycleCycles through the chosen colors in turn (flowing colors like water, fire or neon).Color (list of 2–8 colors), Steps 1–64, Reverse
Shockwave ringA pixel ring spreads out from the center.Center (Center / Pivot / Bottom center / Custom (X, Y)), Max radius 1–128, Thickness 1–8, Color, Fade out (fades as it spreads), Behind image (below)
HologramTints the image like a single-color projection and adds scanlines, a bright band, blinking and glitches (sci-fi hologram).Color and Color strength, Scanline spacing, strength, mode (Darken / Transparent) and Scroll, Bright band with thickness and strength, Opacity, Dither, Blink frequency and strength, Glitch frequency and Max shift, RGB split, Edge glow and strength, Seed

Outline and shadow settings compared

The default outline only looks at the neighbors above, below, left and right, so corners stay open and look stepped. 8 directions (incl. corners) also fills diagonal neighbors for a thicker border. Shade from neighbors paints the line with a darker version of the touching color, for a softer border than black (right — dark orange next to the feet).

Without dither, the background shows through according to the shadow color's transparency (semi-transparent black by default). With dither the shadow becomes a checkerboard-like dot pattern instead of semi-transparency, for a pixel-art shadow (25% and 75% make the dots sparser or denser).

Effect colors and clipping

Effect color settings can be a solid color or a gradient, and with Clip to effect above an effect draws as if only the pixels drawn by the effect before it existed. Together they make combinations such as a rainbow outline or an outline that flashes on its own.

Picking colors

  • Clicking a ③ color swatch opens a popup with the same square, honeycomb, color wheel and slider modes as the main color editor, plus the palette, recent colors and the Pick from image and Pick from screen buttons; the canvas updates as you pick. The two buttons are described under Color pickers in other windows.
  • Each color setting can be a ④ solid color / gradient. Stop editing, the Color space (RGB / HSL / OKLab / OKLCH; new gradients use OKLCH) and the preset menu (the down-arrow button at the end of the bar, or right-click the bar) work as in the gradient tool; picking a preset in an effect also applies its shape, angle, direction, repeat and animation.

Gradient modes

A gradient is laid over the opaque bounds of the image before the effect. Choose the shape and static or animated under Mode, then, depending on the shape, the Direction (forward/backward, inward/outward, clockwise/counterclockwise), the angle and the Repeats/rotations (1–16).

ModeHow it paints
Linear (static) · Linear (animated)Paints straight bands along the angle.
Radial (static) · Radial (animated)Paints rings spreading from the center.
Square (static) · Square (animated)Paints square bands spreading from the center (tilted by the angle).
Angular (static) · Angular (animated)Colors change going around the center (angle = starting point).
Whole sprite, one color (over time)The whole image is one color per frame and passes through the stop colors over the effect range (always animated).

Animated gradients flow or turn over the effect range, as many times as the repeat/turn count. Whole sprite, one color (over time) also has speed and ping-pong settings. Even an effect that doesn't move (such as Outline) changes color over its range with an animated gradient.

Clipping

Turn on ⑤ Apply to → Clip to effect above and the effect draws as if only the unclipped effect before it (the base effect) had been drawn. It receives a layer holding only the pixels the base effect drew, with everything else transparent, applies itself to that layer and puts the result over the picture. In the picture above, the color overlay without clipping covers the sprite and outline as a gradient silhouette (left); with clipping only the outline becomes a gradient (right).

A clipped effect's result can spread beyond the base pixels. In the picture above, the shadow without clipping is the whole image's shadow, cast behind it (left); with clipping the shadow of the outline alone spreads outside the outline and over the picture (right). Clip a fire script effect to a rain effect and the flames rise from the raindrops rather than from the whole image.

  • When several clipped effects follow each other, each one continues from the result of the clipped effect before it. For example, with Outline → Shadow → Color overlay (the last two clipped), the color overlay paints the outline and its shadow together.
  • As far as a clipped effect spreads past the image, it counts toward effect padding and the Expand canvas size. If the base effect is off or the frame is outside its range, the clipped effect isn't shown either. A clipped Reflection mirrors only the base pixels, and a clipped Afterimage leaves only the base effect's pixels from earlier frames as afterimages over the picture. Afterimage can't be a clipping base, and clipping can't be turned on without a base effect before it.
  • Clipped to an outline with Hide original (outline only) on, an effect applies to the line only and the erased sprite doesn't come back. Pixels the clipped effect doesn't touch stay unchanged.

Effect range and common settings

Under each effect's own settings come the same common rows. They decide when the effect applies (effect range), how fast it moves (speed and repeat), what happens after the range ends (Cels after the range), and whether later effects' centers follow it (Later effects). All of them are in the effects dialog's settings; the effect group of the tool options in the right dock shows some of them with the effect's own settings (range and clipping only in the effects dialog).

RowShown forWhat it sets
Apply toAll effectsClip to effect above (see Effect colors and clipping)
Speed · RepeatAnimated effects (except Blink and Afterimage)Easing and repetition of the motion within the effect range
Later effects: Move pivot alongEffects that report how far they move the image (Shake, Bob, scripts using ctx.movePivot)Whether the center (pivot) of the effects after it moves too
Cels after the range: Keep the end lookAnimated effects (when the range isn't All frames)Whether frames after the range keep the effect's final appearance

Effect range

The effect range is the frames an effect applies to: All frames (including frames added later) or a start–end frame. Animated effects start on the first frame of the range and finish on the last, so a longer range makes the same motion slower and a shorter one faster.

WhereHow
Add range in the effects dialogPick the range for newly added effects beforehand. Opening the dialog with Add effect… on selected cels fills in those frames.
Frame strip in the effects dialogDrag over the cells to set the selected effect's range.
Timeline effect boxDrag the ends to resize, drag the body to move, or use the right-click menu for All frames, This frame only and so on (see Effect rows in the timeline).

Effect ranges follow frame numbers just like tags. Inserting frames inside a range extends it, inserting before it pushes it back, and deleting all frames in a range deletes the effect too. Reordering frames leaves the range where it is, so it applies to whichever frames move into it.

Speed and repeat

Speed sets how the motion progresses over the range: Fast start (slowing toward the end) / Linear / Slow start / Slow start and end. As the picture shows, the same effect with Fast start moves a lot in the first frames and almost stops at the end. Effects start with different defaults when added (for example Explode and Shockwave ring use Fast start).

Repeat repeats the motion within the range: choose Once, Loop (start over) or Ping-pong (go and come back) and set the count (1–8).

Later effects, Cels after the range and Expand canvas

For an effect that moves the image, decide ① whether later effects' centers (pivots) move with it; for an animated effect, decide ② whether frames after the range keep its final appearance. Results that go past the canvas are handled with the ③ button in the common settings.

  • ③ OK and Apply permanently don't grow the canvas by themselves; effect results that go past the canvas are cut off (the parts of every effect to be cut are dimmed in the preview).
  • ③ Set the size with Expand canvas… and the canvas grows on OK. Pixels aren't scaled; every layer, part and pivot moves together (see the effects dialog).

Move pivot along

With Rotate after Shake, ⑤ Move pivot along (on by default) makes Rotate's center follow the distance Shake moved the image. Turn it off to move only the image and keep later effects' centers in place. The tooltip explains the same.

  • The row appears only for effects that report movement: the official Shake and Bob (1.0.2 or later) and script effects with ctx.movePivot in the source that are allowed to run.
  • Later effects that use the moved center are pivot-based ones such as Rotate and Squash & stretch. A clipped effect receives the center moved before it but doesn't move it itself, and effects drawn last such as Afterimage use the final moved center.
  • In the part view with effects shown, the part's own and group effects are drawn at the moved position and the pivot marker follows (group effects that depend on other parts may differ from the final composite). The original, editable pivot is marked with a small circle and a dotted line for the Pivot tool.

Keep the end look

Turn on Cels after the range: Keep the end look for an animated effect and frames after the range keep its final appearance. For example, if a throw effect covers frames 1–18, frame 19 stays where the object landed. The timeline effect row shows ⑥ a faint tail after the range.

  • Cels after the range that are linked to the range's last cel look identical to it, pixel for pixel.
  • Unlinked cels (different drawings) keep only the final position from effects that report movement (Shake, Bob, scripts using ctx.movePivot). Rotation, color and other end-state details apply only to linked cels, and the tail is even fainter over the unlinked ones.
  • With Move pivot along on, later effects' centers and the pivot marker follow the final position too. Turning the effect off restores the original drawing and position.
  • With the range set to All frames there are no frames after it, so the option is unavailable. Unlinking such a cel bakes the held end appearance into it as shown.

Effect padding

Effects that draw outside the image, such as Outline, Shadow and Explode, reserve padding around it (an outline by its thickness, a shadow by its offset). This padding decides the sprite size in part atlases (Expand canvas measures the actually drawn result, not the padding), where the pivot is shifted to keep pointing at the same spot. Effects that report movement get their padding widened automatically on that frame, so art moved beyond the configured padding isn't cut off (Apply permanently bakes the same size). Effects that don't report movement draw only up to their configured padding, and package previews have a fixed size, so they show only the padding.

Effect rows in the timeline

Like a video editor, each effect gets a thin effect row under its layer. Without opening the effects dialog you can add effects, drag their ranges, move them to other layers and copy them.

⑯ Each effect row has the on/off checkbox and effect name on the left and a ⑰ rounded effect box spanning the frames it applies to. Click fx in the layer name cell to collapse or expand that layer's effect rows.

Adding an effect to selected cels

Select cels and choose ⑱ Add effect… from the right-click menu to open the effects dialog with Add range set to those frames.

Changing by dragging

  • Drag either end of a box to change its range, or drag the body to move the whole range. Double-click a box to open the effects dialog.
  • Drag an effect name up or down to reorder it, or drop it on another layer's row to move it to that layer. Select and press Delete to delete, Ctrl+C/V to copy.
  • Effect ranges follow frame insertions and deletions like tags (see Effect range and common settings).

Right-click menus

Right-click ⑲ an effect box for the menu below. Right-clicking an empty cell outside the box adds ⑳ the extend and add-copies items.

Menu itemWhat it does
Effect settings…Opens the effects dialog with that effect selected (same as double-clicking the box).
To all frames · This frame only · From this frame to end · From start to this frameChanges the effect range relative to the right-clicked frame.
On / OffTurns the effect on or off without deleting it.
DeleteDeletes the effect (Delete).
Apply effect permanentlyBakes the effect into the cel pixels; enabled effects above it are baked too, shown as "including N effects above" (see Layer effects).
Expand canvas…Opens a small window that grows the canvas to fit that effect's result (described under the Expand canvas… button in the effects dialog).
Copy effect · Paste effectCopies the effect and pastes it onto the current layer (Ctrl+C/V).
⑳ Extend to hereEmpty cells outside the box only: extends the effect range to the right-clicked frame.
⑳ Add copies from hereEmpty cells outside the box only: adds a copy of the effect from the right-clicked frame to the end.

Effect presets

An effect preset is a named bundle of several effects with their values and clipping relationships (formerly called custom effects or effect recipes). Insert it into other layers or projects in one go from the Presets tab of the effects dialog; with parameters, a few values adjust the whole bundle. Presets are used on this computer and shared as asset files, but not published online — to publish an effect, make it a script effect in the Effect Maker.

Use them to give every character the same outline + shadow combination, to insert a frequently used combo such as hit flash + shake in one go, or to change the color of several effects with a single value (parameters).

Basic workflow

  1. Build the effect list you want in the effects dialog.
  2. Click Save Current Effects as Preset at the top of the Presets tab, choose a name and preview chick, and save.
  3. In the effects dialog of another layer or project, double-click the preset on the Presets tab or drag it into the effect list.
  4. To adjust the whole bundle with a few values, define parameters (see Setting parameters below).

Presets tab

  • Build an effect list in the effects dialog and click ① Save Current Effects as Preset to open the save window; saving makes it ② one of your presets. ③ Received presets (downloaded with the Asset Manager or imported from asset files) show on the same tab (the tooltip names the Store entry, or the author). Double-click a tile or drag it into the effect list. Inserted presets are copied by value, so they stay in the project and remain editable even if you change or delete the original preset.
  • Tile right-click menu: your presets have Rename, Edit parameters, Delete and Show in Asset Manager; received presets only have Show in Asset Manager. To change a received preset, make your own copy with Copy to My Assets in the Asset Manager.

Saving a preset

Enter ④ a name (it starts as the first effect's name). If one of your presets already has that name, you are asked whether to overwrite it. Under ⑤ Preview chick, pick None in the first slot or one of 20 numbered chicks (chick 1 by default; you can pick again when overwriting). When you export the preset as an asset file, a preview of the effects on this chick (animated for moving effects) is made automatically. With None, the effects are drawn on an empty transparent canvas of the default chick size without a chick (for effects that draw on their own, such as rain or snow).

Setting parameters

A parameter ties one setting from each of several effects in the preset to a single value. For example, link the outline color and the shadow color to a "Color" parameter, and after inserting you change both with one value.

  1. Right-click one of your presets on the Presets tab and choose Edit parameters (your preset's details in the Asset Manager have the same button).
  2. Click Add to create a parameter in the ⑥ list and give it a ⑦ Name.
  3. Under ⑧ Linked settings, check the settings this value changes together. Each preset effect lists the settings it can expose as "number. effect name · setting key (kind)". The first link sets the parameter's kind and default (that setting's current value); settings of another kind and settings already linked to another parameter are disabled.
  4. Depending on the kind, set ⑨ the Default, the Range for integers or the Choices for choices, then click OK.

Invalid links or ranges are listed as problems at the bottom of the window, and OK stays off until they are fixed. The preset details in the Asset Manager list the number of parameters with their names and kinds.

Using a parameter preset

  • ⑩ A preset with parameters appears in the effect list as one row, "preset name · preset, N effects" (with an expand arrow and a group icon).
  • ⑪ Selecting that row shows only the preset name, source and parameters in the settings; changing a value updates every linked effect setting together.
  • ⑫ Show effects lists the effects inside under the group row so you can edit them one by one, and Hide effects folds them again. Ungroup turns them into ordinary effects with their current values (the parameters go away).
  • A collapsed group moves, drags, deletes and applies permanently as one unit.

Script effects

A script effect is a layer effect written in JavaScript. The official effects such as Outline and Shadow, effects from the Store and effects you make yourself are all script effects, and you add and edit them like any other layer effect. The source is saved inside the project file, so it draws the same on another computer, and it runs in an isolated engine with no file or network access, within time, statement and memory limits per frame.

This page covers using script effects (settings, editing the source, allowing them to run, errors). New effects are made and published in the Effect Maker, and the names and rules you can use in a source are in Script API.

In the effects dialog

  • ① After the official effects and Text, the Effects tab has tiles for received script effects and the script effects you made. Double-click a tile or drag it into the effect list. New scripts are made and saved in Tools > Effect Maker….
  • With a script effect selected, the top of the settings shows ② the declared name, the author, the first 12 characters of the source hash and a status line (time per frame in the current preview, or an error). ③ Edit script… opens the editor, and Open JS file… replaces this effect's source with a file (settings with the same key keep their values).
  • ④ Save as asset: saves this source as one of your script effects (an asset) to reuse from the Effects tab and the Asset Manager and to edit in the Effect Maker. Only scripts allowed to run can be saved, and saving the same source twice keeps one copy.
  • ⑤ The settings come from the declared settings (see Settings below). A script declared as animated also gets Speed and Repeat, like the official animated effects.

Settings

  • ⑥ A color setting (color) can be a solid color or a gradient, like the color fields of the official effects. When the script reads it with ctx.color, each pixel gets its gradient color. With alpha: false only opaque colors can be picked.
  • ⑦ Color lists (colorList) and color pair lists (colorPairs) number each item and have move up/down and remove buttons (remove is off at the minimum count); add items with Add color or Add pair (off at the maximum). In a pair, the top row is the color to replace and the bottom row the replacement; every item can be a gradient.
  • ⑧ An integer with widget: "angle" is set by clicking or dragging an angle dial (0° = right, 90° = up; hold Shift to snap to 15°). ⑨ An integer with widget: "point" and its Y setting named by pair are set together by dragging on a point picker (top left is the smallest value; the number fields work too).
  • ⑩ A layer picker (layer) lists (none) and then the current document's layer names top to bottom like the timeline (groups and parts included, each name once, without the layer the effect is on and the group containing it). A saved name that isn't in the document stays as a "name (missing)" item. Changes apply right away like other settings.
  • ⑪ An integer setting with the key seed shows a number field and a dice button instead of a slider. Each click picks another seed in its range for a different random look; a look you like stays fixed at that seed value.

Integers and numbers get a slider and a number field (just the field without a range), on/off settings (bool) a checkbox labeled with the setting name, and choices (choice) a list. Long setting names wrap so the fields aren't cut off.

Script editor and code editor

  • ③ Edit script… opens an editor for the source of this effect in the layer (assets are made and published in the Effect Maker). Edit in the ⑬ code editor following ⑫ the hint at the top; ⑮ Apply (Ctrl+Enter) shows the result in the effects dialog and on the canvas right away.
  • ⑭ Syntax and declaration errors are listed as "Line n, column m: …"; click one to move the cursor there. The error spot in the source gets a red wavy underline that shows the message when you hover it. The line below the list shows the run status in the current preview (time per frame, or a run error).
  • The code editor is the same as in the Effect Maker: JavaScript syntax colors, line numbers, and hovering a name shows its shape and a one-line description.
  • Applying an edited source switches the effect to the new source; old sources no longer used are dropped from the project when you save.

⑯ Autocompletion: suggestions appear after img., ctx., ctx.source., ctx.pivot. and Math., after s. (the keys in the source's settings; r, g, b, a after s.key. for colors), after variables holding a result such as const c = ctx.color("key"), at key positions of the declaration and setting items, and inside type: ", area: ", previewChick: " (with chick numbers) and ctx.color(" (color setting keys); elsewhere they are globals, names in this source and keywords. The selected suggestion shows its shape and a one-line description beside the list. The list opens as you type a dot, letters or a quote, or with Ctrl+Space. Enter or Tab inserts; typing a dot or bracket inserts the selected suggestion first. Inside a function call a parameter hint shows (current argument in bold).

Allowing scripts to run

Opening a project with scripts you haven't seen before (with Open, drag and drop and so on) shows the Run script effects? window. ⑰ Each script has a checkbox (on at first), its name, author and full hash; ⑱ Run runs only the checked ones and Don't run runs none.

  • Your choice is remembered on this computer per source hash. Effects that don't run aren't drawn and stay in the project as they are; you can allow them later with Allow to run in the effects dialog settings (they draw right away).
  • These run right away without asking: sources written or imported on this computer, your own script effects, script effects installed with the Asset Manager (from the Store or an asset file — installing counts as allowing), sources written by the AI and the official effect sources (including versions exported by older apps, so opening an older project after an official effect changed doesn't ask). Sources you set to Don't run when opening a project stay blocked until you allow them or install them again.

When a script can't draw

When a script effect can't draw, a warning icon appears at the end of its effect list row and on its timeline effect row (at the right end of the name cell and of the effect box); hover the icon in the effect list for the reason.

StatusCause and fix
Missing sourceThe project doesn't contain the source. Open the same JS file (Open JS file… in the settings) to draw it again.
Not allowedThe script isn't allowed to run. Click Allow to run in the settings.
Source errorThe source has syntax or declaration errors. Fix them in the editor.
FailedA frame went over the limits (2 seconds, 50 million statements and 256 MB of allocations, plus 100 µs, 500 statements and 6 KB per pixel of the image) or the script threw an error. Only this effect is skipped on that frame.
StoppedIt failed 3 times in a row, so computing stopped. Fix it, then click Run again in the settings or turn the effect off and on.

Script effect assets

  • Tiles of received and own script effects on the Effects tab have a code icon (two angle brackets) at the top right (official effects don't). The tooltip shows the source's description and, for received effects, the Store name (or the author), and says so when the script isn't allowed to run. Right-click one of your script tiles for Edit in Effect Maker, Delete and Show in Asset Manager; received effects only have Show in Asset Manager (the name comes from the declaration in the source).
  • When you save a preset containing script effects or export it as an asset file, the source goes with it, so it draws the same on another computer.
  • In the Asset Manager, script effects have the kind Script effect and show under the Effects filter; their details list the author, source hash, Script run (allowed / not allowed) and the number and names of settings. Scripts not yet allowed get an orange note. Edit in Effect Maker in the details of your own script effect closes the Asset Manager and opens it in the Effect Maker. For a received script effect, Copy to My Assets makes your own editable copy.

Script API

This page is for writing or editing a script effect's source yourself. It covers the shape of a source (declaration), setting types, the img and ctx used in render and the global helpers, the fast path and limits, reading other layers and frames, and randomness and gradient colors. The same rules and full list are also in the Effect Maker's Rules window and the code editor's autocompletion.

Script API: declaration

One source is one effect. Call effect({ … }) exactly once at the top and keep only const values and function declarations besides it (no import or export). render(img, ctx, s) is called for every frame, changes img.data in place and returns nothing. The same image, frame and settings must always give the same result, so Date, Math.random, eval/Function, files, network, timers and console aren't available (use ctx.hash and ctx.random for randomness). The same rules and the full list are in the Effect Maker's Rules window.

Below is the skeleton that New script in the Effect Maker starts from (comments removed). It declares one setting (Strength), and render moves the red of every non-transparent pixel toward 255 by that strength.

effect({
  name: "New effect",
  language: "en",
  settings: [
    { key: "strength", label: "Strength", type: "int", min: 0, max: 100, default: 50 },
  ],
  render(img, ctx, s) {
    const d = img.data, k = s.strength / 100;
    for (let i = 0; i < d.length; i += 4) {
      if (d[i + 3] === 0) continue;
      d[i] = lerp(d[i], 255, k);
    }
  },
});
NameMeaning
nameEffect name (1–100 characters, required), shown in the effect list and assets
descriptionShort description (up to 200 characters). The Effect Maker's description field and the initial short description when you publish
authorAuthor name (shown in the run confirmation window)
languageLanguage code of the declared name, description and setting labels ("ko", "en", "zh-Hans" …). In that UI language the declared values are shown
translationsName, description and labels in other languages, { name, description, labels } (labels: setting key → label, "key.choiceValue" → choice label). The one for the UI language is shown in the effects dialog, the layer effect list and the Asset Manager
tagsUp to 8 lowercase English tags (letters, digits, hyphens). Used as the tags when you publish
settingsList of settings { key, label, type, min, max, step, default, choices, alpha, widget, pair, when } (table below). when shows a row only when an earlier choice/bool matches; even hidden values are read as s.key in render
render(img, ctx, s)Called for every frame; changes img.data in place. The same input must always give the same result
animated · motion · easinganimated: true for effects that change with progress (adds speed and repeat settings). motion: false hides speed and repeat (for example blinking timed in frames). easing is the initial easing of a newly added effect ("Linear", "EaseIn", "EaseOut", "EaseInOut")
paddingMargin the effect adds outside the image: [left, top, right, bottom] or padding(s, width, height) { … }
area · radius"global" (default, once per frame with the whole image) or "local" (per tile, with radius px around it)
repeat · repeatCount · movePivotAnimated effect defaults: Once/Loop/PingPong (default Once) · repeat count 1–8 (default 1) · whether later effects' centers move with the image (default true, false turns it off)
deferredWhen true, draws after all other effects regardless of its place in the list (like Afterimage). Only such effects get ctx.renderedFrameSource
previewChick · previewEndChickPreview chick ID (default "original") and end chick ID. In number order 1–20: original, painter, pencil, palette, easel, paint-bucket, designer, heart-canvas, cursor, hatch, knight, wizard, archer, ninja, pirate, astronaut, viking, king, gamer, healer (anything but lowercase letters, digits and hyphens is a declaration error). previewChick: "none" draws the preview on an empty transparent canvas of the default chick size without a chick (for effects that draw on their own, such as rain or snow; the check always uses the 20 sample chicks)
previewFramesNumber of frames in the preview, the check and the published preview (2–20, default 10)
previewMotionPreview chick movement: "none" (default), "right", "left", "up", "down" (1 px per frame) or one [x, y] per frame (−32 to 32; later frames keep the last one). Only previews move
previewPivot · previewEndOffset · previewEndPivotPreview position and pivot (each [x, y] in px, right and down are +, −64 to 64, 0 when omitted): offset of each frame's pivot (ctx.pivot) from its default place, the chick's center (every frame but the last when there is an end chick) · offset of the end chick from its place · offset of the last frame's (the end chick's) pivot. The two end values are used only with previewEndChick. Only previews change

Script API: setting types

typeField in the effects dialogs.key value
int · numberSlider and number field (min, max, step; number shows the digits of step). widget: "angle" = angle dial, widget: "point" + pair = X/Y point picker. Key seed adds a dice buttonNumber
boolCheckbox labeled with the setting nametrue / false
colorColor swatch with solid/gradient (opaque only with alpha: false). default like "#ff8800"{ r, g, b, a } — gradients via ctx.color
choiceDrop-down list (choices: [{ value, label }])The chosen value
colorListColor list (min, max = item count; default 0–16, up to 64)[{ r, g, b, a }, …] — item colors via ctx.colorAt
colorPairsFrom → to color pair list (default [{ from, to }, …])[{ from, to }, …] — ctx.pairColor
layerLayer picker. default is a layer name like "ground" (up to 64 characters; none chosen when omitted). min, max, step, choices, alpha and widget aren't allowedThe chosen layer name ("" when none) — read the layer with ctx.layer(key)
whenShow the setting only when an earlier choice/bool setting matches. Hidden values remain stored and accessible to renderwhen: { facing: "rotate" } · when: { facing: ["rotate", "flip"] } · when: { closePath: true }

A script setting with when: { facing: "rotate" } or when: { facing: ["rotate", "flip"] } appears only when an earlier choice setting has that value. when: { closePath: true } tests an earlier bool. All conditions must match; if a dependency is hidden, its dependents are hidden too. Changes show or hide rows immediately in the effects dialog, tool dock and Effect Maker preview. Hidden values remain saved and accessible as s.key in render(img, ctx, s). Referring to a missing/later setting, a non-choice/non-bool setting or a nonexistent choice value is a declaration error. Autocompletion suggests when as well.

Script API: img, ctx and globals

NameMeaning
img.width · img.height · img.dataSize of the padded image and its RGBA numbers (length = width × height × 4; the red of pixel (x, y) is at (y * img.width + x) * 4; written values are rounded to 0–255)
img.blank(w, h) · img.blendOver(src, x, y)Makes an empty (transparent) image · alpha-blends src at (x, y)
ctx.progress · ctx.phase · ctx.tProgress 0–1 within the effect range: progress includes loop and easing (animated effects usually use it), phase only the loop, t neither
ctx.frame · ctx.step · ctx.spanFrame being drawn (from 0) · frames since the first frame of the range · range length − 1
ctx.rangeStart · ctx.rangeEnd · ctx.frameTagFirst and last frame of the effect range (0 and the last frame in the Effect Maker preview) · name of the tag containing this frame ("" when none or in previews)
ctx.source · ctx.pivot · ctx.centerWhere the original sits in the padded image { x, y, w, h } · pivot { x, y } · center { x, y } of the source (canvas, or the part rectangle) — always the center, unlike the pivot
ctx.movePivot(dx, dy)Report image displacement in pixels to this frame's pivot, rendering bounds and later effects. Fractions allowed; calls accumulate; no return value; works on the fast path
ctx.slice9-slice border { left, top, right, bottom } (project border for the canvas, part border for parts). null when there is none, it doesn't fit, or outside a project
ctx.color(키, x, y, 범위?) · ctx.colorAt(키, i, x, y, 범위?) · ctx.pairColor(키, i, "from"|"to", x, y, 범위?)Color { r, g, b, a } at (x, y) of a color setting, color list item or pair side. The solid color, or for gradients sampled like the official effects over the opaque bounds of the image before the effect (or the given bounds { x, y, w, h })
ctx.hasGradient(…)Whether a color slot has a gradient: "key", "key[0]", "key[0].from", or (listKey, index, "from"|"to")
ctx.hash(x, y, seed, salt?) · ctx.random(seed)A number from 0 up to 1 fixed by position, seed and purpose (salt), or by frame and seed (always the same)
ctx.bayer2(x, y) · ctx.bayer4(x, y)2×2 (0–3) and 4×4 (0–15) ordered-dither thresholds
ctx.frameSource(i)The source of frame i before effects (same size). null when missing or a different size
ctx.renderedFrameSource(i)Deferred effects only: frame i with this layer's other effects applied, { image, offsetX, offsetY }
ctx.matchPixels(a, b, mode?)Pairs the opaque pixels of two images (sizes may differ) as { count, data } ([ax, ay, bx, by] per pair; pixels of the smaller side are reused). Mode "color" orders by brightness first (default: by position). null when a or b is null
ctx.layer(키) · ctx.layerWithEffects(키) · ctx.layerEffects(키)The layer chosen in a layer setting on this frame (without / with its effects, aligned to img) and the names of its enabled effects. null or an empty array when there is none
lerp · hsvToRgb · rgbToHsv · rgbToOklab · oklabToRgb · MathGlobal helpers: lerp(a, b, t), HSV and OKLab conversion (the app's own OKLab math), Math (no Math.random). Typed arrays: Float64Array, Int32Array, Uint8Array

Scripts that move their image must report per-frame pixel displacement with ctx.movePivot(dx, dy). Arguments are pixels in image coordinate directions (fractions allowed), repeated calls add up, and nothing is returned; it runs on the fast execution path too. Later effects' pivots, part pivot markers, automatic render-padding growth and post-range held positions use the report. Existing scripts such as Follow path or Gravity throw that do not report motion need a call at the actual movement calculation in Edit script. Declaration keys: repeat is Once/Loop/PingPong (default Once), repeatCount is 1–8 (default 1), and movePivot defaults to true; false disables following-center motion on newly added effects. AI inserts also use these defaults.

Fast path and limits

  • A source runs on the fast path or on Jint. When render uses only numbers, booleans, loops, if, Math, lerp, helper functions taking numbers and img.data, the app converts and compiles it (fast path); strings, objects, arrays, closures, recursion and the like run on Jint with the same result, only slower. The Effect Maker status shows which path is used and why (what on which line).
  • These run on the fast path too: ctx.center, ctx.frameSource, ctx.matchPixels, ctx.color/ctx.colorAt/ctx.pairColor with the key written literally, and ctx.layer/ctx.layerWithEffects (including the usual null checks if (!g), g ? … : …, g === null). ctx.slice, ctx.layerEffects and reading a layer setting value s.key run on Jint.
  • Limits per frame: 2 seconds, 50 million statements, 256 MB of allocations and call depth 256, plus 100 µs, 500 statements and 6 KB per pixel of the image (bigger images get more; the fast path has no image size cap). Over a limit the effect is skipped on that frame, and after 3 failures in a row it stops. Keep the work proportional to the pixel count (no big loop per pixel).

Reading other layers

  • To read another layer or draw in relation to it, add a layer setting (for example { key: "ground", label: "Ground", type: "layer", default: "ground" }) so the user picks the layer, then read it in render with ctx.layer("ground") (without effects), ctx.layerWithEffects("ground") (with this frame's effects) or ctx.layerEffects("ground") (names of enabled effects). There is no way to read a layer by position or by a name written in the code.
  • It returns the nearest layer with the chosen name (excluding this layer and the group containing it; hidden layers count), aligned to img (same size and position, transparent outside, without layer opacity). When nothing is chosen, the layer or cel doesn't exist, the key isn't a layer setting or there is no project, it returns null (ctx.layerEffects: an empty array), so always check for null. ctx.layerWithEffects skips that layer's effects that read other layers with effects, so layers reading each other never loop.
  • Changing the pixels, effects, name or order of the layer being read, or the 9-slice border, redraws the effect right away on the canvas, the timeline, the effects dialog preview and exports. In the Effect Maker the layer is picked from the preview scene, and publishing always adds the layer-reference tag.

Effects that use several frames (transform)

  • ctx.frameSource(i) reads another frame before effects. Effects using it also draw on frames without a cel, so put pictures only on the first and last frames and apply a transform effect over that range to fill the frames in between.
  • Transform example: get a = ctx.frameSource(ctx.rangeStart), b = ctx.frameSource(ctx.rangeEnd) and m = ctx.matchPixels(a, b), clear img.data, then draw each pair moving from (ax, ay) to (bx, by) and blending its color with ctx.progress.
