Preview and check it before saving with the Effect Maker's End chick; publishing always adds the frame-reference tag. ctx.frameTag lets an effect draw differently per frame tag.

Randomness and gradient colors

  • Randomness comes from a seed: add { key: "seed", label: "Seed", type: "int", min: 0, max: 9999, default: 1 } to get the dice button in the effects dialog, and use ctx.hash(x, y, s.seed, salt) and ctx.random(s.seed) so each seed gives a different look and the same seed always the same one. Give each random value its own salt (e.g. 0 = direction, 1 = speed) and feed values that don't change per frame, such as a piece number, so pieces follow the same path in every frame.
  • Users can switch color settings to gradients. Instead of reading s.key once, pick the color for each pixel you write with ctx.color("key", x, y) (lists: ctx.colorAt, pairs: ctx.pairColor) so gradients are followed. Pass bounds { x, y, w, h } as the last argument when the default area doesn't fit, and use s.key only when the effect really needs a single color. The Effect Maker's check points out color settings that draw in a single color only.

Effect Maker

The Effect Maker is the window for creating and editing script effects, previewing them on sample chicks, checking them and publishing them to the Store. One source is one effect, and its title, description, tags and preview chick live in the source's declaration too, so one source file is the same effect on any computer. Effect presets (bundles of several effects) aren't made here.

Open it with AI > Effect Maker… (sparkles icon, at the bottom of the AI menu). The AI panel on the right is open from the start (close and reopen it with the AI button). Edit in Effect Maker on your script tile in the effects dialog (right-click) or in your script effect's details in the Asset Manager opens that effect.

Basic workflow

  1. Start from the skeleton with New script, or pick one of your effects in the list.
  2. Edit the source yourself, describe the effect in the AI panel, or copy the rules window to another AI and paste its answer.
  3. Check the look with the preview and the preview setting values, then Save (Ctrl+S). It is now available on the Effects tab of the effects dialog.
  4. To share it, run Check on the 20 sample chicks and click Upload….

Window layout

  • ① My effects: script effects made on this computer or published with your account (unchanged copies of received effects are left out). Turn on Show all at the top right to also see those copies and received script effects (package icon). Effects linked to the Store show the author's profile picture (a person icon when there is none) and name next to their name; effects made only on this computer show just a code icon and the name. Right-click: Delete (your own only) and Show in Asset Manager.
  • ② New script starts from a short skeleton (a name in the UI language, one "Strength" setting and a loop over the pixels); Open JS file… opens a .js file's source as a new, unsaved effect.
  • ③ Info: the header shows the developer of the open effect on the right (the author's profile picture and name for effects published to the Store, a person icon and 'Made on this computer' for effects made on this computer). Below it are Title (1–100 characters), Description (up to 200) and Tags. Editing them changes name, description and tags in the declaration, and editing the source updates the fields. The language being edited always shows next to the field names: when the UI language differs from the declared language, the translation for the UI language is shown and edited (empty if there is none; the dim text is the base value). Older scripts without language show the UI-language translation if there is one, else the declared values (Base (language not set)). Type tags like #neon and press Space, Enter or a comma to add a chip (X removes it, Backspace in the empty field removes the last one; up to 8, lowercase English letters, digits and hyphens).
  • ③ Languages below opens a window for the title, description and setting labels in each language (add or remove languages, type another language code, empty fields show the source values, languages with text are green with a check mark — translations in the declaration). Base language at the top is the language of the declared title and description; without language it is guessed from the letters (Hangul → Korean, kana → Japanese, Han → Simplified Chinese, Cyrillic → Russian, Thai → Thai, Latin → English). OK writes it into the source as language. The name matching the app's UI language is shown in the effects dialog, the layer effect list and the Asset Manager.
  • ④ Rules (right of the Info header, book icon) shows the rules for writing an effect and the API (see Rules window below).
  • ⑤ The code editor is the same as the script editor of the effects dialog (syntax colors, autocompletion, error underlines — see Script effects). Undo (Ctrl+Z) works in the editor.
  • ⑥ Preview setting values: pick effect-specific settings, animated easing/repeat/repeat count and motion-reporting pivot movement in the same order as the Effects dialog and right dock. Values affect the preview and Insert into layer; Reset restores source defaults, and Set as default writes the currently chosen values as defaults.
  • ⑦ AI (right of the Preview header, sparkles icon) opens and closes the AI panel on the right of the window (see AI panel below).
  • ⑧ The preview is the same image that is made when you publish: the effect on the chosen chick over as many frames as the frame count (0.1 s each). It redraws shortly after you edit the source or settings. The frame strip below shows each frame with its number; click one to pause on it (while playing, the highlight follows).
  • ⑨ Preview chick: pick None in the first slot or one of 20 chicks by picture and number (changes previewChick in the source; with errors in the source you are asked to fix them first). With None ("none"), the preview and the published preview draw the effect on an empty transparent canvas of the default chick size (at the same place in the preview scene, if there is one) without a chick. Use it for effects that draw on their own, such as rain or snow. End chick is for transform effects (below).
  • ⑩ Play/pause icon button, Show without effect (eye-off toggle), Frames (2–20, previewFrames in the source — used by the preview, the check and the published preview; a published preview over the size limit merges pairs of frames), the gear = Preview motion and the crosshair = Preview position and pivot (both below).
  • ⑪ Save (Ctrl+S) saves it as your script effect (with its preview scene; it shows in the Effects tab of the effects dialog and in the Asset Manager). The Save button is enabled only when there are unsaved changes, and then turns red to stand out; after saving, or with nothing changed, it is dimmed gray. Insert into layer adds it with the preview setting values to the end of the current layer's effects in the main window (all frames, one undo step). If the effects dialog is open or the layer is locked, you only get a message.
  • ⑫ The status button shows the current state by color; click it for details (see Status and check below).
  • ⑬ Check, Upload… and Close. Picking another effect, starting a new script or closing with unsaved edits asks Save, Discard or Cancel (* after the effect name in the window title = edited).

Preview setting values mirror the tool dock's rows and order when inserted: effect-specific settings → easing, repeat and repeat count for animated effects → Later effects: Move pivot along for motion-reporting effects → an uneditable, off Cels after the range: Keep the end look. Labels use the same column layout as the effects dialog. Change easing, repeat, repeat count or pivot motion in preview and choose Set as defaults to write easing, repeat, repeatCount and movePivot to the declaration; restoring app defaults Linear, Once, 1 and true removes those keys. The area outside the chick's canvas (what would be cut off once added to a layer) is always dimmed. Reset restores source values. Insert on current layer uses the selected preview values.

My effects and published effects

  • ⑭ Effects published with your account (author ID = your handle; when signed out, the last handle you signed in with) show your profile picture (or a person icon) and name together next to the name. Without author details, Published appears there instead, or Published · edited after you changed them. Saving and publishing again makes a new version of the same package.
  • ⑮ Effects published with the current account that aren't on this computer show as grey rows at the bottom of the list. The download button on the right fetches it from the online catalog (checking size and SHA-256), installs and opens it. Clicking the grey row itself doesn't open anything. While signed in, the online catalog is checked when the window opens and when the account changes.

In My effects, an effect whose copy on this computer differs from the one in the online catalog (Store) has its name (and icon) in orange. Hover it to see why: a newer version is online (the online and local versions; you can update from the Store in the Asset Manager) / this computer's version isn't in the online catalog yet (a newly published version may be under review) / edited after downloading or publishing. Effects not in the online catalog (never published) aren't marked, nor is anything when the catalog couldn't be fetched. The online catalog is fetched again when the window opens (even when signed out).

Received effects are read-only

Effects made by others (received script effects) open read-only in the Effect Maker. The source, info, preview chick, frame count, motion, position and pivot, and scene can't be changed, and Save, Upload… and the AI panel are off. You can still try preview setting values, Check and Insert into layer.

  1. Open the effect (turn on Show all if it isn't listed).
  2. Click Copy to My Assets in the bottom row. The received original stays as it is, and your own copy with the same source is created and opened (if you already have your own effect with that source, it opens instead).
  3. Edit and save the copy. The copy is your own asset, so you can publish it too.

The received original keeps getting Store updates. Downloading a higher version from the Store replaces the same effect on this computer without asking, so always make your own copy of an effect you want to change. Effects already placed on project layers keep the source they were inserted with in the project, so updates don't change them (see Asset Manager).

Preview motion

The gear button in ⑩ opens the Preview motion window (its tooltip shows the current motion). Choosing ⑯ None, Right, Left, Up, Down (1 px per frame) or Custom fills ⑰ the table (dimmed); editing the table switches to Custom (−32 to 32 px per frame, right and down are +). OK writes previewMotion into the declaration. The chick is moved only in the preview, the check and the published preview (documents are unaffected), so effects that react to movement, such as afterimages and trails, become visible; ctx.frameSource returns the moved frames too. Without it the chick doesn't move.

End chick (transform effects)

Choosing an ⑱ End chick (info box; first item None) changes previewEndChick in the declaration, and the last frame of the preview, the check and the published preview becomes that chick (both chicks stand feet-aligned on a canvas of the same size). Effects that turn one picture into another over several frames read the end picture with ctx.frameSource(ctx.rangeEnd), so the ⑲ frame strip shows the transformation and Check tests it before you save (see "Effects that use several frames" in Script effects).

Preview position and pivot

The crosshair button in ⑩ opens the Preview position and pivot window (its tooltip shows the current non-zero values, for example "Preview position and pivot: End chick position (16, -6)", or "None" when all are zero). Each of the three rows takes X and Y in px (right and down are +, −64 to 64). Without an end chick the two end rows are dimmed and off (their values are kept but not used). Pivot values are offsets from the default pivot (the chick's center), so moving only the end chick leaves the pivot where it is. OK writes only the changed values into the declaration as previewPivot, previewEndOffset and previewEndPivot ([x, y]; zero removes the key), as one undo step in the editor.

RowWhat it does
Chick pivotMoves each frame's pivot (ctx.pivot). With an end chick it applies to every frame except the last (the end chick's frame); without one, to all frames.
End chick positionMoves the end chick from its place (feet aligned with the first chick).
End chick pivotMoves the pivot of the last frame (the end chick's frame).

These values are used only for previews: the Effect Maker preview, Check, AI previews, the published preview and preview scenes (documents are unaffected). Use them to see and check, before saving, morph and rotation effects that only matter when the two images sit in different places or have different centers. In a preview scene the end chick moves from the scene's chick spot and each frame's pivot is kept inside the scene canvas; without a scene the preview canvas widens to fit the moved chick and pivot.

The picture draws the morph example from the ⑲ frame strip (it reads the end picture with ctx.frameSource(ctx.rangeEnd) and moves each pixel). With End chick position 0 it changes in place; moved 16 px to the right, the end picture is read at the moved spot, so the image travels there as it changes.

Preview scene

  • Effects that read other layers (a layer setting read with ctx.layer and so on — landing on a ground, following a path) are previewed on a preview scene. When the source uses the other-layer functions or a scene exists, the ㉑ Preview scene row appears (info box, Edit in main editor, Remove scene, layer names bottom to top with the [Chick] position). Without a scene only the chick is drawn, so the other-layer functions return null.
  • ⑳ The layer picker in Preview setting values lists the scene's layer names top to bottom (without the chick layer). Without a scene it only has (none), with "Add a preview scene to pick one of its layers." dimmed below. Renaming scene layers updates the list.
  • Edit in main editor: without a scene it creates the default one (canvas of chick width × 3 and height × 2 — 48–256 px, an empty ground layer, 1 frame) and opens it in the main editor as a "Preview scene - effect name" tab. Draw with any tool, add layers and name them. Moving the chick layer changes where the chick stands (by its feet), and another chick is placed with its feet on the same spot. A scene with fewer frames than the preview repeats.
  • Limits: canvas 256×256, 8 layers, 20 frames (layers × frames must fit a 512×512 grid). Effects, groups and parts in scene layers aren't used. The scene tab doesn't ask to save when closed; what you draw reaches the preview shortly after (the Effect Maker counts it as an edit — save the effect to keep the scene). The scene is saved with the effect, goes into asset files as previews/name.scene.png, and the check, the AI preview and the published preview are drawn on it too. Remove scene asks and removes the scene (closing its tab).

After Edit in main editor, the Effect Maker becomes a ㉒ small preview window in the top-right corner of the main editor so it doesn't cover the canvas, and the focus moves to the main editor. Editing the scene updates the small preview right away. Clicking the preview or the expand button, or closing the scene tab, restores the original size and position; clicking Edit in main editor again while the scene is open shrinks it again.

Status and check

  • ㉓ Status button: green = OK or check passed, yellow = warnings only (runs on Jint, gradient advice, scene couldn't be read), red = errors (with the count), dim = rendering, checking or no script.
  • ㉔ Clicking it shows the details: time per chick frame, the execution path (fast path / Jint, with the line and reason), source and run errors and the check result. Click an error line to jump to that code.
  • Check: runs the install-time source check and checks the title (1–100 characters), then runs the effect with the declared defaults on all 20 sample chicks × the frame count (with the preview scene, end chick, motion, position and pivot as set) and lists run errors (each one once, "Chick N, step M:") and the slowest frame.
  • Without run errors it also gives gradient advice for each color setting: it draws with a sample gradient and points out, in a yellow line, color settings that still draw in one color when a gradient is chosen (for color lists the first item, for pair lists the first pair's replacement color; unused color settings are skipped). It is only a recommendation: it doesn't count as a problem and doesn't block publishing — picking colors per pixel with ctx.color makes it go away.
  • Upload…: saves, and when the check passes opens the Publish online window for this one effect (the short description starts as the declared description and the tags as the declared tags; the preview is made automatically). Effects reading other layers always get the layer-reference tag, and effects reading other frames the frame-reference tag. If the check fails, you are told to open the status button to see the problems.

Rules window

Clicking ④ Rules shows the rules, the answer format and the script API list in a separate window. Turn on ㉕ Include the current source to append the editor's source, and Copy puts it on the clipboard ("Copied the guide." shows briefly). How to use it: copy → paste into another AI such as ChatGPT on the web and describe the effect you want → paste the code block from the answer into the editor as a whole.

The rules cover: one source with one declaration; always writing name, description, tags and language (plus translations.en when not English); determinism (a seed setting for effects that suit randomness); run limits; setting types; adding useful settings without being asked (an anchor — pivot ctx.pivot, center ctx.center or source bounds ctx.source — plus direction, speed, loop, range, offset, color, strength and size, usually 3–8); picking colors per pixel with ctx.color("key", x, y) where possible so gradients are followed (lists ctx.colorAt, pairs ctx.pairColor, bounds { x, y, w, h } when the shape needs it, s.key only when one color is required — with worse/better examples); the fast path; reading other layers only through a layer setting and ctx.layer("key") (not by position or a name in the code, with the preview scene's layer name as the default, handling null); and other frames (transform, ctx.matchPixels, end chick).

Effect Maker rules and AI guidance require a moving effect to report its displacement with ctx.movePivot(dx, dy). Check warns if the resulting image moved without that call in the source, but publishing remains allowed. Scripts containing the call automatically receive the required pivot-move tag on upload; it cannot be removed and counts toward the eight-tag limit.

AI panel

  • The ⑦ AI button widens the window by the panel's width and opens the AI panel on the right (when maximized, the source area gets narrower). ㉖ Send the effect you want or what to change, and the AI (same Claude Code or Codex login as the AI chat) reads the source → writes the whole source into the editor (one undo step in the editor; drawings and layers stay) → checks it with the preview and Check, following the rules. It adds useful settings on its own and briefly lists them in its answer, makes effects that read other layers with a layer setting, and by default picks colors per pixel so gradients are followed. Saving, inserting into the layer and publishing are up to you with the buttons below. The conversation is gone when you close the window.
  • The header has the Claude/Codex switch, New chat (the source stays) and Close. If the CLI is missing or needs a login, install or log in with Open AI chat, then click Check again.
  • ㉗ The three buttons under the input attach an image file, the current canvas or the current preview frame; Ctrl+V and drag and drop work too. Click a chip to enlarge or remove it (as in the AI chat).
  • ㉘ The model list left of Send is the same list as the model field in the AI chat header. Changing it shows "Model: … — applies from the next message." and the conversation continues. The chosen AI and model are saved right away and kept the next time; the AI chat shares the same setting (the last choice wins).
  • Pressing Enter or Send while the AI's turn is running puts the message in the Queued row above the input (first line, image count and a remove button); it is sent in order when the current turn ends (even when stopped). Meanwhile Send shows only an arrow icon. Remove puts the message back into the input when the input is empty, and New chat clears the queue.
  • The AI panel can look at the open drawing, layers, effects and references but can't change them (the app blocks that with a separate access key for the panel). It reads, writes and checks the Effect Maker source and can read your other script effects for reference ("like my X effect, but …" — it finds and reads it without changing it). Ask it, for example "look it up on the web and make it", and it searches and reads the web (Claude: web search and web fetch; Codex: web search; shown in the AI panel as WebSearch, WebFetch or web_search tool rows). It doesn't search unless asked. Web results are text, so it can't see a page's pictures or animation; it builds the effect from the descriptions it read (motion, timing, shape, color, technique) and rewrites any code it saw with the app's effect API instead of pasting it.

Asset Manager

The Asset Manager handles effect (script effect), effect preset and gradient assets one at a time. It shows the assets you made on this computer and those you received from the Store or files, installs from the Store, exchanges files and publishes your own assets online. Each asset has its own author, license and Store information (Store ID and version). .chickxel-asset package files are only used to move assets: exporting bundles them, and importing or downloading adds the assets inside one by one (a file with several assets adds each separately, inheriting the file's author and license).

Use it to find and install effects and gradients others published in the Store, to get new versions of received assets, to give your effects or presets to a friend as a file, and to share effects you made (see Publish online).

Asset kindWhere it is used
Script effectThe Effects tab of the effects dialog (see Script effects). Can be published.
Effect presetThe Presets tab of the effects dialog (see Effect presets). Exchanged as files only, not published.
GradientThe gradient Presets window and everywhere you pick a gradient (see Gradient). Can be published.

Opening it

  • Open it with Tools > Asset Manager….
  • Open Store at the bottom of the Effects tab of the effects dialog opens the Store tab (effects), and the Asset Manager button under the list of the gradient Presets window opens it filtered to gradients. Show in Asset Manager in the right-click menu of an effect box in the effects dialog or of the Effect Maker list opens it with that asset selected.
  • Click Open in Chickxel on a chickxel.com package page (allow it if the browser asks to open Chickxel) and Chickxel comes to the front (starting if it isn't running), and the Store tab reloads the online list and opens with that package selected. Filters and search are cleared, and nothing is installed until you click Install in the details. If the package isn't in the list (hidden or removed), the status line says the package opened from the website isn't in the online list. Double-clicking a downloaded .chickxel-asset file adds it in the Installed tab just like importing, and selects the asset. If Chickxel is already running, it opens in that window. On Windows, links and double-clicking work after you have started Chickxel once (see Installation).

Installed tab

  • ① There are Installed, Store and Updates tabs.
  • ② Filter by Effects / Gradients (Effects at first) and search by name, author or Store name. Effects shows script effects and effect presets together.
  • ③ Import from File… checks a .chickxel-asset file and adds the assets inside one by one. Export… bundles the assets shown in the list into one file (each with its author and Store information; the ones you made get your account as author — a single asset exactly as received from the Store goes out as the same Store package). Publish Online… opens the Publish Online window below. Restore default effects puts back official effects you deleted and raises older versions to the version in the app (edited ones stay; the button is off until it finishes).
  • ④ The list shows My assets (assets made on this computer or whose author ID is your account handle) and then Received assets. Under each name are the author's profile picture (only for assets linked to the Store; a person icon otherwise) and the author's name, followed by the version for received assets, or 'Published 1.0.2' and 'modified' (if changed after publishing) for your published assets. Assets made on this computer show 'Made on this computer' instead of an author name.
  • ⑤ Shows the selected asset's preview (the first frame of the package preview picture if there is one, otherwise the effect animating on a sample picture; gradients as a strip), description, facts (kind, author, license, Store ID, version, modified, source, added, required app version, source hash, script run) and buttons, as listed in the table. Script effects installed through the Asset Manager (import from file or the Store) are trusted on install, so they draw without asking and Script run is Allowed. Only when a script isn't allowed yet — for example one you chose not to run when opening a project — an orange note says the asset contains script effects (never for official effect packages).
Selected assetButtons
My assetsFavorite (gradients), Edit in Effect Maker (script effects), Rename…, Edit parameters (effect presets), Export as Package File…, Publish Online… (Publish New Version… if already published; not for effect presets), Delete. Edited in place.
Received assetsFavorite (gradients), Copy to My Assets, Export as Package File…, Open Web Page (when the author has a website), View License (when there is a license file), Delete. Editing makes your own copy; the original keeps receiving updates.
  • Delete removes the asset right away (there is no restore bin). Official effects that the app installs by default aren't reinstalled once deleted; bring them back with Restore default effects. Effects and gradients already in a project are copied in, so they stay even if you delete the asset.
  • Importing from a file checks the file list, sizes, checksums, effect settings and preview images first. If anything is wrong, each problem is listed and nothing is added; an asset that needs a newer Chickxel tells you which version. If the same Store asset or an asset with the same content already exists, the Import Asset window asks Replace / Keep Both / Skip, and Apply to the remaining items uses your choice for the remaining overlaps.
  • On first launch, your old effect presets (formerly custom effects) and gradient presets move into My assets. The old files stay in the settings folder as effect-presets.backup-date.json and gradient-presets.backup-date.json.
  • Export… and Export as Package File… make package files for exchanging assets. Assets you made get your signed-in account's display name as author.

Store tab

  • ⑥ The online list is downloaded from chickxel.com the first time you open the tab ("Loading the online list…" while loading, then "N assets · time downloaded"). There are the Effects / Gradients filter, search (name, description, author, tags), the All tags list, Refresh and Open on Website (opens the chickxel.com assets page on the tab of the kind you are filtering).
  • ⑦ Each row shows the name and, under it, the author's profile picture and name, the version, the kind and the status (installed version, new version, or can't be installed on this version). Names and descriptions appear in the display language when the package has a translation.
  • ⑧ Preview image, short description, ID, version, installed version, author, license, kind, required app version, size, published, updated, review (Official Chickxel / Reviewed / Community — uploaded by users), tags, and the Install (or Update to …, Show in Installed) and Open Web Page buttons.
  • Clicking Install shows a progress bar, percentage and Cancel in its place. If the downloaded file doesn't match the checksum in the list, Chickxel only reports an error and changes nothing. An asset that can't be used on this version shows why, for example "Can't install: requires Chickxel 1.20.0 or later", and its Install button is off. If an asset linked to the same Store package is already installed, it is replaced without asking, but only by a higher version (see Store versions and local edits below). Assets from the Store have the source Online catalog.
  • The 23 official effects such as Outline and Shadow are installed the first time the app starts (source Installed with the app). The Store also has 8 official gradients (Rainbow, Repeating Rainbow, Sunset, Ocean, Transparent Fade, Fire, Forest, Grayscale) and effect presets (Outlined Drop Shadow, Hit Reaction, Shiny Pickup).
  • Author profile pictures are downloaded only when the Store list has a chickxel.com profile picture address; they are kept small on this computer and reused (official effects use the Chickxel picture). Without a picture, or when it can't be downloaded, a person icon is shown.

Updates tab

  • Check for updates downloads the online list again and compares it with assets that have Store information (even if imported from a file, when the Store ID matches). Each asset with a new version gets an "Installed 1.0.0 → new version 1.1.0" row and an Update to … button. New versions are installed only when you click, and effects already in projects stay as they are. Local edits are replaced by the new version without asking. The tab name shows the number of updates (e.g. Updates (1)), or "All received assets are up to date." when there are none.
  • Without an internet connection, the Store and Updates tabs say in orange that the online list couldn't be reached and show the last downloaded list instead. Installed assets keep working.

Store versions and local edits

Assets from the Store stay linked to their Store package and receive updates. Downloading from the Store (Install on the Store tab, the Updates tab, or downloading a published effect in the Effect Maker) works like this:

  • The package ID and version inside the downloaded file must match the online list, or nothing is installed.
  • If an asset linked to the same package is already installed, it is overwritten only by a higher version. Local edits are replaced by the new version without asking, and favorites are kept. The same or a lower version isn't installed (nothing is downgraded).
  • If several assets are linked to the same package (duplicates from earlier), only one is kept.
  • If it overlaps an unlinked asset with the same content, the Import Asset window asks what to do. Importing from a file asks for every overlap.
  • To change a received asset, first make your own copy with Copy to My Assets. The copy has no Store link, so updates never overwrite it. Received script effects are read-only in the Effect Maker too (see Effect Maker).
  • Effects already placed on project layers keep the source and values they were inserted with inside the project, so they don't change when the asset is updated or deleted.

Using received assets

  • Received effects appear on the Effects tab of the effects dialog, and received effect presets on the Presets tab, together with your own. Their tooltips show the Store name (or the author), and Rename, Parameters and Delete are only for your own (see Effect presets).
  • In the gradient Presets window, received gradients show the Store name (author) next to their name. Edit one and click Save to My Assets to keep the original and make your own copy (see Gradient).
  • To modify a received asset, make your own copy with Copy to My Assets in its details.

Publish Online

Turns one of your assets into a public package and uploads it with your Chickxel account (one package per asset). If you aren't signed in, the account window opens first. Publish Online… at the top of the Installed tab opens it for the asset selected in the list (or the only one shown, if none is selected); Publish Online… or Publish New Version… in your asset's details and publishing from the Effect Maker open it for that asset. Received assets can't be published — you're told to copy one to My assets and publish the copy. Effect presets aren't published (chickxel.com doesn't accept them either); publish effects as script effects.

  • ⑨ Author: the profile picture, display name and @handle (author ID) of the account you are signed in with are shown together and can't be edited here. Change them in Help > Chickxel Account… (the handle can't change after you upload a package).
  • ⑩ Package: the top line tells whether this is a new package or an update (new package / 'Name update: old → new version' / checking / couldn't check because of the connection — plus a note when a package of yours with the same name gets the new version). Then name, short description (up to 200 characters; scripts are filled in from the declaration's name and description), Languages… (a name and short description per language, shown to people viewing the Store and chickxel.com in that language; languages with text show green with a check), package ID, version and tags. The package ID can't be edited and is set automatically: the ID of your published package if the asset came from it (or a copy of it), otherwise 'handle.name-part' (a short hash if the name has no English letters). The same handle and name means an update, so rename to publish a different package. The version field suggests the next patch version (1.0.0 → 1.0.1). Type tags like #neon and add a chip with Space, Enter or a comma; remove one with its X, or with Backspace in an empty field (up to 8, lowercase English letters, digits and hyphens, 32 characters each; rule breakers turn orange). Effects that read other layers always get the layer-reference tag and effects that read other frames the frame-reference tag pre-filled (can't be removed; counts toward the 8 — if the tags are already full, a check error asks you to remove another tag).
  • ⑪ License: always CC0-1.0. Anyone may copy, change and use the asset without conditions (including commercial use, without credit), and the package includes the CC0 text as LICENSE.txt.
  • ⑫ Asset: the name and kind of the asset being published. It becomes the package's asset.
  • ⑬ List preview: the picture shown in the Store list and details and on chickxel.com. It starts as the asset's preview; replace it with a PNG (up to 512×512, 1 MB) via Replace with Image File… or go back with Use Asset Preview. Effects with a preview scene also upload previews with the scene layers hidden (with and without the effect), which chickxel.com details can switch to with 'Hide helper layers' (the effect still draws as it read those layers; not included when you use your own image). The automatic preview is an animated PNG (APNG) of the effect run over several steps (10 for effect presets, the declaration's previewFrames for script effects, 0.1 s each) on the chosen chick. A step identical to the one before is merged, so an effect that doesn't move becomes a single image. The package also gets the original without the effect, previews/<name>.original.png, so hovering the large picture on the chickxel.com asset page compares the original (left of a vertical line) with the effect (right) — not included when you choose your own picture. Exporting a package adds the same previews; the Asset Manager details and this window show only the first frame.
  • ⑭ Pre-upload check: checked as you type, with the same rules as the server. Red X = error (blocks uploading), orange exclamation mark = warning (you can still upload).

Uploading

  1. With no errors, a blue check says all checks passed and Upload turns on. Click it.
  2. While uploading, a progress bar and Cancel Upload appear. If the server rejects the package, the reason appears at the end of the checks as 'Rejected by the server: …'.
  3. Once uploaded, the package appears right away in the Store list and on the chickxel.com assets page (chickxel.com/packages/<ID>), and a message says it was uploaded. The package page isn't opened automatically: click Open website in the message to open it in your browser (OK just closes). The asset gets Store information, so later uploads become new versions of the same package.

After pressing Upload, its label shows 'Uploading… N%' with the same percentage as the progress bar below. When transfer finishes and the server checks and saves the package, it shows 'Checking on server…' and an indeterminate bar. Upload cannot be pressed again while busy; Cancel upload stops it.

Checks and tags

  • Main errors: package ID, version (SemVer), name, short description, language settings or tag format; no room for a required tag; no list preview; a name that clashes with another package; a version not higher than the highest one already published (online list and this computer's upload records) — "Enter a version higher than X" (published versions can't change). Warning: no tags.
  • Uploading a script effect whose source uses ctx.movePivot automatically adds the pivot-move tag. Like layer-reference and frame-reference it cannot be removed and counts toward the eight-tag limit.

Deleting a published package

When a published package is deleted on chickxel.com (by you, by an admin, or through report handling), it disappears from the site, the Store list and your profile, the XP it earned is removed, and its storage quota is returned right away. The server still keeps it, though, so an admin can restore it or delete it permanently. Until an admin does either, the deleted package's ID can't be used again: publishing with the same ID is refused by the server.

Preview

The preview at the top right plays the animation, effects included, at real speed. It runs separately from the frame you're editing, so you can check the motion while drawing.

NumberItem and use
①The playback view. If a tag is selected in the timeline, it plays that tag following its direction and loop settings (a tag with Loop off plays once and stops); with no tag selected it loops all frames. Click the image to open the enlarged view.
②The preview-only FPS (0–60). 0 plays each frame for its own duration; 1 or more plays every frame at that rate (0 at first).
③Click to cycle Fit → 1× → 2× → 4×. Click the small down arrow at its right end for a list of zooms (Fit, 1×, 2×, 4×, with a dot at the current one) to pick one directly; right-clicking the button opens the same list.
④The background button (a swatch of the current background). Click it to cycle checker → black → white → gray → green → custom color; click the small down arrow at its right end to open the background list. Right-clicking the button opens the same list.

Playback

  • Toggle playback with the play button or Shift+Enter.
  • Tap Space for under 0.4 seconds in the main window to play/pause the preview, like Shift+Enter. Hold it while dragging to pan the canvas without changing playback. Focused text fields, buttons, combo boxes and active text editing receive Space instead.
  • If effect computing falls behind, playback keeps showing the current frame until the next one is ready (it never inserts blank frames), with a loading icon while it waits.
  • FPS, zoom and background are app settings, not project settings, so they carry over to other documents and the next launch.

Copying and saving

⑤ Right-click the preview image to open copy and save commands.

CommandWhat it does
Copy imageCopies the shown frame to the clipboard as a transparent PNG without the preview background.
Copy enlarged (×4)Copies a PNG enlarged 4× with crisp pixels. This is useful for pasting into chats such as Discord.
Save frame…Saves the shown frame as <document>_<frame number>.png.
Save as GIF…Opens the Export as animated GIF dialog.

During playback it copies or saves the frame that was visible when you right-clicked; you do not need to stop playback.

Background

⑥ Choose checkerboard, black, white, gray, green, or a custom color from the background list. Picking the custom color from the list opens the color picker; cycling with the button uses the last custom color as is.

The background is for the preview only and never goes into the image or exports. Copy image and Save frame also stay transparent without it.

Outfit

In projects with groups, an Outfit row appears below the preview. Each group has a list to choose the skin to show (disabled if it has one skin; the first skin is marked "(default)"). Choosing one changes that group's skin while skins chosen for individual parts stay. A * after a group name means some parts use individually chosen skins. Click a group name or Manage… to open the skin manager.

Enlarged view and preview tab

⑦ The enlarged preview shows the playing image, outfit and background at a whole-number zoom fitted to its window.

Click the hand-cursor preview image to open a resizable Enlarged preview window showing the same playback, background and outfit at the largest integer zoom that fits. Only one opens and clicking again brings it forward. Its size is remembered for the current app session. Inside, Space plays/pauses, Esc closes and right-click opens the preview menu. With a preview tab open, clicking the image focuses that tab instead.

In the enlarged window and the preview tab, the wheel zooms around the cursor (0.25× steps from 1 to 2×, 0.5× steps from 2 to 4×, and the step doubles with every doubling above that, up to 64×), and dragging pans. Double-click to fit to the window again; in the enlarged window 0 also fits.

Use Open as tab in the preview header to show the editing document larger in a split-view preview tab. While that tab is open, clicking the preview image brings the tab forward.

Export dialog

The Export dialog sets how the project is exported as PNGs for Unity and other tools. There are two outputs, the frame sheet that lays out the frames and the part atlas that packs part images, and the settings are saved in the project (.chickxel).

Opening and layout

Open it with Ctrl+E or File > Export…. Settings apply as soon as you change them (one undo step per setting), and the blue ① Export button at the bottom closes the dialog and exports. If export on save is on, Ctrl+S alone exports too. There are two tabs: ② Export and ③ Import Settings.

  • ④ Left = settings, ⑤ right = output preview (sheet and cell name list per file). Drag ⑥ the divider to split the width; the dialog size and collapsed sections are remembered.

The window position, size, split ratio and collapsed sections are app settings, so they stay the same in other projects.

Setting sections

SectionMain settings
⑦ CommonOutput (frame sheet, part atlas, both), skins (current outfit, chosen skins), skin packing, layout (grid, auto layout), sort order, cell count, separate files, cell names, spacing between cells, trim borders, power of two, scale
⑧ FilesExport on save, folder, file format (PNG/JPG/TGA/BMP), Unity .meta (off, auto, always)
⑨ Frame sheetName, Single frame, path
⑩ Part atlasName, keep original positions, Sprite Library, path
⑪ AdvancedPer sheet

The frame sheet and part atlas share the same sheet settings. Turn on ⑪ Advanced > Per sheet to keep separate settings for the part atlas.

Common section

A new project starts with the frame sheet only, grid layout, the current outfit, automatic cell count, 1 px spacing between cells and 1× scale. The numbered settings:

  • ⑫ Cell count (grid layout): auto (smallest sheet), N columns or N rows (1–1024, 4 at first).
  • ⑬ Sort order: drag the part, skin, tag and frame rows or use Alt+↑/↓ to reorder them, and use New row here between rows to set where lines break. Sorting changes only positions; cell names stay the same.
  • ⑭ Separate files: split files per tag and per part (both can be on). Splitting per skin is chosen under Skin packing.
  • ⑮ Cell names: Frame number or By tag (default for new projects). They're used as sprite names in Unity .meta, so changing them shows a warning.
  • ⑯ Choose skins…: choose skins to export, outfit presets and layers to include in the composite.
SettingWhat it does
LayoutGrid places cells of equal size in order; Auto layout packs cells of different sizes tightly.
Max width and packing order (auto layout)The sheet's maximum width (0 = auto) and the order cells are packed in (In sort order or Largest first).
Spacing between cells0–64 px (1 px at first).
Trim bordersCuts the transparent border off each cell (off at first).
Power of twoRounds the sheet width and height up to powers of two (off at first).

Scale (at the bottom of the Common group, below power of two; 1–16× — type a number or pick 1, 2, 3, 4, 6 or 8): enlarges the frame sheet and part atlas images by a whole number (nearest neighbor, so pixels stay crisp). Cells are cut out first and then enlarged, so cell spacing and maximum width are px in the enlarged result (spacing 1 stays 1px). 9-slice borders and the Unity PPU (Pixels Per Unit in the .meta) are multiplied by the same scale, so the size in Unity stays the same (PPU 16 · scale 3 → 48), and the pivot stays on the same spot. Single frame sheets are enlarged too (canvas × scale). The preview on the right shows the enlarged result; the motion test shows the original size. As a common setting both outputs use it; with per-sheet settings on, the part atlas has its own. It is saved in the project file and export presets; GIF export has its own scale.

Files and per-output settings

  • Files section: export on save (on at first), folder (empty = the project folder), file format PNG, JPG, TGA or BMP (JPG quality 1–100%, 90 at first), Unity .meta off, auto or always (auto at first = written only inside a Unity Assets folder or where a .meta already exists).
  • Frame sheet section: name (empty = the project name) and path. Single frame appears only when Sprite Mode in Import Settings is Single; it exports one chosen frame at canvas size (only the scale applies) and ignores the common layout settings.
  • Part atlas section: name (empty = the frame sheet name + _parts), Keep original positions (auto layout; on at first), Sprite Library (also writes the part .meta) and path.
  • Turning on Advanced > Per sheet copies the current common values into a second set of settings for the part atlas.
  • The folder and the sheet and part atlas names are written to the project file encrypted with this install's own key, so this computer's paths aren't revealed. As a result, opened on another computer or by another user, or after the settings folder is deleted, they go back to the defaults (the project folder and the project name) (see File types and where they go).

Presets

  • ⑰ Preset (far right of the tab row): saves the current settings under a name in the app settings so you can load them in other projects. Includes built-in presets ("Frame sheet (default)", "Part atlas + Sprite Library", "Separate file per skin"), reset to defaults, rename, overwrite, delete, and JSON export/import. Scale, JPG quality, .meta and export on save are included; per-project values such as file names and folders, chosen skins and outfits, excluded layers, the Single frame number and Keep original positions are not.

Import Settings

⑱ Import Settings tab (shown when .meta is Auto or Always): sets the same names and values as Unity's Texture Importer.

FieldValues (initial value)
Sprite ModeSingle or Multiple (Single). Frame sheet only; the part atlas is always Multiple
Pixels Per Unit1–10000 (16)
Mesh Type · Extrude EdgesFull Rect or Tight (Full Rect); Extrude Edges 0–32 px (1, with Tight)
Pivot9 presets or custom X/Y 0–1 (center). Single only
Generate Physics ShapeOn
AdvancedsRGB (on), Alpha Source (Input Texture Alpha), Alpha Is Transparency (on), Read/Write (off), Mip Maps (off)
Wrap · Filter · AnisoWrap Mode (Clamp), Filter Mode Point, Bilinear or Trilinear (Point), Aniso Level 0–16 (1)
Default platformMax Size (auto or 32–16384), Resize Algorithm (Mitchell), Format (Automatic), Compression (None), Use Crunch (off)

The initial values suit pixel art (Point, no compression, mipmaps off, Clamp).

Output preview and progress

⑤ The output preview lists each file's sheet size and its cells (sprite name, source frame, position in the sheet). Clicking a row takes the editor to that frame (and part and skin, for parts), and the current cell is highlighted. Outputs that are off, or export on save being off, are noted too.

When an export takes a while, a dark translucent overlay covers the whole window until it finishes. A box in the middle shows Exporting, a spinning loading icon, the percentage with a progress bar and Editing is paused until this finishes; meanwhile drawing, shortcuts and file drops are ignored and other windows (such as the AI chat) are disabled.

See also the Files chapter for saving and Unity integration.

Export as animated GIF

File > Export as animated GIF… creates a single GIF for sharing. The project and its export settings aren't changed. Save as GIF… in the preview's right-click menu opens the same dialog.

Settings

  • ① Range: current tag (disabled when the current frame isn't in a tag), all frames, or ⑧ choose a tag (disabled without tags; the list shows each tag's frames and reverse or ping-pong). It starts with the current tag if there is one, otherwise all frames, and frames follow the tag's direction.
  • ② Zoom: 1–10×, nearest neighbor so pixels stay crisp (initially the zoom that makes the result about 256 px).
  • ③ Speed: Original speed (each frame's own duration, the default), Speed multiplier (0.1–10×, every frame's duration divided by the multiplier — 2× plays twice as fast, 0.5× at half speed) or Fixed FPS (1–50, every frame lasts 1 s ÷ FPS). The ⑥ preview and the ⑦ duration follow the chosen speed; the project's frame durations don't change. Upload to gallery has the same setting.
  • ④ Loop: loop forever or play 1–100 times (1 = once).
  • ⑤ Background: transparent (alpha below 50% becomes transparent, the rest opaque) or composited over a background color.

Speed, loop and background are app settings and stay the same next time (speed is shared with Upload to gallery); range and zoom are worked out again each time the dialog opens.

Preview and saving

⑥ The preview on the right plays at the chosen speed, and ⑦ shows the output size, frame count, duration and color count. GIF frame durations are rounded to hundredths of a second and never shorter than 20 ms (so Fixed FPS goes up to 50). With few enough colors (255 with transparency, otherwise 256) the GIF is lossless; with more, each frame gets its own palette and colors are reduced (no dithering).

Export… opens a save dialog. The default name is <project>.gif next to the project (<project>_<tag>.gif for a tag); a never-saved project uses the last folder you used. After saving, the dialog stays open showing the file size and result, so you can change settings and save again (Cancel becomes Close). You can assign a shortcut in Preferences > Shortcuts.

If compositing the preview frames or writing the GIF takes a while, a dark translucent overlay covers the whole window until it finishes, with Compositing frames or Creating GIF and the percentage with a progress bar in a box in the middle.

Upload to gallery

File > Upload to Gallery… (below GIF export) or the gallery button (store icon) at the right of the menu bar uploads the current picture to the chickxel.com gallery. An animation can go up as a GIF in full or just some of its frames.

Before uploading

  • ① Picture: Current frame (PNG) or Animation (GIF) (the frames of the range in ② at the chosen speed, looping forever, transparent background — composed like GIF export; disabled for a single frame). Scale goes from 1× up to the largest zoom that keeps the result within 2048 px wide and tall, at most 10× (1× at first); the info line below shows the size and the frame number (PNG) or the frame count (GIF). Pictures larger than 2048 px can't be uploaded. Speed (enabled only for GIF) is the same setting as in Export as animated GIF — Original speed, Speed multiplier or Fixed FPS — and both windows remember the last choice.
  • ② Frames to upload as the GIF (enabled only with Animation (GIF)): All frames (the default, in order), Tag (one tag from the list, in its playback direction — reverse and ping-pong included; the current frame's tag at first, disabled when there are no tags), Frames (start to end number, counting from 1; plays backwards when the start is greater than the end) and Frames selected in the timeline (shown only when two or more frames were selected in the timeline before opening the window; those frames in number order). Changing the tag list or a number field picks that option for you. The info line's frame count and the ⑤ preview follow the range right away; with no frames to upload or more than 1000, the info line shows why in red and Upload is disabled.
  • ③ Details: title (1–100 characters, the file name at first), description (optional, up to 2000 characters) and tags (optional, comma-separated, up to 8, each up to 32 lowercase letters, digits and hyphens).
  • ④ The account to upload with is shown with its profile picture, name and level chip; Chickxel Account… opens the account window (see Chickxel account). The account is checked when the app starts, so it shows the confirmed details from the start; if it couldn't be checked yet, the window says it will be checked when you upload.
  • ⑤ A still image of the current frame for PNG; for GIF, the frames chosen in ② play in that order at the chosen speed, over and over (the preview size doesn't change with the scale). Below it is a notice that uploads are public right away and that you shouldn't upload other people's work or pictures you have no rights to.
  • ⑥ View Gallery at the bottom left opens the chickxel.com gallery in your browser, so you can browse what others have uploaded without uploading anything.

Sharing the editable file

Turn on Also share the editable .chickxel file (off by default) near the bottom of group ① to upload this project's .chickxel file along with the PNG or GIF, so anyone can download it from the picture's page and open and edit it in Chickxel. The shared copy drops this computer's export folder and keeps the sheet and part atlas names as plain names without folders (see File types and where they go). Script effects' source code is included (the window says so when it's on), and people are asked whether to run it when they open the file (see Script effects). The file can be up to 8 MiB; if it's larger, you're told to turn off file sharing and upload only the picture. The gallery post has separate buttons to download the PNG/GIF and the Chickxel file.

Allowing image download

Allow image download (PNG/GIF) (on by default) at the bottom of group ① decides whether visitors to the picture's page on chickxel.com see the PNG/GIF download button. When it's off, only you and admins see that button; the picture itself stays public. Visitors who aren't signed in are sent to the sign-in page when they click that button instead of downloading; after signing in they return to the same picture page and can download by clicking again. It's separate from downloading the editable .chickxel file (Also share the editable .chickxel file above). You can change it later with Edit on the picture's page.

Uploading

Clicking Upload first opens the account window if you aren't signed in. While the picture is being made, Making the picture is shown; while uploading, a progress bar and Cancel Upload appear; when done, the picture's page (chickxel.com/gallery/…) opens in your browser. A file over 4 MB is refused with a hint to lower the scale or upload just the current frame. If you hit the daily upload limit, you're asked to try again in a few minutes. The project isn't changed.

Uploaded pictures earn XP for your account (see Account level). After the upload the app fetches your account again; if your XP went up, the completion message adds the XP gained and your current level, like +10 XP · Lv 3, and "Level up! Lv 3" if you reached a new level (nothing is added if the server doesn't report levels). Once a document is uploaded, the gallery button in the menu bar sparkles again the next time you save it by hand.

Tool options

Below the preview on the right. ① The title row shows the selected tool's name (e.g. Tool settings · Pencil) and ② only that tool's settings appear. Per-tool details are in the Tools chapter. Drag the border between the preview and Tool options to share the height; it is kept for the next launch.

Tool settings aren't part of undo and are kept for the next launch, except whether horizontal or vertical symmetry drawing is on (Mirror around pivot is kept).

For the Pivot tool (K) settings (5×5 buttons, X/Y, From current frame on), see Pivot.

Collapsing

Click the 'Tool options · tool name' title in the right dock to collapse the entire tool-options panel; the arrow points right when closed. Selecting exactly one timeline effect adds a separate black 'Effects' header below it and an 'Effect · name' group; that header collapses its panel independently. Each group (Brush, Options, effect name) also has a header arrow: right when collapsed, down when expanded. Click a group title to animate the fold; its state lasts for the app session.

Settings of the selected effect

Select the single ③ Outline effect in the timeline to show its separate ④ Effects settings panel below Tool options.

Select one timeline effect to edit its specific settings directly in the 'Effect · name' group in the tool dock: colors, numbers, sliders, choices and animated easing, repeats, held end and pivot movement. Changes update the document immediately; rapid consecutive edits merge into one undo step. The group disappears on other selections or multiple selected effects. Use Open in Effects dialog… to open that effect selected for frame range, clipping, script editing or permanent application. The Expand canvas… button next to it opens a small window that grows the canvas to fit that effect's result (see Effects dialog). If a script cannot render, its reason appears as a warning in the group.

Gradient color strips, editing rows and color-list items in the right-dock effect settings fit the dock's width. At narrow widths the value wraps below its label instead of overflowing horizontally. In wider Effects and Effect Maker dialogs they keep their normal width.

During transform and resize modes

During transform (Ctrl+T), canvas size (Ctrl+Alt+C) and transform whole layer (Alt+Shift+C), the panel shows that mode's number fields and Apply/Cancel buttons instead of the tool settings and effect group. Transform and Transform whole layer use the same transform group; only its title differs, Transform · current cel / Transform · whole layer.

Status bar

The bottom line of the window shows the current state in cells, with ⑦ the latest message at the right end. The cells are for reading only; they can't be clicked.

CellWhat it shows
① Cursor coordinatesThe pixel under the cursor (x, y). Empty outside the canvas.
② ColorThe color of the combined image under the cursor (HEX), or "Transparent".
③ SizeThe canvas size (width × height).
④ Frame/layerFrame current/total · L number layer name, followed by the part size for a part or "(group)" for a group.
⑤ ZoomThe canvas zoom (%).
⑥ Save stateA dot and "Modified" when there are unsaved changes, otherwise "Saved".
⑦ MessagesThe result of the last action and hints (what's selected in the timeline and what Delete will do, save and open results, and so on). It changes with each new message and may end with "N unsaved files".

Progress for long tasks appears in a box in the middle of the window, not in the status bar (see Export).

With the external AI connection on, a plug icon and an External AI cell appear at the right end, briefly highlighting the name of each tool another AI app calls (Using other AI apps).

Tools

The tools sit on the toolbar at the left of the window, and the settings of the selected tool appear in Tool options on the right. The table below sorts the tools by purpose. Click a name to open that tool's page.

PurposeToolsUse it to
BasicsTool list · Drawing basics · Tool groups · Brush settingslearn the mouse and key rules and the brush shape, size and symmetry that all drawing tools share
DrawingPencil · Gradient Pencil · Shape Pen · Eraser · Shape · Textdraw and erase lines, or add shapes and text
ColorFill · Gradient · Eyedropper · Dodge/burn · Dithering · Replace color brushpaint large areas at once, pick colors from the picture, or change the shading, pattern or color of what is already painted
Selection and transformSelection tools · Move · Transform mode · Canvas size mode · Transform whole layerselect part of the picture to move, rotate or scale it, change the canvas size, or transform every frame of a layer together
UnityPivot · 9-sliceset the reference point and the stretchable border of sprites exported to Unity

Each tool page starts with a summary of what the tool does, followed by detailed sections for its settings. In the app, press F1 and then click a toolbar slot or the tool options to open that tool's page directly.

Tool list

When you select a tool in the ① toolbar, its settings appear in ② Tool options in the right dock. Long explanations live in i icon tooltips so they don't take up space: usage hints for Rectangle select, Ellipse select, Lasso select, Magic wand, Move and Gradient sit next to the Tool settings · … title, and the explanations of the canvas size, transform, gradient, 9-slice and eyedropper groups sit next to each group title. Hover the i to read them.

The numbers in the table match the toolbar slots in the picture above. Tools that share a number are grouped in one slot (Tool groups).

ToolShortcutWhat it does
③ PencilBPaints pixels in the brush shape. Supports pixel perfect at size 1 (P) and symmetry drawing
③ Gradient PencilShift+BPaints along the stroke with gradient colors
③ Shape Pen—Stamps shapes from the chosen set (leaves, flowers, sparkles and more)
④ EraserEErases pixels to transparent. With a lower strength or fewer channels it erases gradually or only some color channels
⑤ FillGFills an area of the same (or similar) color
⑤ GradientFDrag to paint a linear gradient
⑥ EyedropperI, Alt+clickPicks a color from the canvas
⑦ ShapeRDrags out 11 shapes such as lines, curves, rectangles, ellipses, triangles, polygons and stars. L switches straight to the line, and U toggles Fill
⑧ TextTTypes text right on the canvas and creates a text layer
⑨ Rectangle select / Ellipse selectMDrag to select an area
⑨ Lasso selectQDraw an outline to select
⑨ Magic wandWSelects an area of similar color
⑩ MoveVMoves the selection. With no selection it shifts the whole cel, and pixels pushed off the canvas are kept (Moving and pixels outside the canvas)
⑪ Dodge/burnDRub with the left button to lighten and the right button to darken. The mode picks hue shift, saturation and more
⑫ Dithering—Paints by mixing primary and secondary colors in a pattern
⑬ Replace color brushCRepaints only the first color it touches with another color
⑭ PivotKSets the pivot
⑮ 9-sliceSSets the Unity sprite border

Whatever tool you're using, holding Ctrl over the canvas temporarily switches to the Move tool, and releasing it returns to the original tool. In drawing tools, holding Alt temporarily switches to the eyedropper.

Drawing basics

These rules are shared by the brush tools (pencil, gradient pencil, eraser, lighten/darken, dithering, color replace brush) and by the shape, fill and gradient tools. What differs per tool is on that tool's page.

Mouse buttons

  • The left button paints with the ① primary color and the right button with the ② secondary color. Press X to swap the two colors.
  • With ③ Right button = eraser on, the right button of the pencil, gradient pencil, shape and fill tools erases instead of using the secondary color. Turn it on in the Options group of the tool options or in Drawing options in the Tools menu. The pencil and gradient pencil follow the eraser's strength and channels; the shape and fill tools always erase completely.
  • Tools where the buttons mean something else: Dodge/burn lightens with the left button and darkens with the right; with Dithering the right button swaps where the two colors go; the Eyedropper puts the picked color into the primary (left) or secondary (right) color. The Gradient tool drags with the left button only.

Strokes and undo

  • Everything from pressing a button to releasing it is one stroke, and one stroke is one undo step. The undo history shows the tool name (Lighten or Darken for lighten/darken, the shape name for shapes).
  • Pressing the other button mid-stroke cancels that stroke and restores the picture to how it was before it. The status bar shows Cancel stroke and nothing is added to the undo history.
  • Colors painted with the pencil, fill, shape, dithering and color replace brush tools are added to the recent colors in the color panel. Strokes of the eraser, lighten/darken, gradient and gradient pencil, and strokes that paint transparency, are not added.

Using keys

④ The top bar always shows a one-line hint of what the current tool can do. These keys work with the drawing tools:

KeyWhat it does
Shift+clickBrush tools: draws a straight line from the end of the last stroke to where you click. While Shift is held, the line is previewed translucently, including brush size and symmetry (Pencil). Shapes: keeps the proportions (15° steps for lines, squares, circles and so on). Gradient: 45° steps.
AltWhile held, the tool becomes the eyedropper and clicking picks a color. Releasing it returns to the original tool.
CtrlWhile held, the tool becomes the Move tool. Releasing it returns to the original tool.
Ctrl+wheelChanges the brush size by 1. The status bar shows the current size.
[ / ]Decreases or increases the brush size by 1 (1–32).
XSwaps the primary and secondary colors.

Where paint goes

  • With a selection, painting and erasing happen only inside it.
  • With symmetry drawing on, every drawing tool except the gradient tool also paints at the mirrored positions (Brush settings).
  • In tile mode, strokes that cross the canvas edge continue on the opposite side.
  • If the current layer is locked, nothing is drawn and the status bar shows The current layer is locked and can't be drawn on.

Drawing commands in the Edit menu

The Edit menu has commands that change the whole picture (or the selection) at once. Each is one undo step.

CommandWhat it does
⑤ Add outline (primary color) (Ctrl+Alt+O)
Add outline (with diagonals)		Adds a 1-pixel outline: within the selection (or the whole current cel), transparent pixels that touch an opaque pixel above, below, left or right are painted with the primary color. Add outline (with diagonals) also paints pixels that touch diagonally.
⑥ Replace color… (Ctrl+Alt+R)Set Color to replace (the primary color at first) and New color (the secondary color at first) to change every pixel of that color in the current frame of the current layer. All transparent pixels (alpha 0) count as the same color. With a selection, Selection only limits the change to it. To change only where you rub, use the Replace color brush.
⑦ Flip horizontal (selection/cel) (Shift+H)
Flip vertical (selection/cel) (Shift+V)		Flips the selected pixels, or the current cel when nothing is selected, horizontally or vertically.
⑦ Rotate 90° (selection/canvas) (Ctrl+R)
Rotate -90° (selection/canvas) (Ctrl+Shift+R)		Rotates the selected pixels by 90° (press Enter to drop them), or the whole canvas with all frames when nothing is selected.
⑧ Canvas size… (Ctrl+Alt+C)Sets the new size and the image position right on the canvas (Canvas size mode).

Tool groups

Similar tools share one toolbar slot: Pencil (pencil, gradient pencil, shape pen), ① Fill (fill, gradient) and ② Select (rectangle, ellipse, lasso, magic wand). Group slots have a small triangle in the bottom-right corner.

  • A plain click selects the tool shown in the slot.
  • Long-press or right-click to expand ③ the list of tools in the group.

A group slot shows the tool you picked last. Picking a tool with its shortcut switches its slot to that tool as well.

Brush settings

The brush is the shape and size painted by one dab. The pencil, gradient pencil, eraser, lighten/darken, dithering and color replace brush use it, and the shape tool uses it as the line width. Symmetry drawing is explained on this page as well.

Shape and size

  • ① Pick the shape: Square / Circle / Diamond. ② The box on the left shows exactly which pixels the current shape and size will paint.
  • ③ Size 1–32: type in the number field or click or drag up and down on the up/down buttons at its right end, or change it by 1 with [ / ] or Ctrl+wheel over the canvas.

Size 1 is a single pixel whatever the shape. The circle equals the square up to size 2 and becomes a five-pixel cross at size 3. The diamond covers the pixels whose horizontal plus vertical distance from the center is at most half the size. Even sizes extend one pixel further to the right and down from the center.

Which tools share a brush

  • The eraser remembers its own brush shape and size. The other brush tools and the Shape tool share one brush, so a size set in the pencil is also used by lighten/darken, dithering and shapes. Both brushes are kept for the next launch.
  • ④ In the Shape tool the same group sets the line width and pen shape. The inside of a filled shape is filled regardless of the brush.

Symmetry drawing

  • ⑤ Horizontal symmetry (Shift+X) and vertical symmetry (Shift+Y) also draw on the opposite side of the canvas center axis. They apply to every tool that uses symmetry (pencil, gradient pencil, eraser, shapes and so on).
  • ⑥ Around pivot (Tools menu Drawing options > Mirror around pivot): the axes become the vertical and horizontal lines through the current layer's pivot (in half-pixel steps). It uses the part pivot for a part, and that layer's pivot in the current frame for a normal layer or group (Pivot tool); the guide lines go through the pivot too. Mirrored points that fall outside the canvas aren't painted (in tile mode they wrap around).

With symmetry on, brush tools show a gray brush outline (ghost cursor) at every mirrored position while over the canvas (left-right, top-bottom and, with both, the diagonal).

Apply to frames

Apply to frames draws the stroke on the current frame and then applies it the same way, on the same layer and at the same spot, to the other frames in the chosen range. Use it to fix a pattern that runs across several frames in one go. The group appears in the tool settings of the pencil, gradient pencil, shape pen, eraser, fill, gradient, shape, lighten/darken, dither and replace brush tools, and the info mark next to its title explains how it works.

RangeFrames that also get the stroke
Current frame onlyOnly the current frame (the app always starts with this).
Up to N frames beforeThe current frame and the N frames before it.
Up to N frames afterThe current frame and the N frames after it.
Before and afterThe current frame plus N frames before and M frames after.
Back to the first frameEvery frame from the first one up to the current frame.
On to the last frameEvery frame from the current frame to the last one.
All framesEvery frame in the timeline.

Set the numbers of frames before and after in the Before and After boxes below the range (1–999); the range stops at the ends of the timeline. Below them, the frames affected from the current frame are shown, such as "Applies to frames 1–3 (3)", in orange when more than one frame is affected.

The change to the current frame and to the other frames is a single undo step, and the status bar shows how many other frames were changed. Each tool applies to the other frames like this:

ToolOn the other frames
Pencil, eraser (100% strength), dither, shape, gradient pencilPaints the same color on the same pixels. Empty cels get drawn on too.
Lighten/darken, eraser with partial strengthChanges that frame's pixels by the same amount.
Shape PenComposites the same shapes over that frame's drawing.
Replace color brushChanges only pixels that have the color first pressed on (taken from the current frame).
FillFills the same spot again based on that frame's drawing. With the "all visible layers" reference, it looks at that frame's visible image.
GradientPaints again with the same start and end points.

Linked cels are changed only once, and locked layers are left alone. Drawing outside the canvas on normal layers is changed at the same spot too; for parts, the same spot means the same coordinates within the part. The canvas only shows the current frame.

The before and after counts are remembered, but the range always starts at Current frame only when the app launches, so you never edit several frames without meaning to.

Pencil

The pencil (B) is the basic drawing tool: it paints pixels in the brush shape along the path you drag. Painted pixels are replaced with the chosen color as-is, never blended. The mouse button decides whether the primary or secondary color is used, and by default the path is joined only sideways and up/down so every pixel touches the next by an edge (pixel steps). At size 1, turning on pixel perfect draws clean one-pixel lines joined diagonally.

Primary and secondary colors

  • Drag with the left button to paint in the primary color.
  • Drag with the right button to paint in the secondary color. With Right button = eraser on in the tool options, the right button erases instead, following the eraser's strength and channel settings. Swap the two colors with X or the swap button in the palette.
  • Pressing the other button mid-stroke cancels that stroke. Alt+click temporarily switches to the eyedropper to pick a color from the canvas.

Straight lines (Shift+click)

While you hold Shift, the line from the end of the last stroke to the mouse pointer is previewed translucently (including brush size and symmetry). Click to paint that line; where you release becomes the start of the next Shift+click. Keep the button down and drag to continue drawing freely from the end of the line.

Pixel perfect

Turn on Pixel perfect (P, the checkbox in the tool options, or Drawing options in the Tools menu) and strokes are joined diagonally; at brush size 1 the doubled L-shaped pixels at stair corners are removed as you draw, leaving a one-pixel line. Pixels painted earlier in the same stroke are never removed, so going back over your path leaves no holes. At brush size 2 or more no pixels are removed. The gradient pencil and the eraser use the same setting.

With it off, every turn of the staircase gets one extra pixel in an L shape, so the line looks thicker in places. With it on, those pixels are removed and the line steps evenly along the diagonals.

Shift+click lines are drawn without doubled pixels to begin with, so pixel perfect does not change them. The corner where the previous stroke meets the line belongs to two different strokes and is not removed.

Pixel steps

  • ① Pixel perfect takes priority over pixel steps. When it is on, the Pixel steps row is dimmed and strokes are joined diagonally. The pixel steps choice is kept and used again when you turn pixel perfect off.
  • ② Pixel steps sets how the pixels of a dragged path and of Shift+click lines are joined. Orthogonal (default) joins them only sideways and up/down, so every pixel touches the next by an edge, with an L-shaped corner at each step. Diagonal is the thinnest line, as before, with steps that touch only at corners. The pencil, gradient pencil and eraser share this setting, it is kept for the next launch, and it is separate from the Shape tool's pixel steps.

When you drag quickly, the received points are joined with lines. Diagonal steps touch only at their corners, giving the thinnest line; Orthogonal adds one pixel at every turn so all pixels connect by their edges.

Brush

Set the Square / Circle / Diamond shape and the Size (1–32) in the Brush group. [ / ] change the size by 1. The pixels of each shape, the eraser's separate brush and symmetry drawing are in Brush settings.

Gradient Pencil

The Gradient Pencil (Shift+B) draws like the pencil but takes each painted pixel's color from a gradient, spread once from the start of the stroke to its end or repeated at a set interval. It lives in the pencil group slot, so you can also long-press or right-click that slot to pick it.

  • Shift+click lines, the brush shape and size, pixel perfect, pixel steps, symmetry drawing and the selection work as with the pencil (pixel perfect and pixel steps are the same settings as the pencil's). With Right button = eraser on the right button erases; otherwise it paints the gradient like the left button.
  • Colors painted with the gradient pencil are not added to the recent colors.

Gradient settings

  • ① Mode: Whole stroke (default) spreads the gradient once from the start of the stroke to its end; Repeat starts again from the first color every interval.
  • ② Interval (2–512px, default 16): the stroke length of one pass through the gradient in Repeat mode. It appears only when Repeat is selected.
  • ③ Presets: pick one of your gradient presets or a gradient from an enabled package, or save the current gradient. Default resets to a black → white gradient.
  • ④ The stop bar, position, color, interpolation, color space and dithering are edited with the same editor as the gradient tool (the down-arrow button at the right end of the bar, or right-clicking the bar, also opens the preset menu). The settings are kept separately from the gradient tool and survive restarts.

How colors are assigned

The default mode, Whole stroke, re-spreads the gradient over the length drawn so far while you draw and fixes start → end colors when you release. Choose Repeat to repeat the gradient at a set interval.

Length is measured along the path the mouse took, not as the straight distance between start and end. Opacity stops replace pixels with that value as the pencil does, without blending, so a fully transparent part erases what is below.

Shape Pen

The Shape Pen stamps small pictures (shapes) from the chosen set. Use it when you need many slightly different copies of the same kind of thing, such as leaves, grass, flowers, sparkles or pebbles. It is the third tool in the pencil group slot: pick it from the list you get by long-pressing or right-clicking the slot, or from the Tools menu (no default shortcut).
