  • Click to stamp the next shape centered where you pressed. Shapes keep their own colors and opacity: transparent pixels leave what is below untouched and semi-transparent pixels blend with it. Even-sized shapes extend one pixel further right and down from the center, like the brush.
  • Drag to keep stamping at each spacing (or with optimized spacing). One stroke is one undo step. The shape that will be stamped next is shown semi-transparent with an outline under the cursor.
  • Symmetry drawing (mirrored positions get a flipped shape), tile mode, the selection, locked layers, linked cels and drawing outside the canvas work as with the pencil. Alt+click picks a color.

Shape pen settings

In the Shape pen group of Tool options you choose the set to stamp and how it is stamped.

ItemWhat it does
① SetChooses the set to stamp. Each row of the list shows the set's first shape and its name.
② Set buttonsFrom the left: new set, rename, delete set (red button, asks first — cannot be undone), restore built-in sets, Export set… and Import sets….
③ Shape tilesThe set's shape tiles. The highlighted tile is the shape stamped next, and the highlight moves on with each stamp. Click a tile to stamp that shape next. Right-click a tile, or press the red trash button, to delete the shape (built-in sets' shapes can be deleted too).
④ Add shapeAdds shapes from the selection, the current cel or PNG files (see “Adding shapes” below).
⑤ OrderHow the next shape is chosen (table below). Remembered per set; dimmed when the set has only one shape.
⑥ SpacingThe distance between stamps while dragging (px, 1 or more, default 1). At 1 every pixel passed over gets a stamp, giving an overlapping continuous trail. Dimmed while optimized spacing is on.
⑦ Optimized spacingWhen on (the default), each next shape is stamped at the first position where it no longer overlaps the previous stamp, so stamps follow the drag direction touching but not overlapping.
⑧ Tint with current colorWhen on, keeps the shape's shading and opacity but changes its color to the primary color (the secondary color with the right button). Off by default.

Built-in sets

Twenty built-in sets come with the app, each with 6–10 shapes. Autumn leaves mix orange, red and yellow, flowers pink, blue and white, stars gold and white, and hearts red and pink; sparkles, bubbles, puffs, raindrops, wood grain, cracks and petals contain semi-transparent pixels. Only the dots set uses the In order order; the others are Random.

Built-in sets can also have shapes deleted, be renamed or be deleted entirely. The restore built-in sets button asks first, then puts deleted built-in sets back and resets the built-in sets to their original shapes, names and order (your own sets stay as they are). Built-in sets added in a newer version appear automatically in an older sets file, while built-in sets you deleted stay deleted until you restore them.

Adding shapes

The menu of the Add shape button adds shapes to the chosen set (if there is no set at all, a new one is created). The shape you added becomes the next shape to stamp.

  • From selection: adds the selected pixels of the current cel as a shape (only when there is a selection).
  • From current cel: adds the whole current cel as a shape.
  • From PNG files…: pick one or more PNG files and add them all at once.

Either way the transparent border is trimmed and opacity is kept. Images with only transparent pixels, or with a side longer than 256px, are not added and the status bar says why. Create a new set (the + button) to collect shapes you drew yourself.

Order

Order is remembered separately for each set.

OrderWhat it does
RandomPicks any shape from the set for each stamp. The same shape never comes twice in a row.
In orderStamps the set's shapes in order, going back to the first after the last.
SelectedAlways stamps only the chosen (highlighted) shape tile.

Spacing and optimized spacing

Spacing 1 stamps at every pixel passed over, so the shapes overlap densely into a continuous trail like a bush or fur. Optimized spacing measures overlap with the shape's opaque pixels (the transparent border does not count, and a different next shape is measured as itself) and stamps at the first position with no overlap, so each shape stays visible and touches the next even when sizes differ. To set the distance yourself, turn optimized spacing off and raise the spacing (px).

Tinting and the right button

With Tint with current color on, the most common color in the shape becomes the primary color and the other colors keep their saturation and brightness ratios to it, so the shading (outlines and highlights) stays. Use it to stamp autumn leaves or a different green from the green leaves set. The right button tints with the secondary color. When off, both buttons stamp the shape's own colors.

With Right button = eraser on, the right button erases in the shape, by each shape pixel's opacity.

Exporting and importing sets

  • Export set…: saves the chosen set as a single .chickxel-shapes file. The set's name is suggested as the file name, and built-in sets can be exported too.
  • Import sets…: pick one or more files; each becomes a new set added to the end of the list, and the last one is selected. Existing sets are never overwritten, and a name already in use gets (2), (3)… appended. A plain PNG can be picked too: it becomes a set holding that one image as a shape (transparent border trimmed, named after the file). The status bar shows each imported set's name and shape count, and files that could not be read are listed together in one message.
  • Dropping a .chickxel-shapes file onto the main window (where the canvas does not take it) also switches to the shape pen and imports the set.

A set file holds the set's name, order and shapes (PNG, transparency kept), so you can share it with others or move it to another computer.

Where sets are stored

Sets are saved on every change in a single file, <settings folder>/Chickxel/ShapePen/shape-sets.json (each set's name, order and shapes); the chosen set, spacing, optimized spacing and tinting are kept in the settings. If the file is damaged, it is moved to shape-sets.json.bad and the built-in sets are used.

Eraser

The Eraser (E) erases the pixels along your drag to transparent, in the brush shape. Both the left and right buttons erase. A lower strength fades pixels gradually, and choosing channels removes only certain colors.

  • While you hold Shift, the line from the end of the last stroke to the mouse pointer is previewed translucently; click to erase along it (same as straight lines with the pencil).
  • The eraser remembers its own brush shape and size, separate from the pencil (Brush settings). Pixel perfect and Pixel steps in the tool options are the same settings as the pencil's, so by default it erases along a path joined sideways and up/down, and with pixel perfect on it joins diagonally (removing doubled pixels at size 1). The selection and symmetry drawing work as with the pencil.

Erase strength and channels

  • Strength 1–100% (default 100% = erase completely): lower values reduce alpha by that ratio per stroke. Within one stroke each pixel is reduced once; release and rub again to reduce it further until it becomes transparent.
  • Channels R, G, B: with all on it erases to transparent; with some on, alpha stays and only the chosen color channels are reduced by the strength. With none on, nothing is erased.

One pass at 50% halves the alpha, and releasing and rubbing again halves what is left (the checkerboard shows through). With only R on nothing becomes transparent; only the red component is removed, so the red band turns almost black, the white band turns cyan, and the green and blue bands change only slightly.

An example with only R on. The red area loses its R and turns black, the green area has no R and stays the same, and the blue area loses only the bit of red mixed into it, so its color shifts slightly. No pixel becomes transparent.

Erasing with other tools' right button

Drawing with the right button in the pencil or gradient pencil with Right button = eraser on follows these strength and channel settings. Right-button erasing with the shape and fill tools always erases completely, whatever the settings.

Shape

The Shape tool (R) draws 11 shapes by dragging, from lines and curves to stars and cubes. The shape spans from where you press to where you release, and the result is previewed while you drag. The left button draws with the primary color and the right button with the secondary color (or erases with Right button = eraser on); line width, pen shape and symmetry work as in Brush settings.

Shape settings

  • ① Pick the shape to draw with the shape buttons. L switches straight to the Shape tool's line.
  • ② The line under the shape buttons explains how to draw the chosen shape and what Shift does.
  • ③ Set Corners (3–32) for the Regular polygon, Points (3–32) for the Star and Turns (1–10) for the Spiral.
  • ④ Turn on Fill (U) to paint the inside too; the shape button icons turn filled as well. Lines, curves and spirals can't be filled.

How to draw each shape

ShapeHow to draw
LineFrom where you press to where you release. Shift = 15° steps
CurveDrag to set both ends, release, then move the mouse to bend it (the middle of the curve passes through the mouse; it's only a preview until confirmed). Click the same button or press Enter to confirm; the other button, Esc or Ctrl+Z cancels. Shift = 15° steps while dragging the ends, and a circular arc through both ends and the mouse while bending
RectangleThe dragged box. Shift = square
EllipseAn ellipse that fits the dragged box. Shift = circle
Equilateral triangleThe dragged line becomes one side (drag right to point up, left to point down). Shift = 30° steps
Isosceles triangleFits the dragged box. Drag down for the tip on top, up for the tip at the bottom. Shift = equilateral proportions
Regular polygonWith the set number of corners (5 = pentagon, 6 = hexagon), fitted to the dragged box. Shift = keep it regular
StarWith the set number of points, fitted to the dragged box. Shift = keep it regular
SpiralA spiral that winds outward from the center to fill the dragged box; drag left to wind the other way. Never filled. Shift = square
CylinderTop ellipse, both sides and the front half of the bottom. Shift = square
CubeA cube with three faces in 2:1 isometric lines. Shift = square

Pixel steps (lines and curves)

  • ⑤ Lines and curves have Pixel steps: Diagonal (default) also joins pixels diagonally for the thinnest line, while Orthogonal steps only sideways or up/down so every pixel touches the next by an edge.

Diagonal joins pixels that touch only at a corner, giving the thinnest line; Orthogonal adds a pixel at every turn so each pixel touches the next by an edge. A fill with Connect diagonally too (8 directions) on leaks through the corner gaps of a diagonal line, so draw outlines you will fill that way with Orthogonal.

Text

The Text tool (T, the T slot on the toolbar) types text right on the canvas. When you finish, a new layer named after the first line is created just above the current layer (or its group for a part), with one Text effect. One edit is one undo step, and empty text creates no layer.

Creating a text box and typing

  • Drag = ① text box: lines wrap by word within the box width, and the letter size becomes the size at which one line fits the box height (the Size field in the tool settings changes to match). Click = a single line whose width fits automatically (size from the tool settings). Clicking existing text puts the cursor there to edit it (a locked layer only shows a notice).
  • Letters appear as pixels as you type, including Korean syllables being composed at the cursor. Use the arrow keys and Home/End (Shift = select, Ctrl/⌥ = by word), Ctrl/⌘+A, C, X, V, double-click = word and triple-click = everything. Enter = new line; Esc, Ctrl/⌘+Enter or clicking outside = finish.
  • The eight handles on the dashed border resize the box (auto-width text becomes a text box). Dragging the top or bottom edge (corners included) also changes the letter size so all the text fits the new height; the left and right edges change only the width. Drag the border to move it.

Text

Changes in the Text group of the tool settings apply to the text being edited right away.

  • ② Font: the pixel fonts built into the app and fonts you added.
  • ③ Add Font… copies a .ttf, .otf, .ttc or .bdf file into the Fonts folder in the app settings folder and adds it to the list. Open Font Folder opens that folder.
  • ④ Size (0 = the font's own size): pixel fonts work at any size too (scaled cell by cell so the line height becomes that size; strokes may drop out below the original size). The buttons next to it are bold and italic.
  • ⑤ Alignment: left, center, right.
  • ⑥ Letter spacing and line spacing (px).
  • ⑦ Width and height scale 25–400%, and Smooth (vector fonts only — semi-transparent edges).

Fonts

⑧ The font list starts with the built-in fonts: Galmuri 7, 9, 11, 11 Bold, 11 Condensed and 14; Galmuri Mono 7, 9 and 11; NeoDunggeunmo, NeoDunggeunmo Code and NeoDunggeunmo Pro (pixel-style vector fonts — 16px at size 0, sharpest at multiples of 16); Unifont (16px, almost every Unicode character); Cozette (13px Latin and symbols); Spleen 5×8, 6×12 and 8×16 (Latin). All are under free licenses (SIL OFL 1.1, MIT, BSD), so you can use them freely in your art and games; the license texts are in the app folder's Licenses/.

Only the font name is saved in the project. If that font isn't on this computer, a warning appears under the font field and Galmuri11 is used. Characters missing from a font are also drawn with Galmuri11 (for example Korean in Cozette or Spleen).

Text layers

Text is added as a layer effect, not as pixels. Font, size, position, box size and settings such as typing in letter by letter can also be changed in the Text effect in the Effects window, and dragging with the Move tool (no selection, current cel empty on a text layer) moves the text. To turn it into pixels, use Merge down or Bake in the Effects window.

Fill

The fill tool (G) paints the area of the same (or a similar) color as the clicked pixel in one go. Left-click fills with the primary color and right-click with the secondary color; with Right button = eraser on, right-click erases the area completely. The tolerance, contiguous and source settings decide how far the fill spreads.

Fill settings

  • ① Tolerance 0–100% (default 0): 0 fills only the exact same color, higher values include similar colors, and 100% fills every color. Colors are compared with the clicked pixel; ② Compare by decides how the difference is measured.
  • ② Compare by (default Max channel difference (default)): chooses which color difference the tolerance measures. The tolerance percentage is applied to each method's largest possible difference, so with any method 0% means only the exact same color and 100% means every color. Transparent pixels (alpha 0) compare alpha only; when both colors are visible, the alpha difference must also be within the tolerance. The choice is remembered and the magic wand uses it too.
MethodWhat counts as the same color
Max channel difference (default)The largest R, G, B or alpha difference is within the tolerance (100% = 255). This is the original method.
RGB distanceThe straight-line distance of the R, G and B differences is within the tolerance (100% ≈ 441, 255√3). A color that differs a little in several channels counts as closer than one that differs a lot in a single channel.
OKLab (perceptual)OKLab distance, close to the difference the eye sees (black to white = 100%). Small differences between dark colors count more, differences between light colors less.
CIEDE2000 (perceptual, precise)The color difference ΔE00, a more precise measure of visible difference (100% = 120). It is slightly slower, but each color is measured only once, not each pixel.
Hue onlyOnly the hue angle difference, ignoring lightness and saturation (100% = 180 degrees). Neutral colors such as gray, white and black count as the same hue as each other, and a neutral and a colored pixel count as farthest apart. Use it to pick all the light and dark shades of one color at once.
  • ③ Contiguous only (on by default): when on, only the area connected to the clicked pixel is filled; when off, every matching pixel is filled even if separate. Toggle it with Shift+G.
  • ④ Connect diagonally too (8 directions): off connects only up, down, left and right; on also connects pixels that touch diagonally.
  • ⑤ Anchor: Current layer (default) finds the area from the current layer's pixels, and All visible layers from the composite you see. Either way, the paint goes on the current layer.
  • ⑥ The eyedropper has the same source setting (its default is all visible layers).

How the settings change the result

With Contiguous only off, the same yellow in the other boxes is painted too, even though outlines separate them. Use it to change one color across the whole picture at once.

10% means a difference of up to 26 per channel, so the bands 16 away from the clicked one are filled and the outer bands 32 away are left. Colors are compared with the clicked pixel, so the fill stops once colors drift too far from it, even if neighbors are similar.

With the same tolerance, each method counts different cells as the same color. Max channel difference fills only the nearby red shades and one dark orange cell, and RGB distance fills one more orange cell. OKLab and CIEDE2000 also fill gray cells of similar lightness, and CIEDE2000 fills the darkest red as well. Hue only fills the whole red row and the orange row, whose hue is close, regardless of lightness, and leaves the grays.

Other behavior

  • With a selection, only its inside is filled. With symmetry drawing on, the fill also runs at the mirrored positions with the same settings. In tile mode, clicking outside the canvas fills the matching spot on the other side.
  • On a normal layer you can also fill outside the canvas. The fill only paints on the side you click: clicking outside the canvas spreads within the rectangle around the pixels outside the canvas, treating canvas pixels as walls, and empty space with no pixels is not filled. With a selection or in tile mode, only the canvas is filled (Drawing outside the canvas).
  • If the clicked pixel already has the paint color and the tolerance is 0 (current-layer source), nothing changes and no undo step is recorded.

Gradient

The gradient tool (F) paints a linear gradient in the direction you drag on the canvas. Colors and opacity are set with stops, and you choose whether to blend smoothly, split into bands or mix with a pattern (dithering). Save gradients you use often as presets.

Dragging and settings

Drag on the canvas from the ① start point to the ② end point to paint in that direction (Shift = 45° steps). With a selection it paints only inside it; otherwise the whole cel.

  • ③ Gradient bar: opacity stops sit above the bar and color stops below. Clicking above the bar's middle line (the top row or the upper half of the bar) adds an opacity stop, and clicking below it (the lower half of the bar or the bottom row) adds a color stop. Clicking where a stop sits, even on the bar, selects that stop instead of adding one. Drag a stop to move it and press Delete to remove it.
  • You can edit the selected stop's ④ position % and ⑤ color (or opacity %).
  • ⑥ Interpolation: smooth / steps. ⑦ Color space: the space smooth blending runs in, RGB / HSL / OKLab / OKLCH. New gradients default to OKLCH; older settings and projects without a color space load as RGB. It applies to both the preview and the painting; steps and Bayer dithering still use only the stop colors. ⑧ Dithering: none / 2×2 Bayer / 4×4 Bayer (for pixel art that mixes with patterns instead of semi-transparency).
  • Settings are kept for the next launch. Usage hints such as how to drag are in the ⑨ i icon next to the Tool settings title; hover it to read them. The right button does not paint.

Smooth creates many new colors between two stops, while Steps uses only the nearest stop color and splits the bar into bands. Dithering mixes just the two neighboring stop colors in a pattern to show the blend ratio: 2×2 gives a coarse pattern with four levels, 4×4 a fine one with sixteen. Use it to show a gradient without adding colors to the palette.

Presets

  • Click the thin down-arrow button at the right end of the bar for the preset menu; right-clicking the bar opens the same menu. ⑩ Save current gradient… saves the current settings under a name, and picking one of the ⑪ saved presets (a gradient preview under each name; favorites come first) applies it right away. A preset stores the color and opacity stops, interpolation, color space and dithering; picked in a layer effect's color gradient editor it also applies the shape, angle, direction, repeat and animation.
  • The built-in presets are Rainbow, Repeating Rainbow (red at both ends), Sunset, Ocean, Transparent Fade, Fire, Forest and Grayscale. Deleted or edited built-ins can be restored on the Presets tab of Preferences.
  • ⑫ Manage Gradient Presets… opens the Gradients tab of the Presets window. ⑬ Pick a preset from the list and ⑭ add it to favorites, rename or delete it, or ⑮ edit its stops and settings on the right while watching the preview, then press ⑯ Save changes.
  • ⑰ Asset Manager: opens the Asset Manager filtered to gradients.

Received gradients (from the store or made by someone else) show the store name (or the author) next to their name in the list, and Rename and Delete are off. Edit one and click Save to My Assets to save a copy to My Assets while the original stays as is (and keeps getting updates). Gradients you made are edited in place.

Eyedropper

The Eyedropper (I) picks a color from the canvas and sets it as the primary or secondary color right away. Use it to reuse a color already in the picture without opening the palette or the color picker.

Picking colors

  • Left-click picks into the primary color and right-click into the secondary color. Hold the button and drag to keep picking the color under the pointer; the color where you release stays.
  • Source: All visible layers (default) picks the composite color you see, and Current layer picks only the current layer's pixel. While a selection is floating, its pixels are picked first. This works like the fill tool's source (Fill).
  • Clicking outside the canvas picks the color of a normal layer's pixels outside the canvas, and picks nothing where there are none.

Temporary eyedropper with Alt

While using a drawing tool, hold Alt and the pointer over the canvas turns into an eyedropper; click to pick a color without switching tools. Left-click sets the primary color and right-click the secondary color, using the eyedropper tool's Source setting. Releasing Alt returns to the original tool. An Alt used this way, or during a drag, doesn't open the menu bar when released, so the next Enter goes to the canvas (applying a transform and so on), not to the menu.

  • Tools that support Alt+click: Pencil, Gradient Pencil, Eraser, Fill, Gradient, Shape, Dodge/burn, Dithering and the Replace color brush.
  • In the text, selection, move, pivot and 9-slice tools, holding Alt does not switch to the eyedropper.

Dodge/burn

The Dodge/burn tool (D) rubs over pixels already painted to make them a little lighter or darker. Use it to shade from the colors that are already there without picking new ones. The default mode, Hue shift, also turns the hue toward yellow as it lightens and toward blue/purple as it darkens, for pixel-art style shading.

  • Left button = lighten, right button = darken (in Saturation mode, left = more vivid, right = duller).
  • Each pixel changes only once per stroke. Rubbing the same spot again in that stroke does nothing more; release and rub again to change it further.
  • Transparent pixels stay as they are, and semi-transparent pixels keep their alpha while only their color changes. The brush shape and size, symmetry drawing, the selection and Shift+click lines work as with the pencil.

Lighten/darken settings

  • ① Mode: how colors change (default Hue shift). The table and the comparison below show each mode.
  • ② Strength 1–50% (default 10%): how much one pass changes a pixel. Keep it low and rub several times for an even build-up, or raise it for a big change at once.

Modes

ModeLeft buttonRight button
Hue shiftMoves lightness toward white by the strength and turns the hue a little toward (golden) yellowLowers lightness by the strength and turns the hue a little toward blue/purple. Near-gray colors only change lightness
Lightness onlyRaises only the perceived lightness, keeping hue and saturationLowers only the perceived lightness, keeping hue and saturation
HSV valueAdds HSV value; at full brightness it lowers saturation toward white (the old behavior)Subtracts HSV value
Mix white/blackMixes with white by the strengthMixes with black by the strength
SaturationRaises saturation for a more vivid color, keeping lightness and hueLowers saturation for a duller color, keeping lightness and hue. Grays do not change

Hue shift turns the hue toward yellow as it lightens and toward blue/purple as it darkens, giving livelier shading than Lightness only or Mix white/black, which only make the same color stronger or paler. HSV value raises value while keeping saturation, so highlights approach a loud pure hue, and Mix white/black makes highlights pastel and chalky. Saturation changes only how vivid a color is, keeping its lightness; the gray row stays the same.

Undo names

One stroke is one undo step, shown in the history as Lighten or Darken, or Saturate or Desaturate in Saturation mode. The mode and strength are kept for the next launch.

Dithering

The Dithering tool paints the pixels under the brush alternately with the primary and secondary colors following a pattern. Use it to hand-paint dithering, the pixel-art technique of mixing two colors in a checker-like dot pattern so they read as an in-between color from a distance. Pick it from its toolbar slot; it has no default shortcut.

  • The left button paints the primary color on the pattern pixels and the secondary color on the rest. The right button swaps where the two colors go.
  • The pattern is a 2×2 ordered dither fixed to canvas coordinates, so overlapping or separate strokes always line up into one continuous pattern.
  • The brush shape and size, symmetry drawing, the selection and Shift+click lines work as with the pencil. Both colors are painted as-is without blending, and the undo history shows Dithering.

Patterns

  • ① Pattern: the share of the primary color. Checker (50%) (default) uses every other pixel, Sparse (25%) one pixel in four and Dense (75%) three pixels in four. The pattern is kept for the next launch.

Painting the three patterns side by side gives a hand-made four-step transition from the secondary to the primary color. For a dithered gradient painted in one drag, use the dithering setting of the gradient tool.

Where Chickxel uses dithering

Dithering shows an in-between color through the ratio of dots instead of mixing colors, so it does not add colors to the palette. Besides this tool, the following features offer the same method (Bayer ordered dithering):

FeatureDithering setting
DitheringPaints three 2×2 patterns with the brush
Gradient · Gradient PencilDithering: None / 2×2 Bayer / 4×4 Bayer — mixes only the two neighboring stop colors in a pattern
Convert to palette colorsNo dithering / Ordered dithering (Bayer 4×4) — mixes in a pattern while converting the picture to palette colors
Pixelate AI imageDither in the palette step: None / Bayer 2×2 / Bayer 4×4

Replace color brush

The Replace color brush (C) repaints, where you rub, only the pixels of the same color as the pixel where the stroke started. Other colors such as outlines or highlights stay even when the brush passes over them, so use it to recolor a single color or paint only over that color.

How it decides

  • The color to replace is the color of the pixel where you pressed the button. It does not change even if the stroke passes over other colors.
  • Only exactly the same color is replaced; there is no tolerance. To include similar colors, use the tolerance of the fill tool or the magic wand.
  • The left button replaces with the primary color and the right button with the secondary color.
  • Starting on a transparent pixel paints only transparent pixels, so you can fill empty space behind the drawing without touching it.

What it shares with other brush tools

The brush shape and size, symmetry drawing, the selection, Shift+click lines and the Alt+click eyedropper work as with the pencil. One stroke is one undo step, shown in the history as Color replace brush.

Compared with Replace color in the Edit menu

Replace color… in the Edit menu (Ctrl+Alt+R) changes one color across the whole current cel (or the selection) at once. This brush changes only where you rub, so use it to change just part of a color (see the Edit menu commands in Drawing basics).

Selection tools

The four selection tools all create the same kind of selection. While a selection exists, cut, copy, delete, flip, rotate, transform and add outline apply only inside it, and drawing with the pencil, fill, shapes and so on is limited to it as well. The four tools share the select slot of the toolbar; pressing M repeatedly cycles rectangle → ellipse → lasso → magic wand.

ToolShortcutHow it selects
Rectangle selectNoneDrag to select a rectangle.
Ellipse selectNoneDrag to select the circle (ellipse) that fits the dragged rectangle.
Lasso selectQDraw an outline; on release the closed shape is selected.
Magic wandWSelects the area of similar color around the clicked pixel.

Rectangle select and ellipse select

  • ① While you drag on the canvas, a dotted border follows the area to select. Hold Shift while dragging for a square (a circle with ellipse select).
  • Clicking without dragging deselects.
  • A new drag replaces the previous selection. There are no modifier keys for adding to or subtracting from a selection.
  • Dragging inside the selection (the cursor becomes a four-way arrow) lifts the selected pixels and moves them. See Working with a selection below for details.

Lasso select

  • ② Hold the mouse button and draw an outline; the area to be selected is shown with a blue dotted line. On release the last point joins the first, and the closed shape is selected. Even fast drags leave no gaps between points.
  • Clicking without dragging deselects; dragging inside the selection moves its pixels.

Magic wand

  • ③ Selects the area of similar color around the clicked pixel in one click. The status bar shows the number of selected pixels and the size of the area.
  • ④ The settings are shared with Fill: Tolerance, Compare by (which color difference the tolerance measures), Contiguous only (when off, matching colors anywhere on the canvas), Connect diagonally too and Source (current layer / all visible layers). A value changed in one tool is used by the other as well.
  • Dragging inside a selection does not move it with the magic wand. To move the pixels, use the Move tool (V) or the nudge keys (Ctrl+Shift+Left and so on).

Working with a selection

ActionResult
Drag inside the selection (rectangle, ellipse, lasso), or drag with the Move toolLifts the selected pixels and moves them. Until it is dropped, this is called a floating selection. Pixels on a locked layer can't be moved.
Ctrl+Shift+Left Ctrl+Shift+Right Up Down / Shift+Left Shift+Right Shift+Up Shift+DownMoves the selected pixels by 1px / 8px while a selection tool or the Move tool is active. Plain ←→ go to the previous or next frame and don't move a selection (except with the Move tool while the canvas has focus).
EnterDrops the floating selection where it is.
Right-click the canvas with a selection tool, Ctrl+D, EscDrops any floating pixels and removes the selection.
Ctrl+ASelects the whole canvas (the whole part area when a part is selected). If neither a selection tool nor the Move tool is active, it switches to rectangle select.

Commands that use the selection

CommandWith a selectionWithout a selection
Cut Ctrl+XMoves the selected pixels to the clipboard.Does nothing (a status bar message explains).
Copy Ctrl+CCopies the selected pixels.Copies the part cel for a part; otherwise the whole composited image of the current frame.
Paste Ctrl+VPastes as a floating selection at the top left of the selection.Pixels copied or cut in the app paste as a floating selection at the spot they were copied from (same position in the frame), even on another frame or a part of a different size; anything outside the canvas is clipped or kept as off-canvas pixels when dropped. A frame image copy pastes at (0,0), and images copied from other programs paste at the top left of the visible canvas area. Copying on the canvas clears cels, frames or effects copied earlier in the timeline, so pasting after clicking the timeline still pastes these pixels.
Delete DeleteClears the selected pixels (transparent), even after clicking a timeline frame or cel: the selection is cleared instead of the frame, layer or cel.Does nothing on the canvas. After clicking the timeline, deletes the frames, layers or cels selected there.
Flip horizontal (selection/cel) Shift+H · Flip vertical (selection/cel) Shift+VFlips the selected pixels.Flips the current cel.
Rotate 90° (selection/canvas) Ctrl+R · Rotate -90° (selection/canvas) Ctrl+Shift+RRotates the selected pixels by 90°.Rotates the whole canvas (all frames).
Transform (rotate/size)… Ctrl+TOpens the selection in transform mode.Opens all painted pixels of the current cel.
Add outline (primary color) Ctrl+Alt+OAdds a 1px outline in the primary color around the drawing inside the selection.Adds it across the whole current cel.
Drawing tools (pencil, fill, shapes, gradient and so on)Paint only inside the selection.Paint anywhere on the canvas.

Pasting switches to rectangle select unless the rectangle, ellipse or lasso tool is active. With a selection, the Replace color… window offers Selection only.

Move tool and pixels outside the canvas

The Move tool (V) moves the selected pixels when there is a selection, and the whole current cel otherwise. On normal layers, pixels pushed past the canvas edge are not deleted; they are kept as pixels outside the canvas and come back when you move them in again.

Moving

ActionResult
Drag on the canvasWith a selection, dragging anywhere moves the selected pixels; without one, it moves the whole current cel, and on a normal layer the layer pivot moves along with the drawing. On a text layer it moves the text (Text). Locked layers are not moved.
Right-drag without a selectionMoves only the drawing and leaves the layer pivot where it is. The temporary Move tool you get by holding Ctrl in another tool works the same way.
Arrow keys / Shift+arrow keysMoves by 1px / 8px. When shifting the whole cel, each key press is one undo step.
Hold Ctrl (⌘ on macOS) and drag while using another toolThe Move tool is active only while the key is held; releasing it returns to the previous tool. This doesn't happen in the group view, in transform or canvas size mode, or while typing text.
Select several cels in the timeline, then drag or use the arrow keysWithout a selection, every selected cel moves by the same amount (one undo step), and the status bar shows how many other cels moved. Groups, empty cels and locked layers are skipped. Transform mode (Ctrl+T) changes only the current cel, so use the Move tool to shift several cels together.

Cycle

Cycle in the Move tool settings (menu Tools → Drawing options → Move: wrap around) is off by default. Turn it on to have pixels that leave one side of the cel come back on the other side, which helps when lining up seamless patterns (tiles).

With wrap on, pixels outside the canvas are not used, and a part wraps within its own part area. Moving a selection never wraps.

Pixels outside the canvas

Cels of normal layers keep the pixels that leave the canvas. The parts outside are kept when you drag or push layer contents off the canvas with the Move tool, drop a selection outside the canvas, paste and drop an image larger than the canvas, or place an image (dropping it on the canvas, or Apply in the pixelate window) that is larger than the canvas or sticks out of it. Whether you apply the transform mode that follows placing with Enter or keep the image as placed with Esc, the part outside stays as pixels outside the canvas. Up to 4096px around the canvas is kept; pixels farther out are discarded. Parts use only their own part area.

With the Move tool selected, the current layer's pixels outside the canvas are shown faded (40%) around the canvas. While dragging layer contents the whole moved cel is shown, and for a floating selection the part outside the canvas is shown faded. Compositing, the preview, exports and effects use only what is inside the canvas.

Duplicating a layer or frame copies pixels outside the canvas too, and merging down also merges the upper layer's pixels outside the canvas. Shrinking the canvas size keeps the cut parts as pixels outside the canvas, so they come back when you enlarge it again, and the outside part of a placed image also appears when you enlarge the canvas. These pixels are saved in the project file and the undo history (older versions of Chickxel don't read them).

Drawing outside the canvas

With a normal layer selected, while you use a drawing tool (Pencil, Gradient Pencil, Shape Pen, Eraser, Shape, Dodge/burn, Dithering, Replace color brush, Fill) or the Eyedropper, that cel's pixels outside the canvas are shown clearly at the layer's opacity (semi-transparent for a hidden layer), and you can paint, erase and pick colors on them directly. The brush cursor, symmetry ghost cursors, the Shift line preview and strokes all continue outside the canvas. Other layers' pixels outside the canvas are not shown, and export and the preview still use only the inside of the canvas.

  • Pixels painted outside the canvas are recorded in one undo step together with the part inside. Painting creates or enlarges the pixels outside the canvas, and erasing all of them removes them. Linked cels change too.
  • You can draw up to 4096 pixels around the canvas. Fill only fills the side you click (clicking outside the canvas fills within the rectangle around the pixels outside the canvas, with canvas pixels as walls), and the eyedropper (including Alt+click) picks colors from pixels outside the canvas. With a selection or in tile mode only the canvas (inside the selection) is painted, and the gradient tool always paints only inside the canvas. A part is drawn only within its own part area.

Transform mode (current cel)

Rotate, scale, move and distort the selection of the current cel (or, with none, all its painted pixels) with handles. Enter with Ctrl+T or Transform (rotate/size)… in the Edit menu; a blue dotted box with handles appears. Dragging repeatedly always redraws from the original, so the pixels don't get mushy, and you can go back to the original at any time before applying. To change every frame of a layer together, use Transform whole layer.

Controls

ActionResult
① Drag inside, arrow keysMove (1-pixel steps; Shift+arrow keys = 8 pixels)
② Drag one of the 8 square handlesResize (Shift = keep ratio, Alt = from center; dragging past the opposite side flips)
③ Drag just outside the borderRotate around the rotation center (Shift = 15° steps)
④ Drag / double-click the blue rotation centerMove the rotation center (half-pixel steps) / reset it to the middle of the box
Enter or ⑤ ApplyApply (one undo step)
Esc, Ctrl+Z or ⑥ CancelBack to before the transform

Pressing or releasing Ctrl, Alt or Shift while dragging a handle takes effect immediately.

Doing something else, such as switching tools or saving, applies it first. It doesn't start in the group view, on a locked layer or on an empty cel; the status bar explains why. Entering it during canvas size mode applies the canvas size first.

Entering numbers

⑦ In the Transform · current cel group of Tool options you can enter the angle (−180 to 180°, clockwise, around the rotation center), width/height % (−100% = flip) and center X/Y (the canvas position of the image's middle) as numbers. ⑧ Box width and height are the margin: they change only the box size and leave the drawing where it is (top left stays; same as right-dragging a handle). The ⑨ Flip buttons flip the box horizontally or vertically; the change happens when you apply. Near the bottom of the group the original and current sizes are shown (Original W×H → W×H px).

Resampling

⑩ Resampling is how the color of each rotated or scaled pixel is picked from the original. Neither method blends colors, so no colors that aren't in the original appear.

MethodHow it samples and the result
RotSpriteSamples from a copy enlarged three times with Scale2x (8×). Diagonal lines and corners break less and 1-pixel lines are less likely to split. If the original is larger than 512×512px, it samples with Nearest to save memory, and Tool options says so.
NearestTakes the original pixel under the center of each result pixel as is. It is the simplest method; rotated lines can break into steps or get thicker.

For 0°, 90°, 180° and 270° rotations and pure scaling without free distort, the two methods give the same result. Moving by whole pixels keeps the original exactly.

Free distort and right-click

ActionResult
⑪ Ctrl (⌘ on macOS)+drag a handleFree distort: a corner handle moves just that corner, and a side handle moves that whole side (both its corners) freely, making a trapezoid or any skewed quadrilateral. Add Alt and the opposite corner (side) moves the opposite way. Positions that would twist or dent the shape aren't accepted, and while Ctrl is held the cursor over a handle is an arrow. After a free distort, moving, resizing and rotating keep that shape.
Right-click inside without draggingOpens the ⑫ menu: Rotate 180°, Rotate 90° clockwise, Rotate 90° counterclockwise (around the rotation center), Flip horizontal and Flip vertical.
Right-drag insideMoves only the drawing. The rotation center, and the pivot that would otherwise follow the drawing when there is no selection, stay where they are (also after applying).
Right-drag just outside the borderRotates around the middle of the box (for a distorted box, the middle of its four corners). The rotation center doesn't stay put; it turns with the drawing (Shift = 15° steps).
Right-drag a handleMargin: leaves the drawing where it is and only widens or narrows the box. Narrowing crops what falls outside the box (previewed faded); widening extends a selection by repeating its edge pixels, or adds empty space when there is no selection. While dragging, the handles show as outward arrows in the same color; Shift = keep the corner ratio, Alt = from the center.
Ctrl+right-drag a handleMoves the corners of the margin box freely (a corner handle moves that corner, a side handle both ends; with Alt the opposite one moves the opposite way). The box border follows that shape and everything outside it is cropped (previewed faded); the box becomes the rectangle around it. The shape is kept through moving, resizing, rotating and free distort; dragging the margin without Ctrl or changing the ⑧ box size numbers returns to a rectangular margin.

Rotation center

④ The blue rotation center starts at the current layer's pivot (the part pivot for a part, otherwise that layer's pivot in the current frame). Dragging it snaps to half pixels, and double-clicking it moves it back to the middle of the box. Moving the rotation center doesn't change the pivot.

What changes

Transform mode changes only the current cel. Cels you selected in the timeline don't change with it; to shift several cels by the same amount use the Move tool, and to change every frame of a layer use Transform whole layer.

CaseWhat changes
With a selectionOnly the selected pixels change and the pivot stays. After applying, the selection keeps the transformed shape.
Without a selectionAll painted pixels change, and that frame's layer pivot follows the drawing (that frame gets its own pivot). Whatever you move by right-dragging inside leaves the pivot in place.
PartIf the drawing goes past the part area, the area grows (all frames and skins; the art stays in place). Only moving it, without a selection, moves that frame's part position instead of the drawing (Part position per frame). Rotating or distorting removes the 9-slice borders.

The preview is shown without layer effects, as in canvas size mode.

Canvas size mode

Set the canvas size of all frames and layers, and where the image sits in it, right on the canvas instead of in a dialog. Enter with Ctrl+Alt+C or Canvas size… in the Edit menu. The canvas border is a ① light-blue dotted line and ② pixels that end up outside the new canvas (including pixels already outside the canvas) are shown faded (40%), while the inside of the new canvas shows the result, including pixels outside the canvas that come back in.

Controls

ActionResult
③ Drag a handleStretch: moves that canvas edge and the image edge together, so the margin stays the same and the image is resized (nearest neighbor, or 9-slice when Stretch with 9-slice is on).
③ Right-drag a handleMargin: moves only that canvas edge (adds margin or crops the image). The image keeps its size; while dragging, the border turns yellow and the handles become outward arrows.
Shift / Alt while draggingKeep ratio (the image's when stretching, the canvas's for margin — with a side handle the other direction follows at the same ratio, centered) / from the center (the opposite edge moves by the same distance, as with Anchor at canvas center on)
Ctrl (⌘ on macOS)+drag a handleMoves the canvas corners freely (a corner handle moves that corner, a side handle both ends; with Alt the opposite one moves the opposite way), and the image is skewed in perspective to that shape (all layers, frames and skins; resampling follows the transform mode setting). The new canvas is the rectangle around the shape, and the space left over is transparent margin. Pivots and part positions follow, and 9-slice borders are removed.
Ctrl+right-drag a handleMoves the corners the same way, but the image stays in place and everything outside the shape is cropped, including pixels outside the canvas and parts.
④ Drag inside the image, arrow keysMoves the image within the canvas, including an image skewed in perspective (Shift+arrow keys = 8 pixels).
Enter or ⑤ OKApply (one undo step)
Esc, Ctrl+Z or ⑥ CancelExit without changes
⑮ Flip horizontal / vertical buttonsFlips the whole canvas at once (all layers and frames, including pixels outside the canvas and pivots). Same as Flip entire canvas horizontally (all frames) / Flip entire canvas vertically (all frames) in the Edit menu; it is one undo step, the mode continues, and the status bar reports it.

Pressing or releasing Ctrl, Alt or Shift in the middle of a drag takes effect immediately, as if it had been held from the start. While Ctrl is held, the cursor over a handle is an arrow. A Ctrl drag of the other kind, or dragging a handle without Ctrl, starts again from the enclosing rectangle.

Tool options

Set ⑦ canvas width/height, ⑧ Anchor at canvas center (when on, the center stays put and both opposite edges move together), ⑨ Stretch with 9-slice and the middle fill (Stretch / Tile), ⑩ image width/height, ⑪ Keep image ratio, ⑬ image position X/Y and ⑭ the 9-direction anchor (snaps the image to that spot of the canvas) with numbers and buttons. Changing a number moves the handles on the canvas too. The anchor starts at the center, and the chosen anchor stays shown even when the image and canvas are the same size. The ⑫ Aspect ratio button next to ⑪ opens the aspect ratio window described below.

Aspect ratio

  • ⑯ Ratio: pick 1:1, 4:3, 3:4, 3:2, 2:3, 16:9 or 9:16, or type width:height in the two fields below (1–100 each).
  • ⑰ Keep: Keep width keeps the current canvas width and sets the height from the ratio; Keep height keeps the height and sets the width.
  • ⑱ Method: Canvas only leaves the image as is and adds blank space to, or crops, the changing side according to the anchor (the center when no anchor button is pressed; cropped pixels are kept as pixels outside the canvas). Stretch image stretches or shrinks the image to fit the new canvas exactly, with nearest neighbor (or 9-slice when it is on), and turns ⑪ Keep image ratio off when the result no longer has the original image's ratio.
  • ⑲ The preview shows the current canvas (dotted), the new canvas (accent border) and where the image goes, with parts leaving the new canvas faded. ⑳ Below it you see Canvas W×H → W′×H′ and the image size; if the new size is outside 1px to the maximum size, a warning line appears and OK is disabled.

The ⑫ button exists only in canvas size mode. OK only changes the mode's values: the canvas changes when you apply the mode (Enter), and cancelling (Esc) reverts it. The chosen ratio, basis and method are remembered until you quit the app.

Right-drag margin and Ctrl corners

㉑ While you right-drag a handle (margin), the light-blue dashed outline and the size label turn yellow and the white square handles become outward yellow arrows. ㉒ Add Ctrl (⌘ on macOS) and only the dragged corner moves (both ends of the side for a side handle); the border follows that shape, and everything outside it — the image, pixels outside the canvas and parts — is cropped (it disappears from the preview as well). The new canvas is the rectangle around the shape. A Ctrl+drag without the right button skews the image in perspective to the same shape instead.

What applying does

Applies to all frames and layers, and part positions are adjusted as well. Parts cut off by shrinking are not deleted but kept as pixels outside the canvas, so they come back when you enlarge it again. Resizing the image (dragging a handle or image width/height) scales pixels inside and outside the canvas together by the same ratio relative to the original canvas, without seams (1:1 outside the canvas with 9-slice), and flipping or rotating the canvas also carries the pixels outside the canvas.

During the mode, the pivot mark shows where the current layer's pivot will land after applying. Pivots follow the same point of the image, so moving or resizing the image moves the pivot mark with it, and applying moves every layer pivot and the canvas pivot accordingly. If you only change the margin by right-dragging handles and the image stays put, the pivots stay at the same spot on the image.

Stretch with 9-slice

  • When the frame (or a part) has 9-slice borders, ⑨ Stretch with 9-slice is on by default. The controls are the same as in the table above (dragging a handle or image width/height = image size, right-drag = margin); when the image is resized, corners stay as they are, edges stretch in one direction only and only the middle grows, like a Unity UI image with Image Type = Sliced. When it is off or there are no borders, the whole image is stretched with nearest neighbor. Skewing in perspective with Ctrl removes the 9-slice borders.
  • Middle fill: Stretch = nearest-neighbor stretch or shrink (Unity Sliced), Tile = repeat the original pattern at its size (Unity Tiled — good for patterned middles).
  • While dragging, the borders are shown as green dotted lines, and the image can't get smaller than both borders plus one pixel.
  • After applying, the L, T, R and B values stay the same number of pixels from the image edges (plus any margin you left), so the Unity Sprite Border still matches. Parts with their own borders are stretched with those, and pivots follow the same mapping.

Transform whole layer

Rotate, scale, move and distort every frame of the current layer (every skin of a part) together with one orange box. The canvas size stays the same. Enter with Transform whole layer… in the Edit menu (Alt+Shift+C); the handles work as in transform mode, which changes just the current cel. Applying is one undo step.

The box starts at the selection if there is one, at the part area for a part, and otherwise at the rectangle around the painted pixels of all frames (including pixels outside the canvas). The view stays as it is when the mode starts. It doesn't start for a group, a locked layer or a layer with nothing drawn in any frame; the status bar explains why.

Controls

ActionResult
Drag inside, arrow keysMoves the drawing of every frame together (Shift+arrow keys = 8 pixels).
① Drag a square handleResize (Shift = keep ratio, Alt = from center). Drag with Ctrl (⌘ on macOS) for free distort.
Drag just outside the borderRotate (Shift = 15° steps). Each frame turns around its own pivot.
Drag the rotation centerMoves the rotation center of every frame by the same amount.
Right-click, right-dragSame as in transform mode: the rotate/flip menu; inside = move only the drawing (the rotation center and pivots stay); just outside the border = rotate around the middle of the box (the rotation center turns with the drawing); ① handles = margin (pixels to be cropped show faded when you narrow it).
Click another frame in the timelineOn another frame of the same layer the mode continues and shows that frame. Selecting another layer cancels the mode without applying.
Enter / EscApply / cancel

Pressing or releasing Ctrl, Alt or Shift in the middle of a drag takes effect immediately.

② Tool options show the same group as transform mode, titled Transform · whole layer: angle, width/height %, center X/Y, box width/height (margin), ③ Flip (flips the box horizontally or vertically — the change happens when you apply) and resampling (the same setting as transform mode). When you change a part that has 9-slice borders without a selection, a Stretch with 9-slice and middle fill row is added (the same setting as canvas size mode). The preview is drawn between the layers below and above without layer effects, and parts that go outside the canvas are shown faded.

What applying does

CaseWhat changes
With a selectionOnly the pixels in that area change in every frame, and the pivots stay. A part area grows only when the drawing goes past it. After applying, the selection keeps the transformed shape.
Normal layer (no selection)The painted pixels of every frame (including pixels outside the canvas) and the layer's pivot (its default pivot and per-frame pivots) all follow. If you narrowed the box, pixels outside it are removed in every frame. Other layers' pivots and the canvas's own pivot stay as they are.
Part: resized or rotatedThe part area follows the box (the size around the rotated box when rotated), and the change applies to every skin and linked cel. Stretching without rotating keeps the borders and grows only the middle according to the 9-slice setting; rotating or distorting removes the 9-slice borders. The part pivot follows the same point of the image.
Part: only movedThe part's base position moves instead of the drawing, so every frame moves by the same amount and per-frame differences are kept. The part pivot moves along. To move the position of just one frame, move it in that frame with transform mode and no selection (Part position per frame).

Whatever you move by right-dragging inside leaves the pivots in place. Moving a part with the Move tool (or Ctrl+drag) only moves the pixels in the cel and doesn't affect the part position or part pivot.

Pivot tool

The pivot is the reference point of the image. Every layer (normal layer, group or part) has its own pivot, used as the rotation center of transform mode, Around pivot in symmetry drawing, and the center of layer effects such as rotate and squash; the canvas pivot used for sheet and Unity exports is separate. Set it with the Pivot tool (K) by dragging on the canvas or with the buttons and numbers in Tool options.

Moving the pivot

  • ① Drag on the canvas and the pivot mark (a circle with a cross) follows; on release it is set, snapped to half pixels (one undo step). The mark is white while the Pivot tool is active; with other tools it is shown in orange when the Pivot view in the top bar (View menu Show pivot) is on. While that view is on, the pivots of the other visible layers and of the canvas also appear as small labeled dots (Canvas). With the view off, the Pivot tool shows only the pivot it edits (plus the part pivots in the group view).
  • ② The 5×5 buttons pick 0, 1/4, 1/2, 3/4 or the end, horizontally and vertically. They snap to whole pixels, so 1/2 rounds down for odd sizes (for example width 49 → 24).
  • ③ X is measured in pixels from the left and Y from the bottom (as in Unity). You can enter 0.5px steps; the target's width and height are shown next to the fields.

From current frame on

④ Turning on From current frame on gives the current frame through the last frame their own pivot for the target (and, for a part, their own position) separate from the other frames. It works for normal layers, groups, parts and the canvas pivot. Nothing changes visually when you turn it on, and the target text changes to frame N to the end.

  • With it on, changing the pivot with the numbers, buttons or a canvas drag shifts this frame and every later frame by the same amount. Differences that already existed between frames are kept, and earlier frames don't change.
  • Turning it off removes the separate pivots and positions from the current frame to the end, so those frames follow the target's default pivot again (the part pivot and base position for a part).
  • Separate pivots are also created in these cases (the box then shows as on in that frame): applying transform mode without a selection (that frame's pivot follows the drawing — or, when a part is only moved, that frame's part position moves), and permanently applying an effect such as shake or float with Move pivot along on to a part (the part position moves per frame by the motion, while the image stays in place in the cel).

Target

⑤ The top of the pivot group in Tool options names the pivot you are changing. The Pivot tool edits the pivot of the current layer selected in the timeline; clicking the Canvas W×H text in the chip at the top left of the timeline edits the canvas pivot instead (the text gets a highlight border). Clicking a layer name or selecting another layer goes back to the layer pivot.

Selected layerPivot that changesTarget text
Normal layerThat layer's pivot, measured against the canvas. Effects, transforms and symmetry on that layer use it.Target: pivot of layer "name" (canvas space, width×height)
PartThat part's pivot (its part atlas sprite), measured against the part size.Target: part "name" (width×height, part atlas)
GroupThat group's pivot, measured against the canvas. In the group view each part's pivot is shown in blue and dragging on the canvas moves parts instead of the pivot (Arranging), so set the group pivot with the ② buttons and ③ numbers. Select a part to change its pivot.Target: pivot of group "name" (canvas space, width×height)
Canvas pivot (the Canvas W×H text in the chip)The canvas's own pivot used for sheet and Unity exports, measured against the canvas. With a representative layer, the canvas pivot in each frame is that layer's pivot in that frame.Target: canvas pivot (export, all frames)

While the canvas pivot is the target, a Representative choice (None (canvas's own pivot) plus the layers) appears below the target text. Pick a layer and the canvas pivot in each frame follows that layer's pivot in that frame (target text Target: canvas pivot = pivot of representative layer "name"); edits then change that layer's pivot. The representative layer gets a small crown before its name in the timeline, and you can also set it with Use as canvas pivot representative / Stop using as canvas pivot representative in the layer's right-click menu (one undo step).

⑥ Cels with their own pivot show an orange diamond at their top left in the timeline: always for parts (cels with their own part pivot or position), and only in the current layer's row for normal layers and groups. The diamond on frame headers appears only while the canvas pivot is the target, on frames with their own canvas pivot. The part position menu and the status bar show the position in the current frame; changing that position moves all frames by the same amount.

Frame header menu

Right-clicking a timeline frame header handles just that frame: Use a separate pivot for this frame only keeps the current pivot as that frame's own pivot, and Reset pivot to default removes that frame's own pivot. Both apply to the current pivot target (the current layer or the canvas pivot).

Pivot moved by effects

While effects are shown on the canvas, if an effect such as shake moves the image, the pivot mark is drawn where the effect moved it (the center later effects actually use). With the Pivot tool, the original spot that the tool edits is also shown with a small circle and a dotted line.

Where pivots are used: the canvas pivot (or the representative layer's pivot, if set) is the sprite Pivot for Unity exports and sheets; a layer's pivot is the starting rotation center of transform mode and Transform whole layer, Around pivot in symmetry drawing, and the center for that layer's layer effects (rotate, move pivot, script ctx.pivot and so on use that layer's pivot in that frame). Canvas size mode and flipping or rotating the canvas move every layer pivot and the canvas pivot with the image; Transform whole layer moves all of that layer's pivots, transform mode without a selection moves that frame's pivot, and a left drag with the Move tool moves the layer pivot of the moved frames (right-drag to move only the drawing and leave the pivot in place).

Older .chickxel files from before layer pivots are converted on opening without asking. Each normal layer and group gets a copy of the old canvas pivot (default and per frame), so nothing looks different; the canvas pivot stays the same and there is no representative layer. Saving writes the new format, which older versions of Chickxel can't open.

9-slice

Sets the Unity Sprite Border (9-slice borders). Corners and edges outside the borders stay the same when stretched and only the middle grows, which suits UI buttons and panels used with Image Type = Sliced in Unity. Choosing the 9-slice tool (S) shows four green lines on the canvas.

Setting the borders

Dragging ① the four green lines snaps them to the nearest pixel boundary and changes ② the L, T, R and B values (pixels measured from the image edges). A line can't cross the opposite line. In Tool options you can enter numbers or clear the borders with ③ All 0. Each line release is one undo step.

Target

④ With a part selected it applies to that part; otherwise to every cell of the frame sheet, and Tool options shows the target. In the group view each part shows its green lines, and dragging one changes that part's borders.

When stretched

Stretching without borders thickens the corners and border lines too, while 9-slice keeps the corners as they are, stretches the edges in one direction only and grows only the middle. Besides stretching in Unity, you can resize the image the same way inside Chickxel with Stretch with 9-slice in canvas size mode.

Files · saving · export

This chapter covers the whole path of a file: opening it, keeping your work safe while you edit, saving, and exporting outputs. Everything you edit lives in a single project file (.chickxel); outputs for game engines or other programs, such as PNG and .meta files, are rebuilt from it every time you save.

  1. Open: a .chickxel opens as is; PNGs, other images, animations and videos are imported as new projects (Opening files). Images dropped on the canvas become layers (Dropping images onto the canvas).
  2. While you edit: files that were saved before are saved automatically when you pause (Autosave), and undo history survives closing the file (Undo history that survives closing). To throw all edits away, use Revert to saved.
  3. Save: Ctrl+S writes the .chickxel and, depending on the export settings, the images and .meta files too (Saving). Cell names are covered in Export cell names, and old outputs that are no longer produced in Cleaning up old outputs. For the export settings themselves see Export dialog; for GIFs see Export as animated GIF.
  4. Close: closing unsaved documents or quitting gathers them in one dialog that asks whether to save (Save prompt when closing).

Preferences and the settings folder are in Preferences; unexpected errors, logs and recovery files are in When errors occur.

File types and where they go

Chickxel works with two kinds of files: the project file, which is the editable source, and the outputs made from it when you save or export. Outputs are rebuilt on every save, so always edit the project file. Opening an exported PNG opens the project that made it (Opening (importing) PNGs).

FileRole
.chickxelThe project file (editable source). Layers, effects, frames, tags, parts, skins, outfit presets and export settings are all stored only here. Double-clicking it in Explorer or Finder opens it in Chickxel.
Exported images (PNG, etc.)The output: frame sheets and part atlases written as PNG (default), JPG, TGA or BMP, for use in Unity or other programs.
.metaThe meta file Unity reads (sprite cells, pivot, 9-slice border, etc.). It is named after the image with .meta appended (hero.png.meta).
.spriteLibUnity Sprite Library. Created only when turned on (see Parts · skins).
.gifAn animation made separately with File > Export as animated GIF…. Saving doesn't create it (Export as animated GIF).
.chickxel-assetAsset package file. Bundles script effect, effect preset and gradient assets for moving them; importing it in the Asset Manager or double-clicking it in Explorer or Finder adds the assets inside one by one.

Where outputs go

Unless you change the folder or name in the export dialog, outputs are created next to the project and named after it. Saving hero.chickxel gives:

OutputDefault nameCreated when
Frame sheethero.pngExport on save is on (the default for new projects)
Part atlashero_parts.pngThe project has parts and part atlas export is on (Export: frame sheet and part atlas)
Meta fileshero.png.meta, hero_parts.png.metaDepending on Write .meta and the folder (Saving)
Sprite LibraryOne per group and skinSprite Library is on (Unity Sprite Library)

Exporting separate files per tag, skin or part creates several files named after the project followed by that name. The folder can be a path relative to the project folder or a full path; empty means the project folder (Export dialog).

What's inside a project file

A .chickxel is a ZIP archive. It holds project.json with the structure and settings, plus a PNG for every cel that has pixels (layer × frame, and per skin if there are skins); empty cels aren't stored. Pixels pushed outside the canvas are saved too. Every save also adds the composited first frame (at most 256 px on the long side) as thumbnail.png. Chickxel doesn't use it when opening the file; only the Windows Explorer preview reads it (see Explorer previews in Opening files).

The export folder and export names (sheet name and part atlas name) are written with export path encryption, locked with a key that belongs to this install. The file only shows chickxel-enc:v1:…, so giving a .chickxel to someone doesn't reveal your folder names or user name. The key is made at random the first time you save and kept in local-path-key.bin in the settings folder (on Windows encrypted so only your user can unlock it; on macOS and Linux readable only by your user).

The same user on the same computer gets them back as is. Opened on another computer or by another user, they can't be unlocked and the defaults are used (the project file's folder and the project name). Deleting the settings folder (including when you delete it while reinstalling) also resets the export folder and names of projects saved earlier to the defaults. Older files with plain paths are still read and are encrypted the next time you save.

Projects saved by this version use a new .chickxel format (format version 5) that stores a pivot for every layer, so older versions of Chickxel can't open them. Effects are stored by stable type IDs, so effects this version doesn't know (for example, ones added in a newer Chickxel) aren't drawn but are kept when you save, and script effect sources are saved inside the project file (see Script effects).

Files in the older format without layer pivots, and effect presets, still open. Older-format files are converted without asking: every normal layer and group gets a copy of the old canvas pivot (the default pivot and any per-frame pivots), so nothing looks different; the canvas pivot stays as it was and there is no representative layer (see Pivot tool). The status bar message for opening ends with Converted from the older format (version N), and when the app reopens your last tabs at startup it reports how many files were converted. Saving writes the new format, which older versions of Chickxel can't open.

A file in a format version from a newer Chickxel isn't opened; you're told This file was made with a newer Chickxel (format version N). Update the app in that case (Updates). .gpixel files from the app's former name (Gpixel) open as projects too.

Undo history, the app trash and preferences are kept separately in the app settings folder, not in the project file (Preferences).

Opening files

Each file opens in its own document tab. Opening a file that is already open switches to its tab instead of creating a new one, and the status bar shows Already open. Whichever way you open a file, it is handled the same way for its file type.

Ways to open

MethodHow
File menuFile > Open (.chickxel · image · GIF · video)… (Ctrl+O). Pick several files at once to open a tab for each.
Recent filesPick from File > Recent files or double-click a recent file on the start screen. Up to 10 recently opened or saved files are remembered; Clear list at the end of the list empties it. Picking a file that no longer exists tells you and removes it from the list. Folders are remembered separately (10) in Recent folders.
File browserClick a file in the file browser on the left. Choose the folder it shows with File > Open folder… or Open Unity project… (File browser).
Dropping on the windowDrop files from Explorer or Finder outside the canvas (file list, start screen, tab strip, panels) to open each in a new tab. Images dropped on the canvas become layers of the current document (Dropping images onto the canvas).
Explorer · FinderDouble-click a .chickxel file (see below).

What each file type opens as

FileOpens as
.chickxel, .gpixelThat project.
PNGThe project that made the PNG if there is one; otherwise the PNG is imported as a new project (Opening (importing) PNGs).
JPG, BMP, GIF, WebP, ICO, animated PNGA new document with one frame per image frame (Opening images, animations and videos).
Videos (MP4, WebM, MOV, …), AVIF, HEICRead with ffmpeg into a new document. Videos first show the Import video window (Opening images, animations and videos).

Opening by double-clicking in Explorer or Finder

Double-click a saved .chickxel project file in Explorer (Windows) or Finder (macOS) to open it. Double-clicking a .chickxel-asset asset package imports the assets inside. This file association is registered as follows on each OS.

OSFile association
WindowsRegistered once you run the new version of the app. Every start registers the current Chickxel.exe for your user account (no administrator rights needed), so if you move the exe, start it once again.
macOSAssociated once Chickxel.app is in the Applications folder.
LinuxNo file associations yet. Open files with File > Open or by dropping them on the window.
  • If the app is already running, the file opens as a document tab in that window instead of a new window (or switches to its tab if it is already open).
  • If another app is set to open .chickxel files, right-click the file and choose Chickxel under Open with (Windows) or Open With (macOS).

Previews in Windows Explorer

In Windows Explorer's Medium icons, Large icons and Extra large icons views, a .chickxel file shows the project's first frame instead of the app icon. The picture is enlarged without blurring the pixels and centered, with the palette chick badge at the bottom right so you can tell it's a Chickxel file. The Small icons, List and Details views keep showing the app icon.

Explorer reads only the thumbnail.png stored inside the file on save (see File types and where they go). Files saved before this feature show the app icon; open them in Chickxel and save once to get the picture.

Like the file associations, the preview is registered for your user account automatically when Chickxel starts (no administrator rights needed). If you move the exe, it's set up again the next time you start it.

Files changed outside the app

When an open project file is changed by another program (a sync app, another Chickxel window, …), Chickxel notices and does the following.

CaseWhat happens
The document you are viewingAsks whether to reload (warning that your edits will be lost if there are any). Choosing No keeps the document as is; saving afterwards overwrites the externally changed file. Autosave pauses while asking.
Another tab without unsaved editsThe file is reread when you switch to that tab.
Another tab with unsaved editsKeeps the edits in memory and notes it in the status bar. Saving overwrites the externally changed file.

Opening (importing) PNGs

Opening a PNG first looks for the project that made it. If a .chickxel with the same name is in the same folder, or a project in the same folder has a record of exporting that PNG, that project opens instead of the PNG. Otherwise the PNG is imported as a new project.

Saving an imported PNG

An imported project already has a save location: a .chickxel with the same name next to the PNG (hero.png → hero.chickxel). The status bar says saving will create hero.chickxel, and the first Ctrl+S saves there without asking.

The export settings point at the original PNG's folder and name, so saving rewrites that PNG as the project's output and keeps the guid of its existing .meta. Until the first save there is no .chickxel, so autosave doesn't run (Autosave).

Sheets with cell information

  • If the .meta next to the PNG (Unity sprite mode Multiple) contains cell information and the cells differ in size, it's imported as parts: one group named after the file, with one part per cell (keeping each cell's name, size, pivot and border).
  • If there are two or more cells and all are the same size, you're asked to choose ① As parts or ② As animation frames.
  • Importing as animation frames makes one frame per cell, and each cell's name becomes that frame's export cell name. Frames are aligned so every cell's pivot lands on the same spot.

Splitting sheets without cell information

Sheets without cell information can be split with ③ Frame·Layer > Split into frames by grid: ④ pick the method and split by ⑤ cell size, ⑥ cell count or ⑦ automatically (by blobs).

Opening images, animations and videos

Opening an image other than PNG, an animation or a video creates a new document. There's no .chickxel yet, so the first save asks where to put it. The result is the same whether you open from the menu, the file browser, recent files or by dropping on the window.

Images and animations

① File > Open (.chickxel · image · GIF · video)… (Ctrl+O) lets you pick several files at once and opens each in ② its own tab. Images other than PNG (JPG, BMP, GIF, WebP, ICO, animated PNG) become a new document: canvas = image size, ③ one layer named after the file, ④ one frame per image frame.

  • Frame durations come from the file (0–10 ms become 100 ms, like browsers). Transparency is kept, and partial GIF/APNG frames with their disposal (restore to background / previous) are composited so every frame is a complete picture. Photos follow their EXIF rotation.
  • Files with more than 300 frames ask ⑤ "Open all / First 300 only / Cancel".

Videos and ffmpeg

Videos (MP4, WebM, MOV, MKV, AVI, …) and AVIF/HEIC images need ffmpeg (not included with Chickxel and never downloaded automatically — ffmpeg.org, or winget install Gyan.FFmpeg on Windows). It's found automatically on PATH or in common install locations; otherwise choose ⑦ the executable in ⑥ Edit > Preferences… > Video (ffmpeg) with ⑧ Browse….

Opening a video first shows the Import video window: ⑨ range (start–end seconds), ⑩ frames per second (default: the video's, at most 12), ⑪ max frames (default 120), ⑫ size (original / fit long side — turn ⑬ Smooth off to keep pixels crisp), and ⑭ an estimate of frames, size and memory. ⑮ Import shows a progress window you can cancel; each frame lasts 1000 ÷ frames per second.

More frames use more memory. When the estimated memory exceeds 1 GB, the Import video window shows a warning; reduce the size or lower the frames per second or max frames.

Smooth on and off

Smooth only applies when the size is set to Fit long side. On averages pixels while resizing, which suits live-action or ordinary video, but pixel art gets blended colors along its edges that weren't there before. Off takes the nearest pixel as is and keeps only the original colors, which suits recordings of pixel art.

Dropping images onto the canvas

Drag images (PNG, JPG, BMP, GIF, WebP, APNG, …) or videos from Explorer/Finder or the file browser onto the canvas and each file becomes a new layer. While dragging, the canvas edge lights up and ① a dashed rectangle shows where the image will go.

Name and order of the new layers

  • ② The new layer is added above the current layer and named after the file (without the extension). Several files stack in drop order, and the last one ends up on top as the current layer. With a part or group selected, it goes above that group, like Add layer.
  • The whole drop is a single undo step (Drop image).

Placement and the area outside the canvas

The image is placed with the pixel you dropped on at its center, in the current frame, with its colors unchanged. Anything past the canvas edge isn't cut off; it's kept as pixels outside the canvas. An image larger than the canvas is centered on the canvas and the status bar tells you so — enlarge the canvas in Canvas size mode to see the part that sticks out.

Animated GIF, WebP and APNG files and videos (after the Import video window) go into one layer starting at the current frame; if there aren't enough frames, new ones are added at the end (with the image's or video's frame durations).

Transforming right after the drop

A single-frame image goes straight into ③ transform mode (the last one if you drop several; ④ the status bar explains the keys): the whole image is held, so move or resize it and press Enter to apply, or Esc to keep it as inserted (insert and transform are two undo steps). Either way, anything past the canvas edge isn't cut off; it stays as pixels outside the canvas. Multi-frame images are inserted without transform mode.

Dropping outside the canvas

Dropping outside the canvas (file list, start screen, tab strip, panels) doesn't add layers — each file opens in its own tab. Dropping only a .chickxel on the canvas opens it in a new tab too.

Saving
