Ctrl+S (① File > Save (+ export sheets)) saves the .chickxel, also exports the images and meta files if export is on, and ② shows the result in the status bar. When there is nothing to save, the toolbar's save button is disabled.

What a save does

  1. The project is written in full to a temporary file in the same folder (….chickxel-tmp), which then replaces the original .chickxel.
  2. If Export on save is on in the export dialog (the default for new projects), the frame sheet, part atlas, meta files and .spriteLib are written (where outputs go). If it is off, the status bar adds (export off).
  3. Old outputs exported last time but not produced this time are moved to the app trash (Cleaning up old outputs).
  4. The status bar shows Saved: file name with the exported sheet names and cell counts (plus + .meta if meta files were written), and the file is added to recent files and undo history.

When meta files are written

The meta file next to each image (.meta) is written according to Write .meta in the export dialog.

SettingMeta file
Auto (default)Written only when the image is inside a Unity project's Assets folder or already has a .meta.
AlwaysAlways written.
OffNot written. However, with Sprite Library on, the part atlas meta file is always written (the library must point to its sprites), and .spriteLib files always get a meta file too.

When rewriting an existing meta file, its guid and each sprite's spriteID are kept. Sprites with the same cell name keep their IDs, and a renamed cell inherits the ID of the old cell at the same position, so references on the Unity side don't break.

The name on first save

The first time you save a never-saved new project (Save or Save as), the save dialog suggests its temporary title as the name (without characters not allowed in file names; sprite if nothing is left or there is no temporary title). If that name already exists in the folder the dialog opens in (the file browser's current folder, otherwise the last folder), the first free name such as sprite_1.chickxel, sprite_2.chickxel… is used.

Save as

Ctrl+Shift+S (File > Save as…) saves to a location and name you pick, and the current document then points to that new file. It doesn't make a separate copy, so later saves go to the new file. The original file and the outputs exported under its name stay where they are and aren't cleaned up. Outputs that used the default name are created under the new project name; a folder or name set explicitly in the export dialog is kept as is.

Exporting without saving

The Export button in the export dialog (Ctrl+E) writes only the outputs with the current settings, without saving the .chickxel (a never-saved document asks for a save location first). Old outputs are cleaned up the same way as on save. GIFs are made separately with Export as animated GIF.

If saving fails

If the .chickxel was saved but only exporting the outputs failed (for example because another program has an image open), ③ you're told about it. The project counts as saved.

MessageMeaning and what to do
Couldn't saveThe .chickxel couldn't be written. The document stays modified; remove the cause and save again, or save under another name.
The file is read-onlyTurn off read-only in the file's properties, or save under another name.
Another program has the file openClose that program (image viewer, game engine, sync app, …) or save under another name.
Export file names collideThe project is saved and only the export fails. Rename the frame sheet, part atlas, tag, part or skin so they differ.

When saving takes a while

If saving or opening a project takes a while, a dark translucent overlay covers the whole window until it finishes. A box in the middle shows Saving or Opening, a spinning loading icon, the percentage with a progress bar and Editing is paused until this finishes. Meanwhile drawing, shortcuts and file drops are ignored and other windows (such as the AI chat) are disabled; AI tool calls run once it finishes. Closing the window then closes it after the save completes.

Autosave

On by default. After 3 seconds without edits, it saves without asking, just like Ctrl+S (including export), and shows Autosaved in ① the status bar.

When it saves and when it doesn't

  • It waits while you're drawing, dragging, transforming or while the AI is working.
  • Only files that already have a .chickxel on disk are saved. New projects and imported PNGs, images or videos that haven't been saved yet must be saved manually once.
  • Autosave writes in the background without the overlay, so you can keep editing while it saves; changes made meanwhile go into the next autosave. Only very large documents (over about 768 MB of pixels) show the overlay during autosave too. Saving, exporting, Revert to saved or closing the window during an autosave continues once the autosave finishes.
  • It pauses while you're asked whether to reload an externally changed file, save unsaved documents or revert to saved, so edits you are about to discard aren't written in the meantime.
  • If an autosave fails, no dialog appears; the status bar shows Autosave (file name) — followed by the reason. Pressing Ctrl+S yourself shows the failure dialog with details (Saving).

Turning it on/off and the wait time

  • ② Turn it on/off and change ③ the wait time (1–600 seconds) in Preferences > General.

Revert to saved

① The button at the bottom of the toolbar or ② File > Revert to saved discards unsaved changes after ③ confirmation and reopens the file. The current layer, frame and zoom stay the same. The revert itself can't be undone.

When it is available

It is enabled only when the current document has unsaved edits and there is a file on disk to revert to. An imported PNG not yet saved as .chickxel rereads that PNG. There is no default shortcut; you can assign one in Customizing shortcuts.

Undo history that survives closing

On by default: even after closing a file or quitting the app, you can keep using undo and redo when you reopen it. History is kept only up to the saved state; changes discarded with "Don't save" aren't kept. The history is stored separately in the app settings folder, not in the .chickxel.

① Turning it on/off, ② retention period (default 30 days), ③ maximum size (default 500 MB) and ④ Clear all history are in Preferences > General.

Both undo and redo steps are kept, up to Undo steps. Opening the file loads the history and the status bar adds carried over N undo steps. If the file changed outside the app, you're told the file changed externally, so the previous undo history was discarded.

When history is discarded

  • The file is missing or was changed outside the app (its modified time or size differs from the record)
  • The file wasn't opened or saved within the retention period (0 days = unlimited)
  • All history together exceeds the max size — starting with files not opened for the longest
  • The file was deleted in the file browser (restoring it from the app trash doesn't bring the history back)
  • You press Clear all history (the current undo of open files stays)

History is looked up by file path. Renaming or moving a file in Chickxel's file browser carries its history along, but a file renamed in Explorer or Finder loses the connection to its history.

Export cell names

Every cell (Unity sprite) in an exported sheet gets a name. To name one frame yourself, right-click its header in the timeline, choose ① Export cell name… and ② enter the name. Leave it empty to use the rule below.

Naming rules

Cell names in a frame sheet are decided in this order. Numbers start at 0. The overall rule (Frame number or By tag) is chosen under Cell naming rule in the export dialog.

CaseCell name
The frame has an export cell nameThat name
The rule is By tag and the frame is inside a tag{Tag}_{number within the tag} (e.g. run_0, run_1)
Otherwise{sheet file name}_{frame number} (e.g. hero_0)

Control characters are removed, / and \ become _, and leading/trailing spaces are trimmed. If nothing is left, the name is sprite. Duplicate names within one sheet get _1, _2, … appended to the later ones.

Cell names in part atlases come from part, tag and skin names, not from frame cell names (Export: frame sheet and part atlas).

Cell names in Unity

Cell names are written to the meta file as sprite names. Unity links sprites by name, so renaming sprites already used in prefabs or animations may break those references; changing the cell naming rule in the export dialog shows this warning. A renamed cell still inherits the ID of the old cell at the same position (Saving).

Cleaning up old outputs and renaming files

Chickxel records in the project which outputs it exported itself. It uses that record to clear away old outputs that are no longer produced and to move outputs along when you rename the project. Everything cleared goes to the app trash, so it can be restored.

Clearing old outputs on save

Old output files (and their .meta) that are no longer produced because you changed the export method or name are moved to the app trash on the next save or export, and ① the status bar ends with cleaned up N old outputs (app trash). If they can't be moved, the save still succeeds and you're only told about it.

Only files this project recorded as its own exports last time are cleared. Files you created yourself are never touched. Save as starts a new record, so outputs under the old name stay, and older projects without a record don't clear anything.

Renaming or deleting in the file browser

Renaming or deleting a .chickxel in the file browser also moves the outputs exported under the default name (starting with the project name) in the project folder, with their .meta, or sends them to the app trash (hero_walk.png → knight_walk.png). The .meta moves along, so Unity guids are kept.

Outputs with a custom export folder or name are left in place. To delete only the project file and keep its outputs, use Delete .chickxel files only in the right-click menu (File browser).

Restoring

Press ② Ctrl+Z in the file list to restore the most recently trashed item to its original place. For older ones, open ③ View app trash…, pick ④ the entry and press ⑤ Restore (double-click works too). An entry isn't restored if a file with the same name already exists at its original place. For retention time and size, see the app trash in File browser.

Save prompt when closing

Closing a document with unsaved edits asks whether to save. When you close the window, quit, close all tabs or before installing an update, ① unsaved files are gathered in a single dialog with their thumbnails and names.

  • ② Save selected files: saves the checked files like Ctrl+S (including export) and closes. If any save fails, the status bar says so and nothing closes.
  • ③ Don't save: closes without saving. Discarded edits aren't kept in undo history either.
  • ④ Cancel: nothing closes.

How each case asks

How you closeHow it asks
Closing one tab (Ctrl+W, the tab's X, middle click)The same dialog with just that file.
Close other tabs (tab right-click)Asks for each unsaved tab in turn; Cancel stops there.
Closing the window, quitting, installing an updateGathers all unsaved files in one dialog.

In the list, never-saved new documents show Choose a save location and ask for a location when saved. Imported PNGs not saved yet show the file that will be created, such as Saving will create hero.chickxel. Autosave pauses while the dialog is open. Tabs open when you quit are reopened at the next start (Document tabs and split view).

Preferences

App-wide settings are changed in ① Edit > Preferences… (Ctrl+,). Per-project settings (such as export) live in the project file, not here.

General tab

The ② General tab has these groups.

GroupWhat it does
LanguageDisplay language and language CSVs (Changing the display language).
③ Undo stepsUndo steps per document (default 1000, max 10000). Lowering it discards the oldest history of open projects first; higher values use more memory.
④ Undo historyUndo history that survives closing (Undo history that survives closing).
⑤ SaveAutosave on/off and wait time (Autosave).
⑥ App trash retentionThe app trash time limit (off by default) and size limit (default 1 GB) (File browser).
UpdatesCheck for a new version at startup (Updates).
⑦ DiscordShow activity on Discord ("Playing Chickxel") (on by default): while the Discord app is running on this computer, your profile shows the chick picture, "Playing Chickxel" and how long you've been at it. Nothing about your work, such as document names, is sent, and only the Discord app on this computer is told (see Network and privacy).
Reset settingsReset settings… at the bottom: shows what will be reset and asks you to confirm, then returns app settings and shortcuts to their defaults. Recent files and folders, window and panel layout, palettes and recent colors, presets you made (such as Pixelate presets), script trust decisions, accounts and asset publishing records stay, and open documents aren't touched. The display language goes back to your computer's language; if that differs from the current one, you're asked whether to restart.

Shortcuts tab

In the ⑧ Shortcuts tab you ⑨ change, add, clear or reset the key of any command or tool, ⑩ search by command name or key, and ⑪ export or import them as JSON. Assigning a key used by another command ⑫ asks whether to remove it there. See Customizing shortcuts for details.

Other tabs

TabWhat it does
AI StatusAI status and settings (One-time setup: install and log in), and the external AI connection that lets other AI apps use Chickxel's tools (Using other AI apps).
AI referencesThe reference folder the AI uses and example display (References: teach the AI your style).
PresetsPresets: Restore auto optimize presets and Restore gradient presets add back built-in presets you deleted and reset edited built-ins to their original values. Presets you made and favorites stay. Gradient presets themselves are managed with Manage Gradient Presets… in the gradient editor or in Tools > Asset Manager…, and auto-optimize presets in the auto optimize settings window.
Video (ffmpeg)Location of the ffmpeg executable used for videos, AVIF and HEIC (Opening images, animations and videos).

The settings folder

Preferences and app data are in a Chickxel folder at a fixed place per OS. Open it with Help > Open settings file location.

OSLocation
Windows%AppData%\Chickxel\
macOS~/Library/Application Support/Chickxel/
Linux~/.config/Chickxel/
ItemContents
settings.jsonPreferences, recent files and folders and other app settings
export-presets.jsonExport setting presets saved in the export dialog
community-session.binSigned-in Chickxel accounts (the account list, the current account and each account's last known name and handle). On Windows it is encrypted so only your user can read it; on other systems the file is readable only by your user.
local-path-key.binThis install's key that locks the export folder and names written in project files (File types and where they go). On Windows it is encrypted so only your user can read it; on other systems the file is readable only by your user. Deleting it resets the export folder and names of earlier projects to the defaults.
History/Undo history that survives closing (Undo history that survives closing)
Trash/The app trash (Cleaning up old outputs)
Logs/Error logs (When errors occur)
Recovery/Documents saved separately when an error forced the app to quit (When errors occur)
Languages/Languages added from CSV (Creating a new language)
References/The default AI reference folder (References: teach the AI your style)

When errors occur

Even when an unexpected error occurs, the app usually doesn't quit; it shows ① a you can keep working dialog with the error and the log file name. ② Open log folder opens the folder with the log, and ③ Report on Discord lets you report it right away. Please attach that log file when reporting (Feedback).

After the error dialog is closed, open the log folder with ④ Help > Open error log folder.

Log files

Logs are kept in Chickxel/Logs in the settings folder as crash-YYYYMMDD-HHmmss.log; only the latest 20 are kept. Each holds the time of the error, the app version, the OS and the error details. If errors come in quick succession, no more dialogs are shown and they are only logged.

Errors that force the app to quit

If the error forces the app to quit, each document with unsaved edits is saved separately as a recovery file in Chickxel/Recovery/ in the settings folder (<document name>-<date time>-<number>.chickxel). The original files aren't changed. Recovery files aren't opened automatically at the next start: open the settings folder with Help > Open settings file location and open the file in Recovery yourself.

Pixelate AI images

AI-generated "pixel-look" images have uneven pixel sizes (e.g. 7–9 px), blurred edges, JPEG noise and hundreds of colors. The Pixelate AI image window turns such an image into clean pixel art in which each source cell becomes one result pixel. The source can have a solid or transparent background, and can be a sprite sheet with several frames or an animated GIF.

Workflow

When the window gets a source, it lays out one candidate tile per checked preset, and Clean up or Auto optimize fills them with results. Usually you compare the candidates, pick one and apply it; you only change settings when no result looks right.

  1. Load a source: open the window and drop or paste an image. Each checked preset gets a candidate tile; run them with Clean up or Auto optimize.
  2. Compare candidates: click candidate tiles to see the large preview and score, and compare with the source cell by cell in the enlarged view.
  3. Find better settings (when needed): run Auto optimize, or change values in the pipeline steps and press Clean up.
  4. Touch up colors (when needed): remove unwanted colors from the chosen result, or pick a missing color from the source and pin it.
  5. Insert: press Apply or Add to new tab to insert the result as a new layer in the current document or as a new document.

How the conversion works

Each result goes through the steps below in order. What each step does and when to change it is summarized in the table on Pipeline settings.

StepWhat it does
Remove background · Split framesMakes the background transparent and splits a sheet into frames.
Pixel grid · Output sizeFinds how many source pixels make one "pixel" (the cell size) and where the cell boundaries are, then decides the output size.
Cell color · PalettePicks one representative color per cell and merges similar colors into a palette.
Cleanup · Frame consistencyFixes orphan pixels and holes, fits the outline to 1 px, and reduces flicker between frames.
ScoreRates each result from 0 to 100 (similarity to the source, clean outlines and more) so candidates can be compared.

Opening the window and loading a source

You can open the window from three places. A window opened without a source waits for an image; one opened from a file or layer uses that image as the source right away. When a source comes in, each checked preset gets one candidate tile.

Ways to open the window

WhereSourceUse it when
① AI > Pixelate AI image…None — an empty window opens with ② a message in the middle; ④ Apply and ⑤ Auto optimize stay off until a source comes in. Drop an image, paste one with Ctrl+V, or use ③ Browse….The image is in another app or on the clipboard
⑥ AI > Pixelate current layer…The current layer (not a group) — if several frames have content, they are joined into a grid, one cell per frame. If the layer is empty, a message says so and the window doesn't open.You already placed the AI image in a document
In the file browser, right-click ⑦ an image file > ⑧ Pixelate AI image…That fileThe image file is in your project folder

Closing the window (Apply, Cancel, the close button or Esc) only hides it until you quit the app. Opening it again with ① Pixelate AI image… brings back the same source, candidates and selection, and an optimization that was running keeps going while it is hidden. Opening it with a new source, as with ⑥, ⑧ or View conversion settings in AI drawing, closes the previous window and opens a new one.

Loading and replacing the source

MethodDetails
DropDrop an image file anywhere on the window. With several files, the first image is used.
PasteCtrl+V outside text boxes. An image file copied in Explorer or an image file path as text comes first; otherwise the clipboard image is used.
BrowseBrowse… starts in the folder selected in the main window's file browser. PNG, JPG, BMP, GIF and WebP files open.
⑨ Replace sourceThe open-folder icon button in the bar under the source, once a source is loaded. Dropping or pasting again also replaces it.

Replacing the source clears the candidates, preview and optimization results and rebuilds everything from the new image. The window isn't modal, so you can keep it open while you work elsewhere, for example in the AI chat.

Animated images and several frames

When a GIF, animated PNG or WebP comes in by drop, Browse, pasting a file or the file browser's right-click menu, it is split into frames just like dropping it on the canvas. The frames are joined into a grid of roughly equal width and height, one cell per frame, and 2. Frames is set to that grid (one frame per cell). Pixelate current layer joins frames into the same kind of grid.

CaseBehavior
More than 300 framesAs with dropping on the canvas, you're asked whether to load all frames or only the first 300.
Frames don't fit the gridThe grid is limited to 64 cells per side and the maximum image size; beyond that only the leading frames are used and a message says how many.
Empty framesThey are left out of the result.
Replace the source with a single imageThe frame split returns to its remembered settings (the same happens when you close the window).

When a source comes in

When a source comes in, the window only prepares it — background removal, a frame split with the current method and grid detection — and runs nothing. The candidate row gets a Not run yet tile for each preset checked in the Auto Optimize Settings window, and the status text tells you to press Clean up or Auto optimize, or right-click a tile and choose Run only this preset.

  • With Auto optimize on load (off by default) on, all checked presets are auto-optimized as soon as a source comes in.
  • Pressing Apply frame split runs each checked preset once with its saved settings, one tile per preset (marked As set (score)); if no preset is checked, only the current settings run once (Current settings).
  • Opened from View conversion settings in the AI drawing chat, the window uses that image and its conversion settings and runs only those settings once. A window opened this way doesn't remember its step settings when closed.

Source and preview

With a source loaded, the window shows the source view on the left, a large preview of the selected candidate in the middle and the candidate row at the bottom. Check the frame boundaries and the cell boundaries actually used in the source view, and play the result in the large preview.

AreaWhat it does
① Source viewAlways shows the source with the applied frame boundaries and numbers, plus the selected candidate's cell boundaries when Show grid is on. Wheel to zoom, drag an empty area to pan, double-click to fit.
② Large previewPlays the selected candidate at the set FPS next to the same source frame (Result and Source). The source shows only the pixels inside that frame's blue box in the source view; neighboring frames and discarded specks show as empty. Left-click an image once to open the enlarged view; right-click the result image to exclude or restore that pixel's color.
③ Color swatchesThe colors the selected candidate uses, as N colors with 14 px swatches, most-used first. Left-click a swatch to briefly highlight that color's pixels in the result (others dimmed); right-click to exclude it.
④ Playback and frame stripPrevious/next frame, play/pause, an FPS box and a strip of numbered thumbnails (the current frame outlined in blue; click one to go there and pause).
⑤ Candidate tilesClick to select one and load its settings on the left; double-click to apply it (Candidates and scores).

Source view

  • Hovering a cell in the source highlights the matching result pixel and moves the preview to that frame.
  • Drag inside a frame to move it, or drag its edges and corners to resize it (in source pixels, kept inside the source). On release the frames are split with those boundaries and the candidates are rebuilt; the edited boundaries are saved with the settings and preset. Changing split conditions such as the mode or frame count discards the edits, but the boundaries on screen stay until you press Apply frame split.
  • Right-click the source for Copy image and Save as PNG… (the background-removed source if that view is on, with "nobg" at the end of the file name). Right-clicking inside a frame adds items at the top that copy or save just that frame.

The bar under the source

ControlWhat it does
⑥ Remove backgroundEraser icon, pressed = on. The same setting as 1. Background. Off converts without removing the background; on uses the last removal method (Auto at first) and shows the background-removed source. Press Clean up to apply the change.
⑦ Show gridGrid icon, pressed = on (on by default). Draws the cell boundaries the selected candidate actually used on the source view — uneven per frame, so the lines match the color blocks; with local cell correction the lines bend per band. Not drawn on the large preview's Source image, but drawn in the enlarged view's source pane. Lines fade or hide when cells are smaller than about 3 px on screen.

The bar also has the Replace source button before these two (Opening the window and loading a source) and the status text after them. The output size is set in Quick settings in the settings column. If the status text is cut off, hover it to read the full text in a tooltip.

Playback and swatches

  • When the preview has focus, Space plays or pauses and Left/Right change the frame. Clicking an image keeps the focus on the preview.
  • A swatch tooltip shows the HEX value and the pixel count over all frames. Swatches take at most half the large preview's height at first and scroll when they overflow. Drag the short handle at the top of the swatches up or down to change that limit (remembered separately for the large preview and the enlarged view); double-click it to reset to half.

Candidates and scores

The candidate row holds one tile per preset. Loading a source lays out Not run yet tiles for the checked presets; Clean up, Auto optimize and a tile's right-click Run only this preset put their results in (applying the frame split runs the checked presets as set). Every tile has a score, so you can compare which result is closest to the source and cleanest.

Reading a candidate tile

PartMeaning
① Candidate tile thumbnailThe result's first frame.
③ Preset nameThe preset that made the tile; name (modified) if it ran with unsaved edits, Current settings if no preset was chosen.
Colors · size · framesNumber of colors used, output size in pixels, number of frames.
② Outline fit modeThe result's Fit outline to 1 px mode.
Blue line at the bottomWhy the tile was made and its score: As set (73.4), or for an optimized result, why it stopped (Optimized, Time limit, Trial limit, Cancelled — see Auto optimize).

The line under the preview shows the selected candidate as preset name · score · cell size · colors · size · frames, and ④ the score breakdown below it lists each term's value, badness % and weight. The terms are explained in Score and optimize criteria.

⑥ Hovering a candidate tile shows a tooltip with the preset name in bold (in full, even if cut off on the tile), the reason and score, colors and size, cell size, and the per-term breakdown below.

Selecting and applying a candidate

  • Clicking a candidate tile selects it and loads its settings on the left. For a preset tile the preset list switches to that preset too, and each step's Optimize check, 0. Optimize criteria, and the output size's Auto and size become the values the tile ran with.
  • For a tile run with a saved preset as is, the save button turns off; for one run with edited values it turns red. If you edited values on the left and haven't pressed Clean up, clicking another tile asks whether to save (Cancel keeps the current tile).
  • Double-click a tile to apply that candidate right away (Output).

Running and not-run tiles

  • A running tile (Clean up, rerun, Auto optimize or the first candidate row) has a short yellow line with a fading tail circling clockwise over its border (blue on the selected tile, grey otherwise), and its thumbnail shows the best result so far whenever it improves. Click it to watch that best result in the large preview, the source's cell boundaries and the enlarged view; it follows the best as it changes (the selected line shows (running) and the tile's border turns blue). Rerunning the selected candidate tile (Clean up, rerun or Continue optimizing) follows it this way from the start without a click. Colors can't be excluded or pinned meanwhile; when the preset finishes its result tile is selected (or, with no result, the tile selected before).
  • ⑤ A preset checked in the Auto Optimize Settings window that has no result yet holds a blank tile at the end of the row (preset name · Not run yet). It shows even before a source is loaded, appears as soon as you check the preset and disappears when you uncheck it (result tiles already made stay). Click a blank tile to load that preset into the settings on the left (the same as picking it in the preset bar — unsaved changes are asked about first); the tile gets a blue border as the selected tile. There's no result yet, so the preview stays as it is; picking a candidate tile or another preset in the preset bar deselects it. Right-click the blank tile > Run only this preset runs just that preset (once the first setup is done and nothing else runs) and puts the result there.

Candidate tile menu

ItemWhat it does
Copy image · Copy enlarged (×4)Puts the result on both Chickxel's clipboard and the system clipboard, so you can paste it on the canvas or into another app. For a multi-frame result, the first frame.
Save as PNG…Saves the first frame as PNG, named source name_preset name.
Rerun only this presetReruns just that tile in place: a tile from a saved preset uses that preset's current saved values; a (modified) tile uses the values it ran with (disabled while running or if the preset was deleted).

Failed results

A result that is empty (all transparent), nearly empty (covers less than ¼ of the source sprite), has only one color, or has every frame under 8×8 is treated as a wrong pixel-size detection: it scores 0 and goes to the end. It is never preselected or chosen as best by Auto optimize; selecting it shows the reason under the preview, such as "The result is empty — the pixel size was detected wrong (183px)".

  • With an automatic grid, such a result is redone automatically with another estimate (another pixel size source, or ½ or ⅓ of the found value), with a note saying so.
  • The automatic cell size never exceeds 1/5 of the frame's shorter side (the limit appears in the grid info), so frame spacing or big blobs in a sheet aren't taken as cells.
  • If the selected result's long side exceeds 256 px, "Large result (…) — check the pixel size" appears under the preview.

Enlarged view and color decision

The Enlarged view puts the result and the source side by side at the same size so you can compare them cell by cell. Left-click the result or source image in the large preview once (without dragging) and it opens over the dialog. You can also trace step by step why a pixel got its color.

Zooming and panning

① The left pane shows the current frame's result (on a checker), ② the right pane the same frame of the source — only the pixels inside that frame's box, with neighboring frames and discarded specks left empty. Both panes always share the same zoom and position. The view opens at the largest integer zoom that fits both panes.

ActionResult
WheelZooms around the pointer. Between 1× and 2× it steps by 0.25 (1, 1.25, 1.5, 1.75, 2×), between 2× and 4× by 0.5, and above that the step doubles with every doubling of zoom (up to 128×). Below 1× it changes by a factor of 1.25.
DragPans both panes together.
④ Fit imageThe button next to the grid button. Fits both images to the panes without stretching, until the width or height touches the pane edge (the zoom may not be a whole number).
Double-clickRefits at the largest integer zoom that fits.
Space · Left/Right · previous, play/pause and next in the top barPlay and step frames with the same frame and FPS as the large preview. The view follows changes to the selected candidate and excluded colors.
Esc · click outside · Close buttonCloses the enlarged view.

③ The Show grid button (pressed = on; it changes together with the same button under the source) draws the real cell boundaries on the source pane and a one-cell-per-pixel grid on the result pane in the same line style (hidden when pixels are smaller than 4 px on screen). Hover the info icon in the top bar to see the controls, one per line.

Showing where a color is used

Where you clickResult
Left-click a result pixelThe color's swatch in ⑤ the color swatches gets a thick accent border and a brief pulse (the row scrolls to it if needed), and the clicked pixel and the outline of same-color areas show briefly. The status line shows the HEX value and result coordinates, and the color decision row for that pixel opens.
Right-click a result pixelExcludes or restores the color shown there (excluding colors).
Left-click a source cellMarks the clicked source cell and the result pixel it became with blue squares, highlights the result's swatch and same-color areas, and opens the color decision row for that result pixel. The status line shows "Source cell #cell color → result #result color" (the cell color is the color before palette mapping). Right-clicking a source cell does nothing.
⑤ Color swatchesThe same swatches as the large preview. Left-click shows where a color is used (for an excluded color, where it used to be), right-click excludes/restores or unpins, and + at the end adds a color from the source.

While the eyedropper (add color from source) is active, clicking a source cell adds a pinned color instead. The frame strip is hidden when there is only one frame.

Color decision

⑥ The Color decision (x, y) row appears above the swatches when you left-click a result pixel or source cell. It reruns that pixel with the same settings and shows, as swatches and HEX values in order, how its color changed at each step; the last color in bold is the result.

Step nameMeaning
Cell color (method)The color picked from the source cell's inner pixels by the cell color method, before palette mapping.
PaletteThe palette color nearest to the cell color by the palette color distance. Shown as Palette (prefer shade family) when the shade family rule picked something other than the nearest color, and Palette (dithering) with dithering.
Later stepsOnly steps that changed the color appear: snap outline color · redraw as vector · remove orphan pixels · fill 1-px holes · thin outline to 1 px · outline · reduce L-corner doubles · remove outer L corners · match neighbor frames · pinned color · excluded color.
Closest palette colors (distance):The three palette colors nearest to the cell color with their distances, and in parentheses the OKLCh difference from the cell color (lightness ±, chroma ±, hue). The chosen one is bold.
  • Hover a step name for that step's rule, or the cell color for its OKLCh lightness and chroma. Working out how this color was chosen… shows while it computes; the row disappears when you change the selected candidate or the excluded or pinned colors (click again to recompute).
  • OKLab distance weighs lightness, chroma and hue equally, so a color with the same lightness plus some chroma can come out closer than a neutral color that differs only in lightness. In that case try another color distance.
  • A cell whose color is black can still end up light. Thin outline only and By shrinking in Fit outline to 1 px repaint inner dark outline cells with the surrounding light color to make the outline 1 px. To keep such a cell black, set Fit outline to 1 px to Off and compare.

Optimization info

The middle of the top bar shows optimization info. While you view a preset tile that Auto optimize is running, it shows the progress line (name (k/n) · pass · trial n/budget · best score · t s) and the score graph as it grows; for an optimized candidate, its summary (k passes · a → b · n trials · t s · stop reason) and that run's graph. It is empty for a candidate run as set.

Excluding and pinning colors

Touch up the selected result's colors without rebuilding it. Remove unwanted colors by excluding them, and bring back colors that the source has but the result lost by pinning them. Both apply to the current result only; other pixels, candidate ranking and scores stay as they are.

Excluding a color

  • Right-click a swatch to exclude that color. Only that color's pixels change, each to the remaining color nearest to its source cell, so N colors drops by exactly the number excluded. Right-clicking a pixel in the large preview's result image or the enlarged view's result pane excludes the color shown there.
  • ① An excluded swatch stays dimmed at the end with a red border and hatching; right-click it again to restore it. With colors excluded, ② "N colors excluded · Restore all" appears. The last remaining color can't be excluded (the status line says so).
  • Left-clicking an excluded swatch highlights where that color used to be.

Pinning a color

  • Press ③ + (add color from source) at the end of the swatches to start the eyedropper. The source view, the large preview's source image and the enlarged view's source pane get an eyedropper cursor that highlights the hovered cell, and the visible source image's border slowly pulses to show where to pick.
  • Click a cell and its representative color (using the selected candidate's cell boundaries and cell color method) becomes a pinned color. The eyedropper ends after one pick (Shift+click keeps it on); Esc, right-click or pressing + again stops it. Empty (transparent) cells and already pinned colors are reported.
  • A pinned color changes only the pixels whose source cell color is closer to it than to their current color, adding one color; unpinning returns those pixels. A pinned swatch has a blue border and a blue triangle in the top-right corner (shown dimmed even when unused); right-click it to unpin, and "N pinned colors · Clear all" appears. Pinned colors are never excluded.

Pixels that 7. Clean up changed on purpose (outline fit, Thin outline to 1px, orphans, holes, L-shaped jaggies, frame flicker) are compared using that cleaned-up color instead of their source cell color. A pinned color therefore leaves them alone (it doesn't undo the cleanup toward the source), and excluding a color turns them into the remaining color nearest to the cleaned-up color. The color check line shows no pinned-color step for them either.

What carries over

SituationExcluded and pinned colors
New candidates and Auto optimize resultsApplied with the same post-processing. A color that shifted slightly with different settings still counts as the same within OKLab 0.03.
Apply · Add to new tabIncluded in the inserted result.
Saving a presetNot saved.
Replacing the sourceCleared.
Viewing a running tileCan't be changed until it finishes.

Presets and Clean up

A preset is a named, saved set of step settings. Choose and save presets in the preset bar at the top of the settings column, and press the green Clean up button to run the current values on the left as a candidate. Changing values or choosing a preset alone doesn't rebuild anything.

What a preset holds

A preset holds the settings of steps 1–8 (except pinned and excluded colors), each step's Optimize check and 0. Optimize criteria. The preset bar stays at the top while you scroll the settings. Presets are stored in the app settings, and the last settings and chosen preset are remembered.

The preset bar

ControlWhat it does
① Preset listChoosing one only loads its values on the left; nothing is rebuilt. With unsaved changes, choosing another preset asks whether to save (Cancel keeps the current one). New preset at the end makes a new preset from the current values; it joins the list when saved.
② SaveTurns red when the values on the left differ from the chosen preset and is disabled when they match. Click it to save the current values to the chosen preset.
③ RenameRenames the chosen preset (see the picture below).
④ CopySaves name copy 1 with the current values right after the chosen preset and selects it — 2, 3… if the name is taken; copying a copy just raises the number on the original name. The copy starts with the Auto optimize check off.
⑤ DeleteThe red button. Deletes the chosen preset (the last one can't be deleted).
⑥ Clean upThe green play icon. See Clean up below.

Rename asks for ⑦ the preset name. If another preset already has that name (ignoring case), it says so and asks again.

Clean up

⑥ Clean up runs the chosen preset once with the current values on the left and replaces only its tile in the candidate row. Other candidates stay; if no tile has that name, a new one is added at the front. A short yellow line circles the running tile's border.

CaseBehavior
Some steps have Optimize onOnly those steps are auto-optimized, starting from the values shown, with the budget and scoring of 0. Optimize criteria.
No step has Optimize onCleans up once as set (the tile shows As set (score)).
A saved preset as isThe values found are written straight into that preset; if it's open, the values on the left follow while it runs, and Revert reverts the preset too.
Unsaved editsRuns as a preset name (modified) tile and leaves the preset unchanged. An unsaved new preset uses its name; with no preset chosen it's Current settings.

Built-in presets

A fresh install has the presets below, in this list order; the candidate row shows checked presets in the same order. All can be edited and deleted, and Reset in the Auto Optimize Settings window restores them. Every built-in preset starts with Keep background and no dithering.

PresetCharacterOptimized stepsChecked at firstBudget (trials · s)
Defaultk-means palette (automatic color count); the outline is fitted to 1 px by shrinking.NoneYes2000 · 300
Same hue familyk-means with 32 colors (average colors) and a high hue weight so shades of one color stay together; median cell color; outline fit off. The initial main preset for AI drawing.AllYes10000 · 300
Small sprite (32px)Fits the result to a fixed 32×32 size.NoneNo2000 · 300
Fewer colors (16)k-means with 16 colors, target colors 16.NoneNo2000 · 300
Keep original colorsKeeps the cell colors without reducing to a palette.NoneNo2000 · 300
OKLabOKLCH grouping palette (average colors) with OKLab color distance.AllYes10000 · 300
Quick checkOKLCH grouping, a short budget for a quick look.Pixel grid · Cell color · CleanupNo1000 · 30
Faithful to sourceKeeps original colors with mean cell color; the score doubles the weight of Fidelity and Major color shift.AllYes10000 · 300
Clean pixelsOKLCH grouping, center-pixel cell color, CIEDE2000 distance; the score weighs orphans, outline gaps and 1-px outline more.AllYes10000 · 300
  • Built-in presets start with a 300-second time limit (Quick check: 30 seconds). A built-in preset still at the old 120 seconds is moved to 300 seconds, but other times you set and your own presets are left as they are.
  • Settings that start without a preset have an initial budget of 2000 trials and 120 seconds. New presets and copies take whatever budget is shown on the left at that moment.

On the bundled sample (a 4-frame walk cycle, 2172×724 px) the outline is 16.3 px thick (color edges 8.25 px, block edges 15.5 px). The default By shrinking gives cells of about 16 px, about 29×35 per frame, about 24 colors and about 99% of outer outline pixels 1 px thick (with local cell correction; about 96% without). Thin outline only with pixel size from Color edges gives cells of about 8 px with about 99% at 1 px.

Pipeline settings

The settings that shape the result are in the settings column on the left of the window, step by step. The column starts collapsed to a thin vertical bar so the preview and candidates get the room. Changing a value doesn't rebuild anything; press Clean up to see the result of your changes.

  • Click ① the bar (settings icon and arrow) to slide the column open, and again to collapse it; the state is remembered.
  • The preset bar stays at the top while you scroll, followed by the steps in order. Each step can be collapsed or expanded.
  • Steps with an Optimize check in their header (1, 3, 5, 6, 7) decide whether Clean up and Auto optimize may vary that step's values (Score and optimize criteria).

Steps at a glance

StepWhat it doesChange it when
0. Optimize criteriaBudget (trials, seconds) and scoring rules for Clean up and Auto optimize.Optimization takes too long or stops too early, or the score prefers results that look worse.
Quick settingsGathers the often-changed output size and One palette for all frames (expanded at first). The same settings as 4. Output size and 6. Palette.You want a given output size, or want frames to share colors or not.
1. BackgroundMakes the background transparent (Keep background at first).The source has a solid background that stays in the result, or a white sticker border.
2. FramesSplits a sprite sheet into frames.Frames merge or split in the wrong place.
3. Pixel gridFinds the size of one source "pixel" (the cell size) and the cell boundaries.The result is far too large or small, or cell lines miss the color blocks.
4. Output sizeSets the output size from the cell size, a fixed size or a factor.You need a specific size such as 32×32.
5. Cell colorPicks one color per cell.Blurred edge colors leak into the result.
6. PaletteMerges similar colors into a palette and maps each cell to a palette color.There are too many or too few colors, shading turns into another hue, or you must use a given palette.
7. CleanupFixes orphan pixels and holes and fits the outline to 1 px.The outline is thick or broken, or dots such as pupils disappear.
8. Frame consistencyMatches pixels that differ slightly from the neighboring frames to reduce flicker.The animation flickers.
OutputHow the result is inserted: new layer or new document.You want another destination or frame duration.
AI (experimental)Asks the AI chat to touch up the result or rank candidates after applying.You want AI help.

Quick settings

Quick settings sits right below 0. Optimize criteria and starts expanded. Its values always change together with the same settings in the steps below and, like other settings, take effect when you press Clean up.

  • ② In the output size row, with Auto on (default) the output size follows the cell size (or the factor in 4. Output size) and the width × height boxes show the selected result's size in grey. Turning it off switches to a fixed size, starting from the current result size, and the result is fitted to the width × height you enter.
  • With the link button (keep aspect) on, changing the width updates the height (and vice versa) by the current result's ratio, and the sprite keeps its ratio when fitted.
  • In the target colors row, the values are the same as Target colors, Auto and Strict in 0. Optimize criteria. A result with more colors than this loses score, so Clean up and Auto optimize favor results around this color count. With Strict on, a result with fewer colors also loses score by how far it is from the target, so the count lands closer to the target. With Auto on (default) the color count isn't scored (the number and Strict are disabled). It doesn't set the palette size itself; if you need exactly that many colors, set the color count in 6. Palette.
  • ③ One palette for all frames: on converts all frames with one palette so they share colors; off picks a palette per frame. Like the same check in 6. Palette, it applies only to palette methods that extract colors from the image and is disabled otherwise.

1. Background

④ 1. Background starts at Keep background, as do all built-in presets. To remove the background, turn on the eraser button under the source or choose a mode.

SettingDetails
ModeChooses how the background is found. The values and what they do are in the table below.
ToleranceColors within this distance of the background color count as background (default 24). Auto picks it from the image.
Only background connected to the borderOn by default: only background connected to the image border is removed, so the same color inside the sprite (eyes, near-black outlines touching the background) is kept.
Use existing transparencyOn by default: transparency already in the source is used as is.
Alpha thresholdPixels with alpha (opacity) below this value (1–255, default 128) count as transparent. Lower it to keep semi-transparent shadows or glows; raise it to drop a hazy semi-transparent fringe.
Specks (px)After background removal, detached pieces (8-way connected) smaller than this many pixels (default 24) are removed as background; 0 turns it off. Lower it if small detached details such as stars or sparks disappear.
ModeResultUse it when
Keep backgroundConverts without removing anything (the initial value). Only pixels already transparent in the source stay transparent.The source is already a transparent PNG, or the background is part of the picture, as with floor tiles or a scene.
Auto (border color)Takes the most common color on the image border as the background color and removes colors within Tolerance of it. If the border is mostly transparent, the source's own transparency is used.The AI drew the character on a solid backdrop such as white or light green.
Pick colorTakes the color chosen with the color button next to it as the background color and removes colors within Tolerance of it.The sprite touches the border so Auto picks the wrong color, or you know exactly what the backdrop color is.
  • ⑥ Remove sticker border (on by default): finds the white band around the outline (a sticker border) and removes it with the background. It acts only when most of the edge is light and colorless, and undoes itself if the new edge isn't dark. Optimizing the Background step tries it on and off.
  • ⑦ Keep background (one color): keeps the detected background, flattened to the single background color (the result has that background too, and the whole image is one frame). Only matters for a background found by color.
  • The one-pixel blended fringe between a solid background and the sprite is removed too.

2. Frames

⑤ 2. Frames splits a sheet with several frames into one frame each. All frames get the same canvas size (Same canvas size for all frames).

ModeHow it splits
Single imageUses the whole image as one frame. Choose it for an image with a single character or a single scene.
Auto (split by empty space)Splits by fully empty rows and columns. Small detached pieces such as effects (Merge small pieces (%), default 12%) are merged into the nearest frame within Distance (px) (default 48). Sprites that touch or overlap sideways, with no empty column between them, end up as one piece much wider than the others and are parted at the thinnest vertical line. Choose it for a sheet with empty space between frames but uneven spacing.
GridSplits into Columns × Rows cells (1–64 each). The frame boxes are set by connected blob rather than by cutting pixels along the cell lines, so hands and feet crossing a line go into their own frame's box instead of leaving fragments at the edge of the next frame. Two touching sprites are parted at the thinnest spot near the line. Choose it for a sheet laid out at even spacing, or one whose frames touch with no empty line between them.
  • Align: where each split frame is placed on the canvas all frames share, so frames of different sizes line up by this rule.
AlignPlacementUse it when
Bottom centerPuts the bottom of each frame's sprite on the bottom of the canvas, centered horizontally (default).Character animations with feet on the ground, such as walking or running. Even when frames differ in height the feet stay at the same level, so playback doesn't bob up and down.
CenterPuts each frame's sprite in the middle of the canvas.Effects such as explosions or spells, or icons: art with no ground line that grows or spins around its center.
Keep offsetsKeeps each sprite's original position inside its split area (the strip for Auto, the grid cell for Grid).Animations where the position inside the sheet is the motion itself, such as a jump or a throw; bottom center would flatten that height change.
  • Frames are split once with the current method when a source is loaded or replaced; after that, changing split values or running Clean up or Auto optimize keeps the last applied boundaries. Press Apply frame split to split again with the new values (candidates are rebuilt and a running optimization is discarded).
  • A frame contains only the pixels inside its blue box in the source view. Specks outside the box and hands or feet reaching past it are left out of both the result and the source preview; if you need them, drag the box larger in the source view (Source and preview). Loading an animated image sets this step to the frame grid (Opening the window and loading a source).

Pixel grid and output size

3. Pixel grid finds how many source pixels make one "pixel" (the cell size) and where the cell boundaries lie; 4. Output size decides how many pixels the result has. Look at these two steps when the result is far too large or small, or when the cell lines in the source view miss the color blocks.

Grid detection

With Mode set to Auto (edge analysis) in ① 3. Pixel grid, the source is analyzed to find the cell size. Sizes from 3 to 256 px are measured, so blocks enlarged past 100 px are found at their real size too. Cell size and boundaries are always found per frame.

ModeWhat it does
Auto (edge analysis)Analyzes the source and finds the cell size and cell lines per frame (default). It follows the edges even when cells vary between 7 and 9 px, and Pixel size from below decides which measurement sets the size. Enough for most AI images.
ManualUses a straight grid with the cell size and start X/Y you set (see the manual grid table below). Choose it when Auto finds double or half the real cell size, or when you know the scale the pixel art was enlarged by. On AI images with uneven cells a straight grid tends to drift off the edges in places.
Pixel size from valueWhat it measures
Auto (best edge match)Uses block edges when the block spacing is clear; otherwise fits cell lines for each of the three values and picks the one that best matches the source's strong edges. The match subtracts what the same edges would match by chance if shuffled (bigger cells match more by chance). On a near tie the order is outline → block edges → color edges, and a value 2× or 3× the pick wins if it matches almost as well.
Outline thicknessMeasures the dark band touching transparency in several places horizontally and vertically, and picks the size that makes the outline 1 px.
Color edgesEstimates the cell size as a decimal from run lengths between color changes; tolerant to jitter, preferring the finer of two harmonics.
Block edgesIgnoring weak texture such as paper grain (only where neighboring pixels differ by more than the image's noise in OKLab), picks the spacing at which strong color edges line up within a row. It prefers the larger cell even with half-cell blocks mixed in, and places the cell lines on those strong edges.
  • Pixel size from is used when cleanup's Fit outline to 1 px is Thin outline only, Redraw as vector or Off, and is disabled with By shrinking. By shrinking only picks values within ±12% of the outline thickness — unless the outline is thinner than a cell (such as a blurred 1-px border) and a larger value clearly fits better, in which case it shrinks to that size.
  • A clean image with 256 colors or fewer uses its exact integer scale (2–256), and an image already at 1 px per pixel (including large fills with only a 1-px outline) keeps 1. Noisy or JPEG images with more than 256 colors are also found at 1 px or 2 px cells when they are native-size or 2× pixel art.
  • Then the cell lines are fitted onto the real edges even when cells vary between 7 and 9 px. Allowed size jitter (%) (default 15%) is how far a cell may differ from the average.
  • The info line under the settings shows the detected size, offset and edge fit %, plus the three estimates and the pick with its reason, such as "Color edges 5.75px (3%) · outline 24.75px (9%) · block edges 19px (8%) → Outline thickness (edge match 9%)". With Thin outline only or Redraw as vector, an outline that is nearly an integer multiple (2× or more) of the cell size adds a note that the outline is drawn several pixels thick.

Manual grid and grid-first optimization

SettingDetails
Manual · Pixel size (px) · Offset X·YEnter the cell size and start X/Y yourself. Use detected as manual copies the detected values into the manual fields. A manual grid wins over By shrinking in Fit outline to 1 px.
Add to auto pixel size (px)A value added to the cell size the automatic grid finds (Auto optimize changes the cell size through it).
Optimize grid fit firstWhen on, Clean up and Auto optimize first vary only the grid values (cell size, size jitter, pixel size source and local correction; start X/Y for a manual grid) to find the grid with the best cell split score, then keep that grid while optimizing the other steps that have Optimize on. With other steps optimized too, the grid stage uses up to half the trials and time; while on, step 3's header Optimize is disabled (this check replaces it). Saved with the preset.
Preview cellsAdds a tile of that name at the front of the candidate row, selects it and shows its cell lines over the source (turning on Show grid). With Optimize grid fit first on it runs only the grid stage; otherwise it runs once with the values shown. The grid values found are loaded on the left but not written to the preset.

The cell split score looks only at the source and the cell lines: the average of Cell edge fit (higher when the lines miss none of the source's strong edges and lie on them — cells smaller or larger than the real ones both score low) and Cell color purity (higher when each cell holds only similar colors). Add to auto pixel size (px) and the manual grid's start X/Y depend on the image, so Clean up uses them but they aren't saved in presets or for the next session (changing only them doesn't turn the save button red).

Local cell correction

③ Local cell correction (on by default) re-fits the image-wide cell lines per band to that band's strong block edges. It follows art that is drawn half a cell off in places; within a band the lines stay straight so cells are rectangles, and the lines bend where bands change.

SettingRange · defaultMeaning
Max shift (% of cell)5–99%, default 35%Maximum distance a line may move from its image-wide position. Neighboring cell widths stay within 70–130% of the original, so actual movement can be smaller.
Band (cells)1–32 cells, default 2Width of each band fitted separately. Smaller follows more closely (1 = every row), larger is smoother.
Minimum cell size (px)1–16 px, default 4 pxThe average cell size (in source pixels) from which correction applies. Lower values also adjust small cells but may follow blurred-edge noise.
  • Each band counts color edges that continue over several rows, drops 1–2 px noise, and weights high-contrast edges more, so sharp continuous edges are matched first. Two sharp edges within one cell (e.g. two black-to-skin edges 5 px apart) can't both be matched by one line.
  • Even when on, the grid stays straight if the average cell is below the minimum or no line moved. The selected-result line shows whether it applied, such as "Local correction applied to n/m frames". With step 3's Optimize on, Clean up and Auto optimize also try it on and off with different max shift and band values.

4. Output size

② 4. Output size decides how many pixels the result has. Auto next to the mode is the same setting as the output size Auto in Quick settings (off = fit a fixed size, on = the last automatic mode).

ModeOutput size
From pixel sizeEach detected cell becomes one result pixel (default). It keeps the pixel count the source art was drawn at, so it is the most faithful choice when no size is required.
Fit a fixed sizeFits Width×Height (e.g. 32, 48, 64), with Keep aspect and Padding (px). The source sprite is divided into the cell count left after padding: at 64×64 with padding 1, the cell size is chosen so the long side has 62 cells, splitting or merging cells. If the detected cell size is within 2% of that, the detected lines are kept; cells are never smaller than one source pixel. Use it when the game needs a set size, such as a 32×32 character.
Scale factorMultiplies the source size by a factor (e.g. 0.125), using the cell lines found per frame. Use it when you know the enlargement (0.125 for 8×) or want several images reduced by the same ratio.

Cell color and palette

5. Cell color picks one representative color per source cell, and 6. Palette groups those colors into a few and maps each cell to a palette color. Look at these steps when the result has too many or too few colors, when shading turns into an odd hue, or when you must use a given palette.

5. Cell color

The Mode of ① 5. Cell color is how one color is taken from the source pixels in a cell. Cell edges carry blur from neighbors, so Ignore cell border (%) (default 20%) is left out.

MethodColor picked
Most frequent colorThe most frequent color after lightly snapping similar colors by Color snap step (default 20). Default. The safest choice for AI images with even fills and a little noise or bleed in each cell; the result stays close to colors that really occur in the source.
MedianThe per-channel median. Red, green and blue each take their middle value, so a few stray noise pixels don't move it; but since the channels are picked separately, a cell split half-and-half between two colors can get a color that isn't in the source.
Center pixelThe center pixel of the cell. The simplest choice and blind to blur at the cell edges, but a JPEG blotch or noise pixel in the middle comes straight through. Suits clean, noise-free enlarged pixel art.
MeanThe mean; cells on an edge can get an in-between mix of two colors. Natural for soft gradients, but it tends to leave muddy in-between dots between outline and fill (see the figure below).
Center-weighted meanA mean that counts pixels near the cell center more (transparent pixels left out). Less affected by edge blur than Mean, but a cell whose middle an edge crosses still gets a mixed color.
Dominant-color weighted meanGroups similar colors and takes a center-weighted average only inside the group that holds the cell center most, so different fills such as skin and black clothing aren't mixed. Uses the color snap step. Choose it for small sprites where many cells straddle an edge, or when Most frequent color gets pulled toward another fill in a cell corner.
SettingDetails
Ignore cell border (%)This share of each cell's border (0–45%, default 20%) is ignored when picking its color, because neighbor colors bleed into it. Raise it for wide blur; lower it when cells are small (3–4 px) and too few pixels remain.
Transparency threshold (%)A cell becomes transparent when less than this share (default 50%) of its inner pixels is painted (not background). Raising it drops half-covered edge cells so the sprite gets one cell thinner; lowering it paints barely covered cells too, so the sprite gets plumper.
Color snap stepHow far apart (in RGB channel units, default 20) colors can be and still count as one color for Most frequent color and Dominant-color weighted mean. Larger values gather a fill with JPEG noise into one color; too large merges two close shading steps. Other methods don't use it.

Most frequent color and Dominant-color weighted mean pick the fill that covers most of each cell (skin, hair, iris blue) in all six. Center pixel grabs the hair strand and eye highlight that pass through the center, giving a dark and a white cell; Mean and Center-weighted mean turn cells that straddle two colors into muddy in-between colors that aren't in the source, and Median muddies only the cell with outline bled in.

6. Palette method

The Mode of ② 6. Palette is how the colors are reduced. New settings start with OKLCH grouping; each built-in preset uses its own method (Built-in presets).

At the same 11 colors, k-means keeps the hair's white highlight as its own color, while Median cut, OKLCH grouping and Distance grouping merge the highlight with pale skin. Keep original colors keeps every cell color, highlight included, but leaves hundreds of colors.

MethodHow it reducesColor count
OKLCH groupingColors whose lightness, chroma and hue differences are all within Group within L·C·H° (default 0.1 · 0.1 · 20) become one color. The color that groups the most pixels becomes a center first; a color within reach of several centers joins the nearest. Hue is ignored for near-gray colors. Because the three are limited separately, it keeps the lightness steps of one hue apart on characters with many shading steps, and the count fits the image without being set.Set by the range
Distance groupingGroups the same way, with differences measured by ③ the color distance within Group radius (default 0.04, 0.005–0.3). With CIEDE2000, colors that look alike are grouped, so a light gray joins the whites rather than an apricot of the same lightness. Choose it to tidy colors by "this different means a different color" instead of a count.Set by the radius
k-means (OKLab)Splits the colors into the chosen number of groups. It repeatedly adjusts the groups so each color is as close as possible to its group center in OKLab, which gives a balanced overall palette, but colors of small areas are easily absorbed by large ones (Keep distinct hues helps). Choose it when the color count is fixed, such as 16 colors.2–64 or Auto
Median cutGroups by repeatedly halving the color space: the RGB box is split again and again along its widest spread. It is fast, but because it splits in RGB, colors that look alike can end up apart and different ones together. Use it to compare against a k-means palette.2–64 or Auto
Existing paletteUses only the colors of the palette chosen in Palette, from the palette panel or Lospec favorites. Choose it when a game or project requires a set palette.That palette
Keep original colorsKeeps the cell colors without reducing. Use it to see the cell colors alone; slightly different colors remain per cell, so there can be hundreds of colors.As many as cells have
  • Automatic color count picks the elbow of the error curve, then adds colors until small but noticeable ones (such as a red scarf) survive. The two grouping methods get their count from the range or radius, so Colors and Auto are disabled.
  • Don't optimize next to the method (only when the Palette step's Optimize is on; saved with the preset) makes optimization keep the method and vary only its other values (color count, grouping range and so on).
  • With One palette for all frames (on by default), all frames use the same palette. The same check is also in Quick settings.

Palette colors and edge cells

SettingDetails
Palette colorsThe color each group uses with k-means, median cut and the two grouping methods. What each value does is in the table below.
Edge cell weightWith original colors, edge cells touching transparency and blurred cells between two neighbor colors (including between outline and fill) count only this much when grouping colors and choosing the automatic count (0–1, default 0.25).
Snap near-black to one outline colorOn by default: near-black colors at or below the lightness next to it (default 0.24) are merged into one outline color (the most used of them).
Palette colors valueResult
Original colorsEach group takes the one cell color actually used most among its members (default). Every result color really occurs in the source, so blurred edge colors can't pull it: a white robe stays white and a gold button stays a crisp gold. Cells touching transparency and blurred cells count only by Edge cell weight.
Average colorsEach group uses the average of its colors (the old behavior). Blurred edge colors get mixed in, so light colors can come out a little darker and strong ones a little duller, but on fills that really are smooth gradients it lands on the middle tone.

Color distance

③ Color distance is how closeness between two colors is measured. It is used when cells take a palette color (without dithering), for pinned colors, for the replacement of excluded colors, for picking original colors and by Distance grouping. The score uses a separate scoring color distance.

OKLab weighs lightness, chroma and hue equally, so the middle of the grey strip turns skin and apricot, dark gold turns green and red, and dark ends go navy instead of black. CIEDE2000 keeps the greys cleanly grey, navy and black and holds gold almost to the dark end; OKLCh hue-weighted counts hue differences double, so greys stay in the grey family and gold shading stays among warm colors (apricot, red). Pictures with many greys, metals or dark shading do better with CIEDE2000 or OKLCh hue-weighted; for evenly spread colors OKLab is enough.

DistanceCharacter
OKLCh hue-weightedDefault. Splits the OKLab difference into lightness (L), chroma (C, how intense a color is) and hue (H, which color it is: red, yellow, blue and so on), and counts only the hue part more, by the hue weight next to it (default 2). A palette color of the same hue therefore wins even if its lightness is a bit off, so deep apricot hand shading doesn't turn into a gold or red-cloth color of similar lightness. The hue difference is scaled by chroma, so greys, whites and blacks are barely affected. Suits characters where different hues of similar lightness sit side by side, such as skin, hair and clothes.
CIEDE2000The industry-standard color difference formula (ΔE00) used in print and displays, measured in CIELAB (divided by 200 to match the OKLab scale). Differences the eye is dull to (chroma between very saturated colors) count less, sensitive ones (between near-grey colors) count more, and a correction for hue shifts around blue and purple is built in. It separates the fine steps of silver armor or white clothes and the shading of blue clothes well, and doesn't treat a hue change (apricot → gold) as close. It is heavier to compute, so cleanup gets a little slower.
OKLabOKLab is a color space built so that colors the same number apart look about equally different (perceptually uniform: around 0.02 is a barely visible difference whether the colors are dark or light, red or blue). This takes the straight (Euclidean) distance between two colors in it, weighing lightness, chroma and hue equally (the old behavior). Fastest and most predictable, but at the same distance it can't tell one shading step from a neighboring hue, so shading can slip into the next hue. Suits images with few, clearly different colors.
Hue weight (number box next to it)The factor on hue differences for OKLCh hue-weighted (0–1000, default 2). 0 ignores hue, 1 equals OKLab, 2–5 prefer the same hue family, and a very large value picks the closest hue regardless of lightness and chroma. Other distances don't use it.

k-means and OKLCH grouping always use OKLab, and Bayer dithering compares the light-linear mix of two palette colors with the source in OKLab.

Keeping shades and hues

  • ④ Prefer shade family (on by default): if a palette color has a similar hue and chroma to the cell color (a lighter or darker shade of the same color), the cell takes the family member with the nearest lightness instead of a different-hue color of similar lightness. Dithering never mixes in a chromatic color of a different hue either, so deep apricot hand shading doesn't turn gold or get gold dots.
  • Keep distinct hues (on by default): if a source color group with enough chroma (Min chroma, default 0.04) and area (Min area, default 4 cells) would turn into a palette color whose hue differs by at least Hue diff (°) (default 20°) — e.g. small apricot ear shading → gold — that color is put in the palette: added with an automatic count, or swapped for the least important color with a fixed count (up to ¼ of the palette; pinned colors stay). Blurred in-between colors between two palette colors aren't added.
  • Optimizing the Palette step tries both on and off.

Dithering

Dither is None (default) · Bayer 2×2 · Bayer 4×4; a checker pattern of two palette colors makes the color in between (the same patterns as Converting colors to a palette). A pattern is used only when the mix is closer to the source than the single nearest color even after adding how noticeable the pattern is, and colors within a barely visible difference (OKLab 0.02) of a palette color aren't dithered.

None breaks shading into flat areas; Bayer 2×2 alternates two colors in a coarse checker, and Bayer 4×4 has more mixing steps, so the shirt and hair gradients continue in finer patterns.

DitherResult
NoneEach cell takes just the nearest palette color (default). Fills stay clean and edges crisp, which suits most characters and icons, but in-between colors missing from the palette snap to a neighbor, so smooth gradients break into steps.
Bayer 2×2Makes a color missing from the palette with a 2×2 pattern of two palette colors (5 mixing levels). The tight pattern is less distracting on small sprites, but there are few in-between steps.
Bayer 4×4The same with a 4×4 pattern (17 mixing levels). Wide gradients such as skies, glows and rounded shading blend more smoothly with a retro-console feel, but on small sprites the pattern can look like noise.

Don't optimize next to dithering (on by default) keeps optimization from changing it: a dither pattern's average color is close to the source, so it tends to raise the score even though zooming in shows a checkerboard or stray colored dots.

Cleanup and outlines

7. Cleanup fixes orphan pixels and holes in the palette-mapped result and fits the outline to 1 px. 8. Frame consistency reduces flicker between animation frames.

7. Cleanup

The items turned on in ① 7. Cleanup apply in the order of the table below (orphans → holes → outline thinning → outline → L corners); Redraw as vector draws its new line before all of them.

SettingInitial valueWhat it does
Remove orphan pixels · Max contrastOn · 0.18A pixel with no same-colored pixel among its 8 neighbors takes the most common neighbor color. Dots that contrast more than Max contrast (OKLab distance) with their neighbors, such as pupils, are kept; 1 removes them all.
Fill 1-px holesOnFills a transparent pixel painted on all four sides with the most common of those four colors.
Fit outline to 1 pxBy shrinkingHow the outer outline is made 1 px; see the table below.
Vector simplify (px) · Keep small blobs (cells)0.6 · 6Used only by Redraw as vector. Simplify is how far (in result pixels) the outline may be straightened — larger is smoother but follows the shape less, 0 follows the center line exactly; dark shapes not touching the outer outline that are smaller than this many cells (such as eyes) are left as they are.
OutlineAs isHow the dark pixels on the edge (touching transparency) are tidied. What each value does is in the table below.
Reduce L-corner doubles on outlinesOffReplaces doubled pixels at L-shaped outline corners with the inner color.

As is keeps the brown outline on the left leg and the missing outline on the right leg. Unify changes only the dark edge pixels to the single darkest color, so the gap stays open; Ensure closed additionally wraps a 1-px darkest-color outline around edges that had none, closing everything.

OutlineResultUse it when
As isLeaves the outline alone (the initial value). Colors stay as the palette and cleanup made them, so black, dark brown and dark navy may stay mixed along the outline.The art uses colored outlines on purpose, or has no outline at all.
Unify to the darkest colorTurns the dark pixels on the edge touching transparency (up to 0.1 lighter than the outline lightness in 6. Palette, default 0.24) into the single darkest color. The sprite's size and shape stay the same.The AI drew the outline in a mix of black, dark brown and dark navy, so it looks blotchy.
Ensure a closed 1-px outlineFirst unifies to the darkest color, then adds that color to every transparent pixel directly beside (up, down, left, right) an edge pixel that isn't outline, so the whole silhouette is closed. The sprite grows outward by one pixel where it was added.Light fills touch the background directly so the outline has gaps, or the character must stand out on any background.

Fit outline to 1 px

② Fit outline to 1 px is how an AI image's thick outline becomes a pixel-art-style 1-px outline. The score's 1-px outline term measures the result.

ModeWhat it doesUse it when
By shrinkingDefault. Sets the cell size to the outer outline thickness, so the whole sprite scales down and the outline becomes 1 px; leftover doubled pixels get one light thinning pass. It may move only to a larger value that clearly fits the edges better, never to a smaller one such as half the thickness. The thickness is measured at outline darkness 0.24 even if you lower it. A manual grid wins.The outline is drawn as thick as one source cell.
Thin outline onlyKeeps the grid's cell size and peels up to 3 outline layers doubled inward, without breaking the line; inner dark shapes such as eyes stay.The outline is drawn two or more cells thick.
Redraw as vectorKeeps the grid's cell size, traces the center line of the outline band touching transparency in the background-removed source, simplifies it and redraws it on the real cell lines as a 1-px pixel-perfect line. Old outline cells not on the new line become transparent outside or take the nearby inner color inside, so no double outline remains.The outline is bumpy or thick in places.
OffDoesn't fit the outline.You want the source outline thickness as is, or inner black cells must not turn light.

8. Frame consistency

Turning on Match neighbor frames (reduce flicker) (off by default) in ③ 8. Frame consistency makes a pixel that differs only slightly from identical previous and next frames take their color. Pixels that differ by more than Max contrast next to it (default 0.12) are treated as intended changes and kept.

Auto optimize

Auto optimize starts from each checked preset's settings, varies the values of the steps that have Optimize on, keeps the highest-scoring settings, and shows the best one per preset as a candidate. Use it to find a better result without adjusting settings one by one.

Starting and reading the result

PartWhat it does
① Auto optimizeThe button above the candidates; starts right away. Disabled without a source or a checked preset.
② Settings buttonSlider icon. Opens the Auto Optimize Settings window where you choose the presets to run.
