③ Best-score graphPlots the best score so far after each trial, with faint vertical lines where a new pass starts and the best score at the bottom right. After the run it shows the selected candidate's run, and hides for a candidate that wasn't optimized.
④ Continue optimizingYellow refresh-icon button, shown only when the selected candidate stopped because it used up its trial or time budget (Trial limit or Time limit). It searches again with the same preset from that candidate's best settings, with a fresh trial and time budget, and replaces only that tile. Earlier trials aren't reused, and other candidates stay.
⑤ RevertA small button shown after a run. Restores the settings from before the run, including presets the optimizer wrote values into.
⑥ One-line summary"n/m presets · t s", or for a single preset "name: k passes · a → b · n trials · t s · reason".
⑦ Blue line on the tileWhy it stopped and the candidate's score: Optimized (87.6) when it stopped improving, Time limit when the time budget ran out, Trial limit when the trial budget ran out, Cancelled when cancelled.

When it finishes, the candidate row has one best tile per checked preset, and the open preset's tile (or the first tile, or the one you selected during the run) is selected with its settings loaded on the left. For presets run as saved, the values found are written straight into each preset.

While it runs

  • While running, the button turns into the red ⑧ Cancel, and ⑨ "name (i/n) · pass p · trial n/budget · best s" appears next to the graph. With a budget of 0 (no limit), the budget reads unlimited.
  • The candidate row turns into the presets to run: ⑩ a yellow line circles the running tile's border and the tile shows Optimizing (best score) with the best picture so far, ⑪ tiles waiting their turn show Waiting, and each fills with its result as its preset finishes.
  • ⑧ Cancel stops only the running preset, leaving its best so far in its tile (Cancelled (score)), and moves on to the next (stopped before the first trial, the tile is dropped). To stop the remaining presets too, right-click the cancel button > Cancel all. Cancelling everything without results restores the previous candidate tiles.
  • Replacing the source or applying the frame split discards a running optimization (closing the window only hides it, so it keeps going). Pinned and excluded colors don't rebuild anything, so changing them leaves the preset tiles in place.

How it searches and when it stops

PhaseWhat happens
Coarse sweepTries each item coarsely: 7 points across the range for numbers, every value for choices.
ClimbingFrom a few top spots, makes small changes to climb higher, shrinking the step when nothing improves.
Restarts (passes)When a pass stops improving, another starts from the best so far — also from the top result most different from it that hasn't been a start yet, and from slightly perturbed best settings, so it doesn't get stuck. A pass that gains less than 0.1 doesn't stop it at once: up to three more passes run from other top results and stronger perturbations first.
  • Settings already tried aren't rerun, and background removal and frame splitting run only once, so trials run fast on several threads. The same image and settings always give the same result when the run ends by trial count rather than time.
  • Each preset stops at whichever of Trials and Seconds in its 0. Optimize criteria comes first (a budget for all its passes together). 0 means no limit; with both at 0 it runs until it stops improving, and you can stop it with the cancel button.
  • Which values the optimizer varies is set by the Optimize checks in the step headers (Score and optimize criteria).

On the bundled sample (default settings with the former default items and budget — pixel size, color count, cell color method, cleanup and palette colors; 200 trials, 60 s) it took about 3 s and 162 trials to go from 70.3 to 80.8 (cell about 16.1 → 14.9 px, 26 colors, hole filling off, palette colors kept original, 99% of the outline 1 px).

Auto Optimize Settings window

Opened with the settings button (slider icon) next to Auto optimize. Choose the presets Auto optimize runs and the main preset for AI drawing, and view the last trial history. Preset values themselves are edited in the settings column on the left. Choices can't change while a run is going, and they are saved when you close the window.

ItemWhat it does
① Preset checklistChecked presets run in Auto optimize and get a place in the candidate row (one best candidate per preset, named after it). The same checks decide which Not run yet tiles appear when a source is loaded and which presets run once as set when the frame split is applied.
② MainChooses the main preset used to pixelate images generated by AI drawing (only the chosen one is green; independent of the checks; initially Same hue family). Generated images are converted with that preset's saved step settings as they are (background, palette, output size and alignment); only the frame split follows the number of generated frames (a single image isn't split). The drawing settings Result-size correction (the output size from the chat or settings) and Remove background override the preset only when on, and both are off by default. With Auto-optimize the conversion on, the conversion is optimized with the preset's per-step optimize and optimize criteria; off converts once with those settings.
③ Trial historyExpand it to see the selected preset's last run: the top 50 trials by score and the values they changed (rank · score · settings; the start settings appear as (start settings)).
④ Auto optimize on loadOff by default; remembered. When off, loading a source only prepares it and runs nothing (checked presets show as Not run yet tiles). When on, every time a source comes in (open, drop, paste, browse, replace) all checked presets are auto-optimized.
⑤ ResetThe red button. After confirmation, restores the preset list, checks, main preset, preset values (including values written by optimization) and Auto optimize on load to a fresh install, and deletes presets you created, unsaved new presets and the trial history. It can't be undone.

The Presets tab in Preferences can also bring back deleted built-in presets and reset edited ones to their original values (your own presets stay).

Score and optimize criteria

The score on each candidate (0–100) is computed without AI as a weighted sum of score terms. Clean up and Auto optimize look for the settings with the highest score. The scoring rules and optimization budget are set per preset in 0. Optimize criteria, and each candidate is scored by the criteria of the preset that made it.

Score terms

TermWhat it measuresDefault weight
FidelityHow closely the result matches the background-removed source cells: (opacity mismatches + structure loss inside cells + color difference) ÷ samples. Structure loss is how far the source pixels inside a cell scatter from the cell average — large in cells that cut a sharp edge in half, regardless of the result color. A higher weight favors results that follow the source's colors and shapes more closely (smaller cells, more colors); a lower one favors simpler results.4
Orphan pixelsShare of orphan pixels with no same-colored pixel among their 8 neighbors (worst at 5% of opaque pixels). A higher weight favors clean results without specks, but penalizes art with many deliberate single dots such as pupils or sparkles.1
Outline gapsShare of edge pixels that aren't outline (dark) colors. A higher weight favors results with an unbroken outline; set it to 0 for art drawn without outlines.0.5
Color countHow far the color count is from the target. Unlike the others, it multiplies the whole score down (see Target colors below). With the target color count on Auto this weight does nothing.0.5
Frame consistencyHow differently colors are used between frames. Nothing to measure with a single frame; in an animation a higher weight favors results whose colors flicker less from frame to frame.1
Grid fitHow much the grid disagrees with color changes. The closer the cell lines sit between the source's color blocks, the better; a higher weight favors results whose cell size and lines match the source exactly.2
1-px outlineShare of outer outline pixels that are only 1 px thick; doubled bands of 2 px or more count against it. Measured on the final placed pixels at outline darkness 0.24 even if you lower it, so leaving black outlines uncounted earns nothing. 1-px lines running inward from the outline aren't doubling. Lower it if a thick outline is part of the style.4
Major color shiftHow much a widely used source color (at least 1% of opaque pixels) turned into a different color as a whole (e.g. brown boots pulled toward gold). Measured with the scoring color distance (on the OKLab scale): up to 0.02 is fine; 0.10 is the worst. A higher weight favors results where large areas such as hair, clothes and boots keep their original hue.2
Block repetitionHow much the result is still an enlarged image: the share of edge cells (judged by each cell's average source color) that sit in a same-color 2×2 block. Close to 1 when cells are half the real size or smaller and the result is needlessly large; dithering can't hide it. Up to 65% is fine; 95% is the worst. Lowering it penalizes results cut finer (larger) than the source cells less.2

Among built-in presets, Faithful to source and Clean pixels use different weights (Built-in presets). Each term's value, badness % and weight for the selected candidate appear in the score breakdown under the preview and in the tile tooltip (Candidates and scores).

0. Optimize criteria

① 0. Optimize criteria is at the top of the settings column and is saved with the preset.

SettingDetails
TrialsThe most settings Clean up and Auto optimize try (0–10000). 0 means no trial limit; whichever of trials and seconds is reached first stops the run. More trials make better settings more likely but take longer. Type a value or choose one from the right-click presets list. Settings without a preset start at 2000 trials.
SecondsThe most time Clean up and Auto optimize spend on one preset (0–600 seconds). 0 means no time limit; with both trials and seconds at 0 it runs until it stops improving, and the cancel button stops it. Built-in presets start at 300 seconds (Quick check: 30), settings without a preset at 120 seconds.
Target colors · Auto · StrictIf the result has more colors than the target, the whole score is multiplied by (1 − Color count weight × colors over ÷ target): with weight 0.5 and target 16, 20 colors lose 12.5% and 24 colors 25%. Auto (default) ignores the color count; Strict also penalizes fewer colors than the target by the difference.
Fidelity · Center weightedHow Fidelity, the largest score term, measures the color difference between the result and the source, and how pixels inside a cell are weighted. Each value is explained in the How Fidelity compares table below.
Cell color · Group rangeWhich color a source cell counts as when comparing cell by cell, and how close colors must be to form one group. Each value is explained in the Representative cell color table below.
Scoring color distance (major shift, cell match)The color distance used to compare candidates' scores. Separate from the palette's conversion color distance, it measures every candidate with the same ruler and is used only by Major color shift and cell-by-cell Fidelity. Each value is explained in the Choosing the scoring color distance table below.
Score weights (0 = ignore)Each score term's weight (0–10); 0 ignores that term. What each term measures, and which results win when you change its weight, is in the Score terms table above.

How Fidelity compares

The Fidelity box decides how Fidelity compares result pixels with the source. The same result can lose or keep points for dither patterns or stray dots inside an outline depending on this value. It is a scoring rule, so optimization never changes it.

ValueHow it comparesSuits
Cell by cellCompares each result pixel with just its own source cell's color (see Representative cell color below) using the scoring color distance (default). Nothing is mixed with neighbors, so a stray colored dot inside an outline or a checkerboard dither pattern loses points cell by cell.Ordinary pixel-art characters where clean fills and outlines matter, and every cell must match its own source cell.
Blended with neighborsConverts the source cell averages and the result into light-linear lightness, red–green and blue–yellow channels, blurs lightness a little and color more with neighboring cells, and compares in OKLab (S-CIELAB style; always OKLab instead of the scoring distance). A dither pattern that blends into the source color from a distance counts as a match.Art that builds gradients with dithering, or has wide smooth areas such as skies and backgrounds; also for judging dithered results fairly.
Center weightedWhen on (default), pixels near the cell center weigh more when choosing the cell color (a pyramid that falls off toward the edges). Neighbor colors bleeding over the cell edge count less, so the cell color stays steady even when the source cells are slightly uneven. Off counts every pixel in the cell equally.Can be turned off for clean sources with little blur and exact cell edges.

Representative cell color

The Cell color box decides which single color a source cell counts as when comparing cell by cell. Source cells mix in blur, noise and neighboring fills, so the same result scores differently depending on what is taken as the cell's color. Used only with Cell by cell.

The cell split between outline and skin and the cell with outline bled into a corner become skin with Dominant group but a muddy brown with Cell average. So Dominant group accepts a result that paints that cell plain skin, while Cell average rewards a result that paints the muddy brown that isn't in the source. The plain skin cell is the same in both, so the choice only changes the score on cells that mix colors.

ValueThe source cell's colorSuits
Dominant groupGroups similar colors in the cell (within the group range) and takes the heaviest group's average (default). A few black outline pixels in the corner of a skin cell, or blurred edge colors, form small groups and drop out, so a result that paints the cell clean skin color counts as a match.Most AI pixel art with distinct outlines and fills.
Cell averageTakes the average of every pixel in the cell. A cell an outline crosses gets a muddy mix of skin and black as its reference, so the result loses a little whether it paints that cell skin or black.Soft painting where colors really blend inside each cell (gradients, watercolor style), so no single group stands out.
Group rangeHow different two colors can be and still form one group in Dominant group (OKLab distance, default 0.06). Smaller values split a single skin tone with JPEG noise into several groups, so the cell color wavers; larger values merge different fills such as skin and light shading. Optimize grid fit first's cell purity uses this value too.Raise it a little for noisy JPEGs; lower it a little for art with closely spaced shading steps.

Choosing the scoring color distance

A color distance is a ruler that turns how different two colors are into one number. The scoring color distance is used only for cell-by-cell Fidelity and Major color shift and never changes the result colors; it decides which differences count as worse mistakes among the same candidates. All three are on the OKLab scale, so the same thresholds apply (0.02 barely visible, 0.10 worst).

OKLab and OKLCh are color spaces built so that differences match what the eye sees. OKLab places a color at lightness L and two color axes, a (green ↔ red) and b (blue ↔ yellow), and measures the difference as the straight distance between two points. OKLCh writes the same color as lightness L, chroma C (how vivid) and hue H (the kind of color), so you can see along which axis and how far a color moved, like the three strips above. The L·C·H weights of OKLCh hue-weighted multiply the differences on these three axes — raising H counts a change of kind of color (third strip) as bigger than a change of lightness only (first strip).

The scoring color distance measures the color difference between source and result with the same yardstick that picks the "nearest color" in this picture. Under OKLab, apricot in the middle of the grey strip and green where dark gold was count as close, so such a result loses little score; under CIEDE2000 and OKLCh hue-weighted they are a change to another color and are penalized heavily. So for pictures with many greys, metals or shadows, or where kinds of color must not mix, scoring with CIEDE2000 or OKLCh hue-weighted is safer (the distance that picks palette colors is set separately by the color distance in 6. Palette).

DistanceHow it measuresSuits
OKLabOKLab is a color space built so that colors the same number apart look about equally different (perceptually uniform: around 0.02 is a barely visible difference whether the colors are dark or light, red or blue). This takes the straight (Euclidean) distance in it, weighing lightness, chroma and hue equally. Fastest and simplest.Images with few, clearly different colors, or when you want a neutral ruler that weighs no difference specially.
CIEDE2000The industry-standard color difference formula (ΔE00) used in print and displays, measured in CIELAB (divided by 200 to match the OKLab scale). Differences the eye is dull to (chroma between very saturated colors) count less, sensitive ones (between near-grey colors) count more, and a correction for hue shifts around blue and purple is built in. Heavier to compute, so scoring gets a little slower.Art with many grey steps such as silver armor, grey stone or white clothes, or with lots of blue such as blue clothes or a night sky.
OKLCh hue-weightedSplits the OKLab difference into lightness (L), chroma (C, intensity) and hue (H, which color: red, yellow, blue and so on) and adds them with the L, C and H weights below (default 1 · 1 · 2, so hue counts double). The hue difference is scaled by chroma, so greys barely change and only a saturated color turning into another hue is penalized heavily. For example, a skin-colored hand turning red-cloth colored counts as a big mistake even at similar lightness.Characters where different hues of similar lightness touch, such as skin, a red shirt and orange hair; for weeding out results whose shading slips into a neighboring hue.
L weightThe factor on lightness differences (0–1000, default 1). Raising it penalizes shading that became a step lighter or darker more; 0 ignores lightness. Used only with OKLCh hue-weighted; dimmed for the other distances.Raise it when the shading steps must stay exact.
C weightThe factor on chroma differences (default 1). Raising it penalizes colors that faded (vivid red → dull red) or grew more intense; 0 ignores chroma.Raise it to weed out results whose colors turn dull.
H weightThe factor on hue differences (default 2). 1 equals OKLab; higher values penalize a change to another hue (skin → orange, blue → purple) more. 0 ignores hue.Raise it to stop fills told apart only by hue (such as skin and red cloth) from merging.

Per-step Optimize

② The Optimize check is in the headers of 1. Background, 3. Pixel grid, 5. Cell color, 6. Palette and 7. Cleanup. Clean up and Auto optimize vary only the steps with it on, starting from the values shown; steps with it off use their values as set (saved with the preset).

StepValues tried
1. BackgroundRemove sticker border
3. Pixel gridCell size · size jitter · pixel size source · local cell correction (with max shift and band in trials that use it) · a manual grid's start X/Y
5. Cell colorCell color method · ignored cell border · transparency threshold · color snap step
6. PalettePalette method (all five, plus an existing palette when starting from one) · color count · grouping range · group radius · palette colors · edge cell weight · color distance · keep distinct hues · prefer shade family · dithering · snap outline color · outline darkness
7. CleanupCleanup on/off · outline fit mode · vector simplify · small blobs to keep
  • Values that belong to a mode change only in trials using that mode, and a palette method or dithering with Don't optimize on isn't changed.
  • The cell size isn't made smaller without reason. By shrinking searches only within ±15% of the source outline thickness; otherwise it scans the three estimates and their 2×, 3×, ½ and ⅓, going no lower than 0.75× the starting size or automatic estimate (smaller cells always improve Fidelity and would make the result needlessly large). An exact multiple of a clean image keeps its cell size.
  • An automatic grid changes the cell size through Add to auto pixel size (px), a manual grid through its cell size, a scale-factor output through the factor; a fixed size skips it. Optimize grid fit first finds the grid first, separately (Pixel grid and output size).

Output

Apply at the bottom of the window inserts the selected candidate where the settings column's Output step says, and Add to new tab always inserts it as a new document in a new tab. Both close the window afterwards. Excluded and pinned colors are included in what's inserted.

SettingWhat it does
① New layer in the current document (from the current frame)Inserts a new layer starting at the current frame, adding frames if needed. One undo step. Parts larger than the canvas or outside it aren't cut; they stay as pixels outside the canvas (enlarge the canvas in canvas size mode to see them — to grow the canvas while inserting, use Grow to image size below). A single-frame result goes straight into transform mode holding the whole result: move or resize it and press Enter (parts outside the canvas are kept as pixels outside the canvas), or Esc to keep it as inserted. Unavailable with no open document or in canvas size mode.
② New documentOpens a new document the size of the result in a new tab, one frame per sprite frame.
③ Frame duration (ms)Duration of the frames in a new document or added frames (10–10000 ms).
④ Also save the colors as a new paletteAlso saves the colors used as a new palette named source name palette.
  • ⑤ Grow to image size (off by default, remembered): when on, ⑦ Apply into the current document first grows the canvas from the center to the result size if it is smaller (never shrinks; same undo step as the insert). When off, the canvas stays as is and pixels past it are kept as pixels outside the canvas.
  • ⑥ Add to new tab (the button before ⑦ Apply): creates a new document exactly the size of the selected result in a new tab regardless of the output setting (frames and durations kept), and closes the window. It is always a new document of the result size, so ⑤ doesn't affect it.
  • Enter doesn't apply; click Apply or Add to new tab yourself. Double-clicking a candidate tile also applies it.
  • If options in the AI (experimental) step are on, the chat opens after inserting.

Refining with AI (experimental)

The AI (experimental) step at the bottom of the settings column hands the result to the AI chat for touch-ups. It only fills the chat input with the prompt and images and doesn't send them, so check the text and press send yourself.

SettingWhat it does
① Touch up with AI after applyingWhen on, applying opens the chat with the original image attached and ② the prompt filled in. The default prompt asks to fix broken outlines, the eyes and face and stray pixels while keeping the palette, size and frames; Reset to the default prompt restores it.
② PromptThe request filled into the chat input. Your edits are kept for next time, and the reset button next to it (Reset to the default prompt) restores the default text. You press send in the chat yourself.
③ ModelChoose Claude or Codex from ⑦ the list.
④ Add the source to AI referencesAdds the source to the AI references when applying.
⑤ Ask AI to rank candidatesOpens the chat with the original and the top ⑥ Candidates to send candidates attached, and fills in a request to rank them by outlines, face, stray pixels and frame consistency and suggest better settings (not sent).

Part (group) layers · skins

Split a character into parts of different sizes, such as head, body and legs, draw them in each frame, and combine them into one frame to use as an animation while also exporting them as per-part sprites. You can draw several sets (skins) of body types, outfits or gear on the same part setup, swap between them, and swap them the same way in Unity.

The work follows the steps below. Each step is explained in detail on the pages in the right column.

StepWhat you doDetails
1. GroupCreate a group layer that holds one character or one set of gear. You can also move layers you already drew into a group.Creating · Moving layers into and out of groups
2. PartsAdd parts with their own size to the group and draw each part in every frame.Groups and parts · Drawing (part selected)
3. LayoutDecide where each part sits in the frame: move it by hand, turn on auto layout, or place it differently in individual frames.Arranging (group selected) · Changing part positions per frame · Auto layout
4. SkinsDraw another set of art for the same parts. A single part can also wear a different skin.Skins and slots · Empty skins and duplicating · Per-part choices and part-only skins
5. OutfitsChoose the skin each group wears and save combinations you use often as outfit presets.Skin manager · Changing outfits and outfit presets
6. ExportOn save, produce the frame sheet, part atlas and Sprite Library assets for Unity.Export: frame sheet and part atlas · Exporting skins · Unity Sprite Library

Groups and parts

A group is a layer that bundles one character, and a part is a layer inside it that you draw at its own size. A frame is built by placing each part's image for that frame at the part's position.

Structure

  • ② Part layers go under a ① group layer. The group itself has no drawing of its own; its timeline cells show a thumbnail of its parts combined.
  • ③ Each part has its own canvas size (e.g. Head 47×40), and ④ you draw a separate image of that size in each frame.
  • ⑤ The part position is the frame coordinate of the part's top-left corner. Normally every frame uses the same position, but you can also place a part differently in individual frames (Changing part positions per frame). Parts may extend past the canvas.

How a group affects its parts

ItemBehavior
Show · lockHiding or locking a group also hides or locks all of its parts.
OpacityA part is drawn at the group opacity multiplied by the part opacity.
Layer effectsA group effect applies to the combined image of its parts; a part effect applies only to that part (Parts and layer effects).
SkinsSkins belong to a group. All parts in the same group share one skin list (Skins and slots).
OrderParts are reordered only within their own group. To move one to another group or out of a group, see Moving layers into and out of groups.

The canvas depends on the selected layer

Selected layerCanvas
GroupShows the whole frame composite; drag parts to arrange them (Arranging).
PartShows only that part at its own size, with the rest of the frame dimmed (Drawing).

Creating

Create groups and parts when you start a new project, from the menu, or from the timeline's right-click menu. Normal layers you already drew can be turned into parts by moving them into a group.

Starting a new project

In the new project dialog (Ctrl+N), choose ① Start with parts and set ② the first part's name and size to open a project with one group and that one part.

Adding from the menu

Frame·Layer > ③ New group (Ctrl+Shift+N) creates an empty group above the current layer (above its group if a part is selected). ④ New part… (Shift+P) adds a part to the current group, creating a group too if there is none.

In the New part dialog, set ⑤ the name and ⑥ the size. The size starts at the selected part's size (16×16 when no part is selected, no larger than the canvas).

A new part goes in as the top part of the group and is placed, empty, in the middle of the canvas. If its name matches another layer it is changed so it doesn't. Creating it, including any new group, is one undo step.

Adding from the timeline

Right-click a layer name in the timeline and choose ⑦ New group or New part…, or right-click a group and choose ⑧ Add part…, which adds a part to the group you right-clicked.

Moving layers into and out of groups

You can turn a normal layer you already drew into a part, move a part to another group, or take a part out of its group as a normal layer. Each is a single undo step; it doesn't work on locked layers or locked groups, and a group can't go inside another group.

Dragging with Shift

While dragging ① a layer name in the timeline, hold Shift to move it into or out of a group instead of reordering. ② The blue line is the drop position and the highlighted name cell is the receiving group; a not-allowed cursor marks places you can't drop. Release Shift to go back to reordering. When the project has groups, the status bar shows this hint as you start dragging.

Drop onResult
Another group's rowPuts it in as that group's top part. A collapsed group is expanded.
Upper or lower half of a part row in another groupPuts it directly above or below that part.
A normal layer row, the upper half of its own group row, or outside all rowsTakes the part out of the group as a normal layer.

When a normal layer becomes a part

  • The rectangle around the drawing in every frame (including pixels outside the canvas) becomes the part's size and position. The drawing stays at the same place on the canvas.
  • An empty layer, or one with an effect that draws without source pixels (such as Text), covers the whole canvas.
  • The drawing becomes the group's first-skin art and there is no 9-slice border. The layer's pivot (its default pivot and any per-frame pivots) is converted to be relative to the part, pointing at the same spot in the drawing.

When a part leaves its group

  • Only the art of the skin currently shown goes along. Art in other skins and the part's own part-only skins are removed, so if there is any, a confirmation (Other skins will be lost) appears first.
  • When taken out of a group, each frame's drawing stays where that frame's part position put it, and anything past the canvas becomes pixels outside the canvas. The part pivot becomes a canvas-relative layer pivot pointing at the same spot in the drawing, and frames that had their own position or pivot get their own pivot for that frame. The 9-slice border and per-frame positions are removed.
  • If an outfit preset gave that part its own skin, that entry is removed from the preset.

Making a group with Add skin

Right-click ③ a normal layer and, under ④ Add skin (turns into a group), choose New skin (empty) or Duplicate current drawing.

A group named after the layer is created in its place (numbered if the name is taken), the layer goes into it as a part, and a new skin is added and made current. The original drawing stays in the first skin. New skin (empty) stays blank until you draw; Duplicate current drawing starts with a copy of the drawing. The part area is set as in When a normal layer becomes a part above.

Viewing in the timeline

In the timeline a group is one row and its parts are indented rows below it. Each row shows the skin it currently wears and, for parts, the size.

Group rows and part rows

  • ① The triangle on a group row collapses and expands its part rows. The collapsed state is saved in the file.
  • ② Group cells show a thumbnail of the parts combined. It is cropped to the area covering every frame's part positions and enlarged.
  • ③ Part rows are indented and show the name and size.
  • ④ The skin chip after the name is the skin currently worn. Click it to open the skin manager.
  • ⑤ Hiding or locking a group also hides or locks its parts.

Other indicators

  • Parts can be dragged to reorder only within their own group. Dragging a group moves its parts with it.
  • When a part is selected, the info text at the top of the timeline shows its size and its position in the current frame, e.g. Part 47×40 @7,4.
  • A pivot mark at the top left of a part cel means that frame has its own part position or pivot (Changing part positions per frame).

Arranging (group selected)

Selecting a group in the timeline shows the current frame's full composite on the canvas, where you drag parts into place. Moving a part here moves it by the same amount in every frame.

Selecting and moving

  • ① Hover a part to see a dotted border with its name and size. Where parts overlap, the topmost part with a painted pixel at that spot is picked.
  • Click a part to ② select it. Click an empty area or press Esc to deselect.
  • Drag a part and drop it to change its position. Every frame moves by the same amount so that the current frame lands where you dropped it; differences you set per frame are kept. It is one undo step.
  • Arrow keys move the selected part by 1px, and Shift+arrow keys by 8px. This also moves every frame together.
  • Double-click a part to select its layer and switch to drawing.

Locked parts can't be moved; the status bar tells you so. To move a part in a single frame only, see Changing part positions per frame.

Groups with auto layout on

When you start dragging a part of a group with auto layout on, you're asked This group uses auto layout. Turn off auto layout and move it? Confirming only turns auto layout off; drag again to move. When moving with the arrow keys or the part's right-click Position…, confirming turns auto layout off and moves the part in a single undo step.

Pivot markers

③ Part pivots are shown as blue dots. The large orange marker is the pivot of whatever the Pivot tool edits; in this group view that is ④ the group's own pivot (relative to the canvas). Click the text of the canvas chip at the top left of the timeline to target the canvas pivot, and the large marker shows the canvas pivot (the sheet and Unity export pivot) instead. With the Pivot view on in the top bar, part pivots carry the part's name, and layers outside the group and the canvas pivot also appear as small labeled orange dots (Canvas). With the view off, part pivots and the target pivot appear only while the Pivot tool is selected. Change pivots with the Pivot tool.

Changing part positions per frame

A part's position is its base position, shared by all frames, plus a distance moved for each frame. When you want to change only where a part sits, such as a swinging sword or a bobbing head, you can place it elsewhere in individual frames without redrawing it.

Which frames each method moves

MethodFrames moved
Dragging or arrow keys in the group view, the part's right-click Position…Moves the base position, so every frame moves by the same amount. Per-frame differences are kept.
Moving a part without resizing it in Transform mode (Ctrl+T) with no selection and applyingMoves the part position of that frame only; the art stays in place inside the cel. The part pivot moves along; right-drag to move and the pivot stays where it was.
Moving or resizing the part in Transform whole layer (Alt+Shift+C) and applyingApplies to every frame. Moving alone moves the base position, and per-frame differences are kept.
Permanently applying an effect such as Shake or Bob with Move pivot along on to a partIn every frame the effect moved the drawing, the part position moves by that distance (rounded) and the art is baked in place inside the cel.
Turning off From current frame on in the Pivot toolClears the positions and pivots set from the current frame to the last frame, going back to the base position and part pivot.

Moving in one frame only

  1. Select the part layer in the timeline and go to the frame to move. Remove any selection.
  2. Enter Transform mode with Edit > Transform (rotate/size)… (Ctrl+T).
  3. Drag inside the box to move it. Don't touch the size handles or rotation. Right-drag to keep the pivot where it is.
  4. Apply (Enter). It is one undo step; repeat on each other frame you want to move.

Checking and undoing

  • The part pivot is relative to the part, so it moves along with it.
  • Part cels with their own position or pivot show a pivot mark at the top left in the timeline, and the Pivot tool shows From current frame on as on.
  • The part's right-click Position…, the timeline info text and the group view's position messages show the position in the current frame. Changing it there moves every frame by the same amount.
  • To restore the original placement from a frame on, turn off From current frame on in the Pivot tool on that frame.

Edits that keep per-frame positions

Per-frame distances are saved in the project file and the undo history, and survive the following edits.

EditResult
Duplicating frames, Extend frames in the effects dialogCopied frames use the same positions as their source frames.
Duplicating a part layerThe copied part gets the same per-frame distances.
Resizing the part, moving the base position, auto layoutThe per-frame distances are still added to the new base position.
Resizing, flipping or rotating the canvasThe distances are transformed the same way, so they keep pointing at the same place in the picture.
Merging filesMerged parts bring their per-frame positions. When several files have the same part, the first file added wins.
Taking a part out of its groupEach frame's drawing stays as a normal layer at that frame's position.
Part atlas cells and pivots are relative to the part, so moving a part doesn't change them. Moved positions show up wherever frames are composited: the canvas, the preview and the frame sheet.

Drawing (part selected)

Selecting ① a part layer shows ② only that part at its own size, with the rest of the frame dimmed around it. In this view you use every drawing tool as on a normal layer, and coordinates are relative to the part's top-left corner. The dimmed surroundings follow the part's position in the current frame.

Changing size and position

  • Resize: Frame·Layer > ③ Part size… or right-click the part → ④ Part size…. Pick where the image goes with ⑥ the 9-direction anchor. It applies to the art of every frame and skin; drawn pixels stay at the same place on the canvas and the pivot keeps pointing at the same spot in the image.
  • Set the position numerically: right-click the part → ⑤ Position…. The menu and dialog show the position in the current frame; changing it moves every frame by the same amount.
  • To change the size and place of every frame together on the canvas, use Transform whole layer (Alt+Shift+C). To move the part in one frame only, move it on that frame in Transform mode (Ctrl+T) with no selection (Changing part positions per frame).

Pivot and 9-slice

  • With a part selected, ⑦ the Pivot tool (K) and the 9-slice tool (S) apply to that part.
  • The part pivot and 9-slice border are shared by all skins and are exported as the Pivot and Border of the part atlas sprites. Set a different part pivot per frame with From current frame on in the Pivot tool.

Parts and layer effects

Parts and groups can have layer effects too. Where you add the effect decides which image it applies to, how it exports and whether it can be applied permanently.

Differences by where the effect is

Effect onCanvas, preview, frame sheetPart atlasApply permanently
PartApplied to that part's image.Included in the sprites. Effects that spread outward, such as an outer outline or shadow, widen the sprite by the effect margin, and the pivot and 9-slice border are shifted to keep pointing at the same spot.Possible. The part's size and position grow to fit the spread art, and the art of every frame and skin moves along.
GroupApplied to the combined image of the parts.Not included (frame sheet only).Not possible (Group effects can't be baked — apply them to parts.).

Editing a part with effects hidden

With ② Effects on in the top bar, ① the Head drawing and pivot marker appear moved together on a frame affected by Shake. The preview on the right also shows the full character with effects.

Turn off ④ Effects to see and edit ③ the original Head drawing and pivot. The preview on the right keeps showing effects regardless of this button.

Applying permanently to a part

  • Apply permanently in the effects dialog or Apply effect permanently in the effect row's right-click menu bakes effects into the part's art. Locked parts can't be baked.
  • For effects such as Shake or Bob with Move pivot along on, the distance the drawing moved is not baked into the art; the part position in that frame moves by it instead. Those frames then have their own per-frame positions.
  • If the part grows, a group with auto layout on is laid out again.

Auto layout

Auto layout lines a group's parts up by size horizontally, vertically or in a grid, and lines them up again whenever the parts change. Right-click a group in the timeline and choose ① Auto layout…. It is handy for drawing parts spread out like an atlas.

Settings

Change the values while watching ② the preview, then click ③ Apply to turn auto layout on. Turning it on and changing its settings are each one undo step.

SettingDescription
④ DirectionHorizontal · vertical · grid (set column count)
⑤ AlignTop/center/bottom or left/center/right
⑥ DistributionStart · center · end · even
⑦ Spacing · paddingIn px
⑧ OrderFrom top part · from bottom part
⑨ AreaWhole canvas by default; X, Y, W, H can be set manually
⑩ Hidden parts keep their spaceOn by default — toggling visibility doesn't move other parts. When off, hidden parts stay where they are and only visible parts are laid out

While it is on

  • ⑪ A diamond mark appears left of the skin chip on the group's timeline row. Clicking it opens the settings, and the group's right-click item becomes Auto layout settings… with a check mark.
  • The parts are laid out again whenever you add or delete a part or change a part's size or order. With Hidden parts keep their place off, changing visibility also re-lays them out.
  • Trying to move a part by hand asks whether to turn auto layout off (Arranging). Per-frame distances are still added on top of the auto layout position.
  • Turn it off with Turn off auto layout in the group's right-click menu or Turn off auto layout in the settings dialog. The parts stay where they are.

Skins and slots

A skin is one set of art drawn for the same part setup. Each group has a list of named skins (at least one; the first in the list is the first skin), and ① only one of them is shown. The group is the place where skins are swapped, a skin slot.

Skin rules

  • Skins in the same group replace each other (swap).
  • ② Different groups stack in layer order (overlay).
  • The artwork alone differs between skins. Parts, size, position, pivot and 9-slice are shared by all skins.
  • Skin names are unique within a group, including part-only skins.

Example: body, hair, armor

Put "hair" and "armor" groups above a "base body" group at the bottom; picking one skin in each group and stacking them makes one character. The gear groups' first skin, "none", is left empty and the gear is drawn in the other skins.

Group (bottom → top)SkinsPurpose
Base bodydefault · muscular · zombieSwaps the whole body.
Hairnone · short · longStacks on the body.
Armornone · leather · plateStacks on top.

Creating skins

Create skins with New skin (empty) or Duplicate in the skin manager, or from the group's right-click Skin submenu. On a normal layer, right-click Add skin (turns into a group) creates the group and skin at once (Moving layers into and out of groups). For the difference between empty and duplicated skins, see Empty skins and duplicating.

Empty skins and duplicating

A skin shows only its own art. Cels you haven't drawn in a non-first skin or a part-only skin don't borrow the first skin's art; they stay empty. To start from the same art, create the skin with Duplicate.

New skin (empty)

Selecting ① the empty Props skin leaves its Gem and Sparkle cels blank. ② The canvas and ③ the preview show only the Body character from another layer, without this group's accessories. Drawing on a blank cel starts from an empty image of that skin.

Duplicate

Duplicating ④ the Props skin copies its Gem and Sparkle cels, so ⑤ the blue gem and yellow sparkle appear beside the Body character. From then on the two skins' art is edited separately.

Where empty cels stay empty

Empty cels are never filled with first-skin art anywhere: canvas, preview, timeline, skin cards, outfits, export, layer effects or AI. Files from older versions still open, but cels that used to show the first skin's art now appear empty.

Per-part choices and part-only skins

A single part in a group can wear a different skin (a part override). For example, ① the body keeps the group skin "knight" while ② only the head wears "zombie". The visible skin is decided in the order part override → ③ current group skin; if that skin has no art in a cel, the cel is empty. Part overrides are part of the outfit, so they aren't recorded in undo history but are saved in the file.

Part-only skins

You can also create a part-only skin that exists for just one part (e.g. a "hat" for Head). Create it with ④ This part's skins on the part row in the skin manager or from the part's right-click menu (⑥ → ⑦); it's marked "Individual" only in that part's list (⑤, ⑧). A part-only skin is worn only as that part's override.

Promoting and adding as part-only

Right-click a part-only skin card to choose ⑨ Promote to full skin. Right-click a full skin card on a part row and choose ⑩ Add as "Head" only (the quotes hold that part's name) to copy that skin's art for the part into a new part-only skin and put it on that part right away. The name is the original skin's name with a number added so it doesn't clash (e.g. knight 2), and it is added without asking. The original full skin and the other parts stay as they are, and the added skin can be undone.

If something like a hat, clothes or shoes will always be swapped separately, giving it its own group (skin slot) is also cleaner to work with in Unity.

Skin manager

The skin manager shows every group's skins and the outfit presets as cards for viewing and editing. Open it by ① clicking a skin chip, from ② Frame·Layer > Manage skins… , or with ③ Manage… in the outfit row below the preview. You can keep it open beside you while editing.

Choosing with cards

④ The full outfit is at the top, followed by ⑤ skin cards for each group. Click a card to apply it immediately; double-click to apply and close the window. ⑥ New skin (empty) adds an empty skin, and ⑦ Duplicate adds a skin with a copy of the current skin's art.

Renaming, reordering and per-part view

  • In a card's right-click menu (⑧) choose Rename…, Delete or Move forward/back. You can also drag cards to reorder them; the skin in front becomes the first skin. Adding, editing and deleting skins can all be undone. The undo (Ctrl+Z) and redo (Ctrl+Y) shortcuts work even while the skin manager is in front.
  • Expand ⑨ Per part to see skin cards for each part; click one to put just that part in that skin. The leading ⑩ None card clears the part override so the part follows the group skin.
  • Use the ⑪ All | Group skins | Individual skins toggle at the top to filter the cards shown.

Card images

Group skin cards use the timeline view: cropped to the part area and enlarged, showing the editable art without effects. The group skin entries in Export's Choose skins dialog look the same. The full outfit preview at the top and the outfit preset cards instead show composited frames with effects.

Changing outfits and outfit presets

An outfit is the current look: the skin each group wears plus any part overrides. Besides the skin manager you can change it in the ways below, and save combinations you use often as outfit presets.

Changing the outfit

  • Pick a skin per group in ① the Outfit row below the preview.
  • Previous/next skin (current group): Alt+[ / Alt+]. With a part selected, it applies to that part's group.
  • Choose from the ② Skin submenu when right-clicking a group, or the ③ Skin for this part submenu when right-clicking a part.

Changing outfits is a view switch, so it isn't recorded in undo history, but the current outfit is saved in the file.

Outfit presets

Use ④ Save current outfit… in the skin manager to save the current combination under a name, then click ⑤ its card to switch to that outfit. ⑥ Right-click a card to overwrite it with the current outfit, rename, delete or reorder it.

Presets remember groups, parts and skins themselves rather than their names, so renaming keeps them working. Deleted groups or skins are skipped when applying, with a notice, and are used again if you restore them with undo. Groups that didn't exist when the preset was saved keep their current skin. Saving and editing presets can be undone.

Export: frame sheet and part atlas

A project with parts can produce two outputs on save; turn each on or off in ③ Output. Both come out as an image + Unity .meta.

The two outputs

  • ① Frame sheet: an animation sheet with every layer composited in each frame. Group effects and per-frame part positions are included.
  • ② Part atlas: one cell (sprite) per part × frame with content. If ④ the name is empty, it is <frame sheet name>_parts.

Part atlas sprites

  • A sprite's Pivot is the part pivot (or that frame's own pivot if it has one), and its Border is the part's 9-slice border.
  • Part effects are included in the sprites; group effects are not (Parts and layer effects).
  • Layout, sort order and cell names follow ⑤ the common sheet settings in the export dialog (Export). Old files that are no longer produced are moved to the app trash (see Files).

Exporting skins

Choose which skins to export under Skin in the common export settings.

SettingResult
① Current outfit (default)Exports only the outfit currently shown.
② Selected skinsUse ③ Choose skins and combine the selected results in ④ one image (cel names <name>@<skin>) or ⑤ separate files (<sheet>@<skin>.png).

Choose skins dialog

Pick group skins and part-only skins in ⑥ and outfit presets in ⑦. The frame sheet gets one set per selected group skin (only that group changed, the rest in the current outfit) and one set per selected outfit preset.

Part atlas cell names

In a part atlas the first skin uses the original names (Head, Head_1 …), while other skins contribute only cels drawn in that skin, e.g. Head@knight. Leaving the first skin unselected also leaves out its sprites.

Unity Sprite Library

Turn on ① Sprite Library in the part atlas section of the export dialog to create a Sprite Library Asset for the Unity 2D Animation package per group × skin, named ② <part atlas name>_<group>@<skin>.spriteLib. Use them to swap a whole skin in Unity by changing one asset.

What a library contains

  • Category = part, Label = frame (0, 1 … or idle_0 …).
  • Libraries for the same group all share the same Categories and Labels; only the sprites differ. So one animation works for every skin.
  • A part/frame not drawn in a selected skin keeps its Category/Label but has an empty sprite entry; first-skin art is never substituted.

Using it in Unity

  1. Put a Sprite Library component on the parent of each group's part objects and assign one of that group's libraries.
  2. Give each part object a Sprite Resolver (Category = part) and keyframe its Label (frame).
  3. To switch skins, just swap the asset on that group's Sprite Library component for another skin's.

Importing old files and PNGs

Opening an old atlas project, or a PNG sheet whose cells have different sizes, converts it into a group and parts.

Atlas files from older versions

Opening an atlas .chickxel from an older version converts it into one "parts" group with one part per sprite. Unity guids and spriteIDs carry over.

PNG sheets with cells of different sizes

PNG sheets with cells of different sizes are imported as ① one group named after the sheet and ② one part per cell. ③ Frame sheet export starts turned off, so saving rewrites only the part atlas.

AI chat (Claude · Codex)

AI chat is a window where you ask Claude Code or Codex CLI, installed on this computer, to look at the open image and edit it. The AI uses editor tools that Chickxel provides to place pixels, create frames, layers, parts and skins, add layer effects, and turn images made with Codex image generation into pixel art. No API key is needed; usage counts against your own Claude or ChatGPT subscription.

CategoryPages
Getting startedWhat it can do · One-time setup: install and log in · Opening the chat window · Claude and Codex tabs · How it works · Using other AI apps
ChattingChatting · Progress display · Answering the AI's questions · Editing or deleting the last message · Continuing past chats · Help and example requests
Drawing and editingEdits the AI can make · Generate image → pixelate · Asking for layer effects · References: teach the AI your style
SafeguardsUndoing · Limiting edits to the current file · Protecting locked layers
TroubleshootingTroubleshooting

How your work is protected

  • Undo: each AI reply is one undo step. Undo it all at once with Undo this step under the reply or Ctrl+Z (Undoing).
  • Locked layers: the AI never touches layers locked in the timeline (Protecting locked layers).
  • Current file only: when on, the AI edits only the image open when you send and can't open or create other files (Limiting edits to the current file).
  • Saving and tools: the AI saves only when asked, and runs with the CLI's built-in features such as shell commands turned off, using only Chickxel's tools. It searches the web only when you ask it to (How it works).

What it can do

Chat with an AI inside the editor to create and edit images. Ask in plain words, like "add a black outline" or "draw 4 walking frames" (①), and the AI edits the open image directly (②).

What you can ask for

TaskExample requestDetails
Draw and editDraw a 16×16 slime character. Transparent background, 4 shades of green, dark outline.Edits the AI can make
Generate a picture → pixel art (Codex)Draw a knight with a sword.Generate image → pixelate
Animation and framesMake a 4-frame walk animation of this character and tag it walk (120 ms per frame).Edits the AI can make
Parts and skinsMake a character group split into head, body, arm, and leg parts.Edits the AI can make
Layer effectsAdd a 1 px black outline to the character and a white hit flash on frames 3–4.Asking for layer effects
New effects (scripts)Make a script effect that flashes red and scatters into pieces when hit, and save it to my effects.Asking for layer effects
Draw from your referencesDraw a 32px bat like the slime in the references.References: teach the AI your style

What you need

No API key is needed. It uses Claude Code (Claude subscription login) or Codex CLI (ChatGPT subscription login) installed on your computer. Usage counts against each subscription. Pick which one to use with the ③ tabs at the top of the chat window.

One-time setup: install and log in

The first time you open AI chat, the AI Status and Settings window checks the CLI installation, sign-in and editor connection. If something is missing, install and sign in with the buttons in that window or the chat window, then check again. The checks send no model request, so they use no AI usage.

Installing a CLI

The Chickxel download doesn't include Claude Code or Codex CLI. The first time, follow these steps.

  1. If the CLI is missing, click ① Install and log in via terminal, then review ⑤ the command to run and approve it (⑥).
  2. The official install command runs in a terminal, followed by the login command if it succeeds. Finish the browser authentication yourself.
  • If Node.js, which Codex needs, is missing, it's installed too (winget or the official ZIP on Windows, Homebrew or nvm on macOS).
  • If installation fails, check the error in the terminal and use the on-screen ② copy command, ③ set path manually or ④ Check again options.

The AI Status and Settings window

Chickxel checks the install and login status (⑦ ready, ⑧ not installed) and moves on to the chat screen (⑨).

This window appears first each time you open the AI chat window (with Don't show again on, not until a CLI location changes). You can open it anytime with AI > Open AI Status… or Open AI status and settings in Preferences > AI Status. The Chickxel internal connection is at the top, followed by a card for Claude Code and one for Codex CLI, each checking three rows.

RowWhat it checks
MCP serverConnects directly to the MCP server inside Chickxel and checks that it answers initialization (initialize) and the tool list (tools/list). When it works, the number of tools is shown.
CLI/versionActually runs the CLI executable Chickxel picked (--version) and shows its version and full path. If a different executable is on the PATH, that path is shown too.
Sign-inShows whether you are signed in and the account (or sign-in method).
MCP connectionClaude Code is actually started, without any message or model request, with the same connection settings as the chat, and the row shows ⑦ Live CLI connection passed · tool list received only when Chickxel receives its tool list request (tools/list). If it fails, the row shows the stage where it stopped and the CLI's error output (Troubleshooting). For Codex CLI it checks that the real CLI can read the connection settings, and the tool connection is confirmed on the first message.
ButtonWhat it does
Install or updateShows the commands to run and, once you approve, runs the install or update and sign-in in a terminal. Keep the window open: it checks again automatically when a new CLI appears or the existing file changes, and again when you finish signing in.
Sign inOpens the CLI's sign-in command in a terminal. It checks again automatically when sign-in finishes.
Sign outSigns out of that CLI after asking. Chickxel doesn't store account passwords or tokens.
Change executableLets you pick the CLI executable to use.
Find automaticallyClears the chosen path and searches the known locations again.
Check all againChecks every row again.
OptionWhat it does
Don't show againWhen on, the choice is remembered however you close the window (OK, Cancel or the close button), and the chat opens without this window. It appears again when an AI CLI location or a custom path changes, not just because the app or a CLI was updated.
Open in centerOn by default. When on, the AI chat window is placed at the center of the editor when you open it or bring an open one back; when off, it keeps its saved position. Saved when the window closes.

Signing in

If it's installed but not logged in, click ⑩ Log in with Claude / Log in with Codex or ⑪ Log in via terminal. The logged-in account is shown at the top of the window (⑫).

How the executable is found

  • Chickxel runs the executable it found by its full path and adds its folder to the PATH the CLI uses, so you don't need to change your terminal PATH or choose the executable yourself.
  • If the saved path fails to run or to connect, other known locations are checked and a working CLI is switched in automatically.
  • The Codex CLI bundled with the ChatGPT app on macOS is found automatically. Nothing else needs to be installed.
  • On Windows, a Codex CLI installed with npm is started from the package's codex.exe directly instead of through codex.cmd, so long chats and attached images don't hit the cmd.exe command-line limit (8191 characters). If a command is still over the limit (about 32,000 characters), it isn't started and a "command line is too long" error is shown.

Opening the chat window

There are three ways to open the AI chat window. If it is already open, the same command brings it to the front.

  • ① The AI chat button on the right of the bar below the menu
  • Menu ② AI > ③ AI chat
  • Ctrl+L (shown next to ③)

The chat opens in its own small window and remembers its size. If it was open when you closed the app, it reopens next time.

The window position follows Open in center (on by default) in the AI Status and Settings window. When on, the window is placed at the center of the editor each time you open it or bring it back; when off, it opens where you last left it (centered if that position is off every screen).

Claude and Codex tabs

① [Claude | Codex] at the top of the window are tabs. Each tab has its own conversation, input box, attachments, model and undo buttons, and conversations continue when you switch tabs.

  • Switching tabs while a reply is coming in doesn't stop the work; a dot appears after the tab name while it runs (Codex in ①).
  • To keep two AIs from editing the same image at once, only one tab works at a time. While one tab is busy, messages sent from the other tab go into the ② queue above the input box and are sent in order once the work is done (Chatting).

How it works

The AI model isn't built into Chickxel. When you send a message, Chickxel starts the CLI on your computer, and the CLI talks to its company's model while calling editor tools through Chickxel's MCP server.

  1. When you press Send, Chickxel starts Claude Code or Codex CLI (codex exec) and passes it your text and attached images.
  2. The CLI sends the request to the Anthropic or OpenAI model with your signed-in subscription.
  3. When the model calls a tool, the CLI sends an HTTP request to Chickxel's MCP server; the editor runs the tool and returns the result (text or an image). Each tool call appears as a tool line in the chat.
  4. When the reply ends, everything changed in that turn is grouped into one undo step.

The MCP server and temporary MCP config

The first time you use an AI feature (AI chat, the AI Status window or the Effect Maker's AI pane), Chickxel opens an MCP server at an address reachable only from this computer (http://127.0.0.1:port/mcp, or localhost on Windows when that address can't be used). It picks a free port and creates a new random token each time the app starts; requests without the right token are rejected (HTTP 401).

The address and token are passed straight to the CLI as a temporary MCP config: Claude Code gets a config file in the temp folder (--mcp-config, --strict-mcp-config), and Codex CLI gets launch options (-c mcp_servers=…) with the token in an environment variable. This is separate from the MCP servers registered in your own Claude or Codex settings, so you don't need to register anything, and Chickxel doesn't change your settings. Temporary config files are deleted when the app closes.

If Allow external AI apps is on in Preferences > AI Status, Chickxel opens this MCP server as soon as it starts, and other AI apps can use the same editor tools with a token of their own (Using other AI apps).

What the AI can use

CLILaunch settings
Claude CodeAll built-in tools except web search and web fetch are turned off (--tools "WebSearch,WebFetch") and only Chickxel's MCP tools are allowed. The web tools are used only when you ask the AI to look something up (see Looking it up on the web under References). Other MCP servers from your settings aren't loaded.
Codex CLIFeatures such as the shell, the browser and image generation are turned off, and it runs in a read-only sandbox. Web search (web_search="live") stays on but is used only when you ask the AI to look something up. Chickxel's MCP tools are allowed without asking, and project documents such as AGENTS.md in the working folder aren't read.
  • Generate image → pixelate starts a separate Codex CLI with image generation turned on, only while it makes that image (web search off).
  • The AI pane of the Effect Maker window uses a different token and can only use tools that read the document and references, plus replacing that editor's source; it searches the web only when you ask.

What is sent and what is stored

  • Your messages and attached images, and the images and document details the AI looks at with tools, go through the CLI to that company's model. When you ask for a web search, the search terms and the pages it reads go to that model too.
  • Chats are saved on this computer in a per-AI folder under <settings folder>/Chickxel/Chats/ (Continuing past chats).

Using other AI apps

You can let an AI app you already use, such as Claude Desktop, Cursor, Claude Code or Codex CLI, look at and edit the image in the running Chickxel. Ask in that app's conversation, and it places pixels and works with frames, layers, parts and effects through the same editor tools as AI chat. Use it when you'd rather work in an app you know, or alongside that app's other tools. It is off at first.

Turning it on

  • ① Allow external AI apps: on the AI Status tab of Edit > Preferences…. Turn it on and press OK to start accepting connections; turn it off and press OK to cut off connected apps right away. Chickxel keeps this setting the next time it starts. Hover over the info box next to it for a short explanation.
  • ② Allow opening files: when on, the AI can open any image or project on this computer in a new tab by giving its path to open_file. When off, that tool is left out and the AI works only on open images and ones it creates.
  • ③ Allow image generation: when on, the AI can call Generate image → pixelate with generate_pixel_art. It draws with your Codex CLI, so it uses your Codex quota and needs Codex CLI installed and signed in. Options such as size and conversion follow AI chat's drawing settings.
  • ④ Copy connection settings: copies the JSON to paste into another AI app's MCP settings. It contains the path of the running Chickxel executable, so copy and paste it again if you move Chickxel to another folder.

After changing ② or ③, the AI app sees the new tool list only once it fetches the list again: restart the app or reconnect its MCP server.

Registering Chickxel in another AI app

What ④ copies looks like this. When the app starts the Chickxel executable with --mcp, it runs as a small bridge that opens no window and only relays the connection.

{
  "mcpServers": {
    "chickxel": {
      "command": "C:\\Users\\me\\AppData\\Local\\Programs\\Chickxel\\Chickxel.exe",
      "args": ["--mcp"]
    }
  }
}

With the install scripts, the path is %LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\Chickxel\Chickxel.exe on Windows and /Applications/Chickxel.app/Contents/MacOS/Chickxel.App on macOS. Copying it with ④ is more reliable than typing it. Where to paste it in each app:

AppWhere to register it
Claude DesktopAdd the chickxel entry to mcpServers in claude_desktop_config.json, which Settings > Developer > Edit Config opens (Windows %APPDATA%\Claude\, macOS ~/Library/Application Support/Claude/), then quit Claude Desktop completely and start it again.
Claude CodePut the same JSON in .mcp.json in the project folder (it asks before using it the first time), or register it for every folder from a terminal with claude mcp add --scope user chickxel -- "path to the executable" --mcp.
CursorPut the same JSON in ~/.cursor/mcp.json (all projects) or in the project's .cursor/mcp.json, and check that chickxel is enabled in the MCP list of Cursor's settings.
Codex CLIInstead of JSON, add [mcp_servers.chickxel] to ~/.codex/config.toml as below. Wrap a Windows path in single quotes (so TOML keeps the backslashes).
 
[mcp_servers.chickxel]
command = 'C:\Users\me\AppData\Local\Programs\Chickxel\Chickxel.exe'
args = ["--mcp"]

Once registered, the app lists the chickxel tools. Chickxel's drawing rules (look at the document first, coordinates, parts, how to use effects and so on) are passed along when it connects. Try asking the app something like "add a 1px white outline to the open image".

How it works

  1. The AI app starts Chickxel --mcp. This process opens no window; it only passes messages between that app and the running Chickxel.
  2. For each message, the bridge reads the Chickxel MCP server address (http://127.0.0.1:port/mcp) and the external connection token from mcp-external.json in the settings folder. The file exists only while the external AI connection is on.
  3. Chickxel shows and runs only the allowed tools for requests with that token. Tools run in the editor, so the canvas changes right away, and the result (text or an image) goes back to the app.
  4. Tools run in the order the app sends them. Long tools such as image generation send progress notes while you wait, and other requests are accepted meanwhile.

Because the bridge rereads the file for every message, you don't need to restart the app after restarting Chickxel. While Chickxel is closed, the app gets an explanatory error (see If it doesn't connect below).

Connection indicator

⑤ While the external AI connection is on, a plug icon and a dim External AI show at the right end of the status bar. When another AI app calls a tool, it turns the accent color for 4 seconds with the tool's name (the picture is right after a draw_pixels call). It disappears when you turn the connection off.

Differences from AI chat

The editor tools are the same, but since the conversation happens in the other app, a few things differ.

ItemAI chatExternal AI connection
ConnectionChickxel starts the CLI and passes it a temporary MCP config.The app starts Chickxel --mcp. You register it in the app once.
UsageYour Claude or ChatGPT subscription (CLI sign-in)That app's account and plan, plus your Codex quota if image generation is on.
UndoOne reply is one step (Undoing)One tool call is one step ("AI: tool name"). A request that called several tools takes several undos.
AI questionsA question card above the input box (Answering the AI's questions)There is no ask_user; the AI asks in that app's conversation instead.
Opening filesOpens files with open_file.Only with ② Allow opening files
Image generationFollows the drawing mode in drawing settings.Only with ③ Allow image generation. The subject image comes as a base64 PNG (subject_image) instead of an attached image number.
Current file onlyTurned on with the chat button (Limiting edits to the current file).Not used, whatever its state in AI chat.
Locked layersNever touched.The same.
Drawing rulesGiven as the system prompt.The same rules arrive as MCP instructions on connecting (some apps may not use them).
Where you see itTool lines and result pictures in the chat windowThe app's conversation; Chickxel shows only the canvas changes and the ⑤ indicator.

Safety

  • The connection is open only on this computer (127.0.0.1), and only requests with the external connection token are accepted. The token is new each time Chickxel starts.
  • <settings folder>/Chickxel/mcp-external.json, which holds the token, exists only while the connection is on and is deleted when you turn it off or close Chickxel. Programs running under your account on this computer can read it and connect, so turn the connection off when you don't use it.
  • Opening files and image generation must be turned on separately, and external AIs never touch locked layers either. The rules tell the AI to save only when asked.
  • Images and document details the app looks at with tools go to that app's model, as described in that app's privacy information.

If it doesn't connect

SymptomCause and fix
The app gets "Chickxel isn't running or its external AI connection is off"Start Chickxel and check that ① is on. The same happens if Chickxel quit unexpectedly; once it is running again, the app continues without a restart.
The app has no chickxel toolsCheck the config file's location and JSON (commas and brackets) and restart the app. The app starts the executable itself, so the path must point to the real file; if you moved Chickxel, copy it again with ④.
open_file or generate_pixel_art is missing or refused as "not available over the external AI connection"Turn on ② or ③ and reconnect so the app fetches the tool list again. ask_user is always left out for external connections.
Image generation fails with "Couldn't read subject_image as a PNG image"The subject image must be a base64 PNG without a data: prefix. If the app can't pass images that way, describe the subject in words instead.

Chatting

Type a request in the input box and send it; the AI replies and edits the image. You can attach pictures to show what you mean, choose the model, and queue the next request while a reply is coming in.

To do thisHow
Send / new lineEnter (or ① Send) / Shift+Enter
Attach a reference image② Image…, paste a picture (Ctrl/⌘+V), or drop image files on the input box
Show the current frame③ Attach canvas
Change modelThe ④ Model list at the top (per AI; applies from the next message). The chosen AI tab and model are saved right away and come back next time; the Effect Maker AI pane uses the same setting (whichever you chose last).
See past chats / start a new one⑤ New chat (shows the list; type below to start a new chat)
See what the AI can do, examples, and tipsThe ⑥ question mark button next to New chat
Edit or delete the last messageThe ⑦ pencil (edit) and trash (delete) icons under the last message — see Editing or deleting the last message
Stop the reply⑧ Stop (shown in place of Send while a reply is coming in)

Choosing a model

The first entry in the model list, ⑨ Default, also shows the name of the model that will actually be used.

Attaching images: image chips

Attached images no longer line up above the input box: each one goes into the text at the cursor as an ⑩ image chip (a small square thumbnail on a checkerboard and an accent-colored #1, #2… in a rounded accent border, sized to the text line). Pixel-art thumbnails are scaled pixel-exact so they stay sharp. You can type before and after chips, and they're numbered from 1 in text order within the message.

  • A chip is one unit. Backspace right after it or Delete right before it removes the chip and its attachment, and arrow keys skip over it. When chips are removed or reordered, the remaining ones are renumbered.
  • Hover a chip for a ⑪ thumbnail preview; click it to see ⑫ the image large with ⑬ Copy image and Save image… buttons and a ⑭ Remove attachment button.
  • The sent message shows chips (⑮) with the same look (square checkerboard thumbnail and #number) in the same places; hover to preview, click to open the same enlarged view. The AI receives the images as attachments in chip order, and the text says [Image #1] where each chip was, so it knows which image you mean.
  • Reopened past chats show the chips in the same places; clicking one shows the saved 64 px thumbnail enlarged. Chats saved before this feature still show their thumbnails above the text.

Copying messages and saving images

  • ⑯ Every message you sent has a Copy message text button under its bubble. It copies only the text, without the image chips. The pencil and trash icons still appear only under the last message.
  • Images in the chat (chips and attached images in sent messages, result images the AI drew, the canvas snapshot at the end of a turn) open in the enlarged view with one click, and right-clicking them shows Save image… and Copy image. The image in the enlarged view can be right-clicked too. Right-clicking a chip inside a sent message opens this image menu instead of the text selection menu. Copy puts the image on the clipboard immediately; Save writes it as a PNG file at the pixel size shown.

Sending while a reply is coming in

You can send the next message with Enter or Send even while a reply is coming in. It goes into the Queued row above the input box (first line of the text · image count · remove X) and is sent in order when the current turn ends (even if you stopped it). Messages sent while the other AI tab is working wait the same way. Removing one with X puts it back into the input box if that is empty, so you can edit and resend it; otherwise it is discarded. Starting a new chat clears the queue.

Progress display

While a reply is coming in, the bottom line (①) shows what's happening (waiting for response, thinking, writing tool call, running in editor, writing reply) and the elapsed time.

Large jobs that draw many frames at once can take over a minute to prepare. If the received character count keeps growing, it's working.

Answering the AI's questions

When the AI needs a decision from you, a ① question card appears right above the chat input box. The AI doesn't pick an answer on its own and carry on. If you were on the other tab, the window switches to the tab that asked.

  • ② Click one of the options (2–5) to send that answer to the AI; the chosen answer is also added to the chat as a line.
  • ③ Other shows an Other answer box and a Submit answer button. Type your answer and press Enter or Submit answer.
  • ④ Cancel (or Esc in the answer box) closes the question; the AI won't guess an answer and carry on.

Generated images larger than the canvas

Generated image larger than the canvas: if an image made with image generation is larger than the open canvas, you're first asked to choose ② Expand canvas (keeps the existing art and enlarges the canvas) or Choose crop position. The canvas can't be expanded while a layer is locked. If the open document or the canvas size changes while the question is waiting, nothing is inserted.

  • ⑤ The Choose crop position window (opens when you choose the crop option): the area inside the highlighted outline on the source preview is what's kept. Drag the outline or click outside it to move it; arrow keys move it by 1 pixel and Shift+arrow keys by 10. Canvas space beyond the image stays transparent.
  • ⑥ Check the size and position to keep (in source pixels), then press ⑦ Keep this area to crop and insert that area. Cancel inserts nothing and leaves the existing art unchanged.

Chickxel also sends a Windows or macOS system notification when the AI asks a question, finishes, or hits an error (if the operating system allows notifications; not on Linux yet), so you know when to come back even while working in another window.

Editing or deleting the last message

Small ① pencil (edit) and ② trash (delete) icons sit under the last message you sent. They're disabled while a reply is coming in; a turn you stopped with Stop can be deleted.

  • Delete: removes that message and the reply, tool lines and result image after it. The previous message then gets the icons, so you can press again to step back one message at a time, all the way to the start.
  • Edit: removes it the same way, then puts its text and image chips (with the attached pictures, if they're still available) back in the message box so you can change it and send. Images of chats reopened from disk aren't resent.
  • If that reply changed the drawing and the step can still be undone, you're asked Also undo the drawing changes made in this exchange? — ③ Undo (same as Undo this step), ④ Keep drawing, or ⑤ Cancel (nothing is removed).
  • You can also delete or edit the last message of a reopened past chat (the drawing question isn't asked there).

The AI doesn't remember what you removed: after a delete or edit, the next message starts a new AI session and includes a recap of the remaining conversation.

Continuing past chats

Chats are saved automatically each time a reply finishes. Claude and Codex keep separate lists.

  • Press ① New chat to show the ② past chats list in the chat area. Each row shows the title (first message), date, and model.
  • Click a row to see that chat again; messages you send continue the same conversation. The Undo this step buttons of earlier turns aren't recreated.
