  • The ③ star icon makes a chat a favorite: it's pinned to the top and never deleted automatically. The ④ trash icon deletes a chat after asking (⑥).
  • Type a message in the ⑤ box below the list and send it to start a new chat.
  • Up to 20 chats are kept per AI; beyond that, the oldest non-favorite chat is deleted.
  • When you reopen the chat window or restart the app, each tab opens the chat (or the list) you were last on.

Help and example requests

Press the ① question mark button next to New chat to widen the window to the right and open a help panel beside the chat. The chat stays visible and you can keep using it. Press the question mark button again or the ② X at the top of the panel to close it, and the window returns to its original width. If the window is at the right edge of the screen, it moves left so the panel fits and moves back when you close it; in a maximized window the chat area gets narrower instead.

  • ③ What the AI can do: the tools for looking at the drawing, generating an image and pixelating it, drawing, frames and animation, layers, groups and parts, skins and outfits, layer effects, Effect Maker, references, canvas and colors, and files, explained by group.
  • ④ Example requests: click one to put it in the message box (the help panel stays open), then edit and send it.
  • ⑤ Tips: in our tests Codex's GPT-6-Astra drew best, and Codex uses GPT-6-Astra (recommended) by default. Be specific about size, palette, and style, attach a reference picture, and break big jobs into small steps. You can delete or edit the last message and send it again.

Edits the AI can make

The AI looks at the open image and edits it with editor tools that Chickxel provides. The tools are grouped the same way as What the AI can do in the chat help.

The picture shows one "draw a staff" turn. The AI ① looked at the image with the view tool, ② drew lines and an ellipse with the drawing tools, and the result went onto ③ a new layer it created (Staff).

Tool list

CategoryWhat it doesTools
Look at the drawingReads the open document's size, frames, and layers, views it as an image, or reads exact pixel colors.get_document get_image get_pixels
Generate image → pixelateDraws characters, items and animations (all frames on one sheet) with Codex image generation and converts them to pixel art. Choose the mode and options in Drawing settings in the header.generate_pixel_art
DrawPaints blocks of pixels or single pixels, lines, rectangles, ellipses, fills, clears, and replaces colors.draw_pixels set_pixels draw_shape fill clear replace_color
Frames and animationAdds frames (optionally as copies) or deletes them, and sets frame durations and animation tags. Links cels across frames, unlinks them and recolors links.add_frame delete_frame set_frame_duration add_tag link_cels unlink_cels set_link_color
LayersAdds layers, changes their name, visibility, opacity, and pivot, and picks the frame and layer shown in the editor.add_layer set_layer set_current
Groups and partsSplits a character into parts such as head, body, and arms, and sets each part's position and size.add_group add_part set_part
Skins and outfitsCreates alternative drawings of the same parts (such as clothes), switches between them, and saves or applies outfit presets.list_skins add_skin set_skin edit_skin list_outfits save_outfit apply_outfit
Layer effectsAdds, changes, and removes layer effects that leave the drawing untouched, such as outlines, shadows, glow, hit flashes, shaking, and appear/disappear. When asked, it also applies them permanently or enlarges the canvas to fit them.list_effect_types get_effects add_effect set_effect remove_effect apply_effects expand_canvas_for_effects
Effect makingWrites script effects (JavaScript) for looks the installed effects (the official effects and your packages) can't make and checks them on the preview chick. Saves a finished script as a personal script effect and opens it in the Effect Maker (you upload it yourself). While the Effect Maker is open it can read, rewrite and validate the source in its editor.get_script_api write_script_effect preview_effects save_script_effect list_script_effects get_script_effect_source get_effect_maker_source set_effect_maker_source check_effect_maker
Canvas and colorsChanges the canvas size, the canvas (export) pivot and its representative layer, the palette colors, and the primary and secondary colors.resize_canvas set_canvas_pivot set_palette set_colors
FilesCreates a new drawing, sets the temporary title of an unsaved new drawing, saves, and opens files (opening and creating are blocked while "Current file only" is on).new_project set_document_title save_project open_file
ReferencesSearches the images, notes and style rules in your reference folder and the user guide, saves the current drawing as a reference, and captions references.search_references get_reference get_style_rules add_reference set_reference_info
Other toolsAsks you with a question card above the input box when a decision is needed.ask_user

Tool lines

The end of each ①② tool line shows in parentheses how long that tool took (e.g. get_image (22s), get_image (1m 20s)). For a failed tool it follows the error text, and it stays when you reopen the chat. A tool that is still running shows no time yet.

Linked cels

Ask AI 'link frames 4–8' or 'make that link green' to manage linked cels with link_cels, unlink_cels and set_link_color. get_document reports linked frames and colors per layer and group. Unlink results report both unlinked cels and effect end appearances baked into art.

Saving and temporary titles

It saves only when asked. If a never-saved new project has changes, it asks whether to save before switching to another document. When it draws on an unsaved new image that has no temporary title, the AI gives it a short title in your language (the title of new_project, or set_document_title — never for documents that have a file). It can change that image's temporary title even with Current file only on.

Generate image → pixelate

Ask the Codex tab for something like "draw a knight with a sword", and instead of placing pixels one by one the AI calls the generate_pixel_art tool. Chickxel runs the Codex CLI on your computer (codex exec, Codex image generation) with your ChatGPT login (no API key) to generate a high-quality image, then turns it into pixel art with the main preset of the Pixelate AI images window as saved (auto optimize included) and inserts it. Each image takes about 30–90 seconds.

The drawing settings panel

  • Drawing settings: the ① Drawing settings button (sliders icon) next to the question mark button at the top of the chat window opens a settings panel on the right, like the help panel. Only one of help and drawing settings is open at a time, and while it's open the button shows a red X. The panel also shows the ② Codex CLI status (logged in / not installed / not logged in).
  • Hover over an option or its ③ info icon for the longer explanation. Generate image shows only the generation options, Draw pixels directly shows only Use palette colors only, and Auto shows both.
  • Drawing mode: ④ Auto (default) in the Codex tab edits the current picture by placing pixels when the chat's Current file only is on, and otherwise tries Generate image → pixelate first. If it can't generate (no Codex CLI or a generation error), the same request is drawn with the pixel tools, followed by the same whole-picture review as Draw pixels directly. If you press Stop during generation, it doesn't fall back to drawing pixels. ⑤ Generate image → pixelate always generates and converts, and ⑥ Draw pixels directly has the AI place pixels one by one with the pixel tools. If you choose ⑤ or ⑥ yourself, that mode is kept. A Generate image setting saved by an older version switches to Auto once; a saved Draw pixels directly is kept.
    • ⑦ Use palette colors only (under Draw pixels directly — shown for Auto and Draw pixels directly, off by default, saved): when on, the colors the AI paints with the pixel tools (the draw_pixels legend, set_pixels, draw_shape, fill, replace_color's target color, the new_project background) are changed to the nearest color of the active palette (the same color distance as the Pixelate tool). Fully transparent (erasing) is still allowed; semi-transparent colors become the nearest opaque palette color. The tool result tells the AI what changed, e.g. "팔레트 색으로 맞춤: #E63228→#C81E1E", and each message also sends the palette colors (up to 64) so the AI draws with them from the start. It works in both the Claude and Codex tabs and doesn't affect generated images (the conversion's palette settings) or layer effects. When off, all colors and alpha are used as before.
  • Whole-picture review when drawing pixels: with Draw pixels directly (including Auto's fallback), the AI first reads your words and attached images together, lists the subject, size, proportions, pose, colors, style and anything to avoid, and plans the overall layout and where each part goes. Without an image, it works from the conditions you described. When a turn that changed pixels or parts ends, Chickxel automatically sends the AI the current canvas with all layers combined for a review. The AI scores the silhouette, limb placement and style out of 10 against your original request and images, fixes what's wrong, and looks at the whole picture again (up to three rounds of fixes; if it doesn't improve, it explains the remaining problems). The score is the AI's subjective judgment, not an exact similarity measurement. The review and fixes belong to the same undo step as the turn, and the extra AI turns take more time and usage. Generate image → pixelate doesn't use this review.

Generation options

Generation optionWhat it does
⑧ Automatic promptOn by default. On: the subject the AI wrote is wrapped with pixel-art style, background, sprite-sheet layout and pose instructions. Off: your chat message is sent to Codex image generation exactly as written (attached images in order, and the image just drawn after them when you ask to continue it) to make a single image, which is converted to pixel art without the size check, style reference or splitting into animation frames. Result-size correction, Remove background, auto-optimize and Insert as still apply; Style reference, Generation and the default frame counts are dimmed. The Generation prompt card below shows the text that was actually sent.
⑨ Result-size correctionOff by default (older saved settings also load as off). When off, generated images are converted with the main preset's result-size setting (with Auto, at the pixel grid found in the image), and a size you mention in the chat (e.g. "64×64") or one Codex picks for the subject before generating is only passed to the image generation as a rough scale hint. The same goes for a turn that converts an attached picture to pixel art. When on, the result is fitted to the Result size setting or the size given in the chat instead of the main preset's output size. View conversion settings opens with the conversion settings that made the result.
⑩ Result size32 · 48 · 64 · Custom (long side in px). Only enabled when Result-size correction is on.
⑪ Remove backgroundOff (default): converts with the main preset's background setting. On: removes the background automatically when converting, whatever the preset says. The old value (which was on by default) is not carried over, so it starts off.
⑫ Auto-optimize the conversionOn by default. Generated images are converted with the saved settings of the AI image conversion preset — the preset whose Main button is green in the Auto optimize settings window — including background, palette, output size and alignment (only Result-size correction and Remove background above override them when on); only the frame split follows the generated frames (one image is not split, several use automatic split with the expected frame count, falling back to the sheet layout grid). On: like Auto optimize in the Pixelate window, tries settings within the main preset's per-section optimization, trials and time and inserts the top-scoring result (regardless of that preset's Auto optimize checkbox). Off: converts once with those settings (same as Clean up). If the main preset is deleted, the built-in Same hue family preset is used while it exists, otherwise the first preset in the list. The progress reads "Optimizing conversion… pass p · trial n/N · best x". If the top result is a failed one (empty or too small — wrong pixel size detected), nothing is inserted and an error is reported.
⑬ Insert asNew layer · New document.
⑭ Style referenceAuto · None · Specific reference. A pixel-art example sent along with the generation. Auto picks one from your references (your own; built-in examples only when turned on) when you attach a non-pixel character picture to redraw as pixel art, or when a reference matches what is being drawn.
⑮ Default animation framesIdle 4 · Walk 6 · Run 6 · Attack 6 · Jump 6 · Hurt 3 · Death 5 (2–12). A count you give in the chat wins.
⑯ Generation (Animation group)All on one sheet (recommended) (default) · One image per frame (the previous behavior: one frame at a time with frame 1 and the previous frame attached). Choosing one image per frame shows a red warning: per-frame generation often changes the character's look and size between frames and takes as long as the number of frames.
  • Inserting (⑬): as a New layer in the current document (one undo step together with the AI's turn) or as a New document. If the generated image is larger than the open canvas, you're asked first whether to expand the canvas or choose a crop position (see Answering the AI's questions).
  • Background: unless you mention a background, the image is generated on a plain transparent background with no scenery.
  • The options are saved in the settings.

Animations and reference images

  • Animations: ask something like "make a walking animation" and the frames are planned (built-in key poses for each action, or poses the AI chooses), then by default generated in one go as a single sprite sheet: one Codex image generation draws every frame of the same character side by side in one row (from 7 frames a 2-row grid, e.g. 4×2) in one wide image, with the same scale, camera and baseline, a transparent background and no labels or grid lines (the prompt lists each frame's pose). The ⑰ chat progress line shows "Generating sprite sheet (4 frames, Codex)…". The sheet is split automatically by empty columns and rows, and re-split as a grid (N×1, or columns×rows) if that gives a different frame count. The frames are converted together on one canvas, and conversion settings such as alignment, palette and outline come from the main preset as saved, for a jump too. Sheets and per-frame images are converted at the size the generator returned, without shrinking (only if the joined frames would pass 16384 px are all frames shrunk by the same whole factor), so the result matches running the same image through the main preset in the Pixelate window. Animations with many frames take longer to convert and optimize. With One image per frame the progress line shows "Generating frame 2/6 (Codex)…" instead. ⑱ Stop cancels it.
  • Turning a reference image into pixel art: in the Codex tab with Auto, attaching a picture and asking to turn it into pixel art always generates an image and then pixelates it, even if you give a size. The size to generate is, in this order, the size you give in the chat, the result-size correction size if that's on, or a square size between 8 and 256 px that Codex picks before generating by looking at the attached picture (or the subject description); meanwhile the progress line shows "Selecting a pixel size from the reference image (Codex)…". With result-size correction off (the default), this size is only a hint for generation and the conversion uses the main preset's output size. The chosen size is written at the start of the generation prompt and in its style, composition and pixel-grid rules, and the prompt asks to keep the attached picture's character, pose, outfit and colors while drawing with an even pixel grid, a limited palette, crisp outlines and cel shading. For an animation sheet each frame has that size, so the whole sheet can be larger.

Result card

  • ⑲ Result card: when it's done, the chat shows a thumbnail (frames side by side), the insert message a ⑳ View conversion settings button and, next to it, an Add as new layer button. It opens the Pixelate AI image window with the generated sheet (or per-frame images) and the split and conversion settings used, so you can tweak them and insert again. Generation records are kept in <settings folder>/Chickxel/AiDraw/ (the latest 40; the sheet as sheet.png, per-frame images as frame-01.png…).

In the Claude tab

Claude tab: Claude can't create images, so it always draws pixels directly. In the drawing settings a ㉑ red alert icon appears next to the generate option, with the tooltip "Claude can't create images, so it draws pixels directly. Image generation is available in the Codex tab." If you explicitly ask Claude to "let Codex do it", it calls generate_pixel_art and that drawing is generated with Codex (Codex CLI must be installed and logged in).

Generation prompt card

  • ㉒ Generation prompt card: when Codex image generation starts, the chat shows a card with your original request on one line. Click it to expand ㉓ the full generation instruction that was actually sent; the card stays when you reopen the saved chat.

What the AI can do in the chat help (question mark button) has a Generate image → pixelate group (generate_pixel_art).

Asking for layer effects

The AI can use the same layer effects as the Effects dialog. Effects don't change the drawing's pixels, so you can still change or remove them later in the Effects dialog or the ① timeline.

Asking for an effect

  • ② Ask things like "add a 1 px black outline to the character", "add a white hit flash on frames 3–4", "make it appear over the first 4 frames", or "put a shadow 2 px to the lower right".
  • Animated effects (hit flash, blink, shake, appear, and so on) play over the frame range you give. Without a range they cover the whole animation, as in the Effects dialog.
  • Effects are picked by ID (short IDs such as outline or hitflash; asset IDs for received effects and your own script effects) or by display name. The old built-in effect type IDs (chickxel.effect.outline and so on) still work and add the same official script effect (from the source built into the app if you deleted that official effect). Your own and received effect presets can be added by name too.
  • It can also change the settings, frame range, on/off state, order, and clipping of existing effects.
  • Applying effects permanently (baking them into the pixels) and enlarging the canvas to fit effects that spill outside it happen only when you ask (the effects dialog doesn't grow the canvas on OK by itself either).

Effect changes (③) are part of the AI's reply, so one undo step (④) undoes them too. Effects on locked layers are never changed.

Making new effects with scripts

When no installed effect can do it, the AI writes a script effect itself. For example: "Make an effect that flashes red and breaks into pieces when hit, as a script, and save it as my effect." These tools are in the Effect Maker group of the chat help.

  • get_script_api reads the script rules and API together. write_script_effect writes a script effect on a layer, and preview_effects shows the result. Scripts written by the AI run without asking, like source written on this computer (same sandbox and limits). If the source has an error, nothing is changed.
  • save_script_effect saves a layer's script effect as your own script effect and opens it in the Effect Maker window. list_script_effects and get_script_effect_source only read the list and source of your script effects.
  • get_effect_maker_source, set_effect_maker_source and check_effect_maker read, replace and check the editor source only while the Effect Maker window is open.
  • Without being asked, the AI adds useful settings on its own (anchor point, direction, speed, repeat, range, color, strength, size and so on) and briefly lists them in its reply. Effects that read another layer are built with a layer-picker setting. When it uses colors, it picks them per pixel by default so gradients work, and the results of check_effect_maker, write_script_effect and set_effect_maker_source may include a gradient hint (recommended).
  • Publishing is up to you; the AI can't publish effects online.

References: teach the AI your style

References are a library on your computer that the AI looks through before drawing. Put in art you like, your own style rules and technique notes, and the AI finds similar images, follows their proportions and palette, and checks its result against the rules when it's done. It searches this user guide too.

The references window

Open the AI references window with AI > References…. On the left are ① thumbnails (pixel-perfect on a checker background, with title and tags), a ② search box (the same search the AI uses) and a ③ kind filter; on the right are a ④ preview (animated when there are several frames), ⑤ editable title, description, tags and kind, and the ⑥ palette swatches.

ButtonWhat it does
⑦ Add files…Adds several image or note files at once. You can also drag files onto the window.
⑧ Add current canvasAdds the open image as a reference.
⑨ DeleteMoves the selected reference file to the app trash (built-in references can't be deleted).
⑩ Open folderOpens the references folder in Explorer/Finder.
⑪ Edit rulesOpens your own style rules, rules.md (created from a template if missing). The AI reads them together with the default rules.
⑫ Caption with AIPuts a request to add titles, descriptions and tags to uncaptioned references into the input box of the active AI tab and brings up the chat window. It's sent only when you press send, so nothing is spent automatically.

When some references have no description, the window shows ⑬ "N references have no description". Descriptions and tags help the AI find them.

Using references in the chat

  • Asking: say something like "Draw a 32px bat like the slime in the references." and the AI looks up the references before drawing. The chat help (question-mark button) has a References tool group and this example.
  • Adding from the chat: say "save this to my references" and the AI adds the current canvas (or a layer or frame range) with a title, description and tags. Adding and captioning references only write to the library folder, so they work even with Current file only on.
  • Looking it up on the web: for things that aren't in your references, ask directly, for example "look it up on the web and draw it", and the AI searches the web and draws from what it reads (Claude: web search and web fetch; Codex: web search). It never searches unprompted, even when the references have nothing; instead it suggests attaching an image or asking for a web search. Web results are text, so the AI can't see pictures on the pages and draws from the descriptions it read. Searches show in the chat as tool lines (WebSearch, WebFetch, web_search).

Folder and files

  • Folder (⑭): by default <settings folder>/Chickxel/References (%AppData%\Chickxel\References on Windows). Files you put into this folder outside the app are picked up automatically when the window opens.
  • Supported files: images (.png, .gif with frames, .chickxel projects — each frame composited, animations keep their frame count) and text notes (.md, .txt). Title, description, tags, kind (character, item, tile, effect, UI, other), size, frame count, palette and similar info are stored in index.json in the same folder.
  • Built-in references (marked Built-in, can't be deleted): the style rule set Chickxel default rules (1 px or selective outline, a single light direction, 3–4-shade hue-shifted ramps, no orphan or jaggy pixels, a limited palette, a readable silhouette, consistent proportions, and key poses, smears and walk-cycle contacts for animation) and example images — a 16×16 slime, a 16×16 sword, a 32×32 front-facing character, a 4-frame 16×16 slime bounce, and a 16×16 grass tile.
  • Preferences: in Edit > Preferences… > AI references you can ⑮ Change… the ⑭ folder, go back to the ⑯ Default folder, ⑰ Open folder, and turn the built-in example images back on with ⑱ Show built-in examples (they are off by default for now because of their low quality; while off they are not used for search or as image-generation style references).

References stay on your computer. Chickxel uploads and downloads nothing; only the AI CLI you already use (Claude Code or Codex) sees what the AI fetches with its tools.

Undoing

Each AI reply is one undo step, so Ctrl+Z undoes it all at once. Each reply also comes with a ① result image and ② Undo this step / ④ Reapply buttons.

Adding a chat image as a new layer

Images in the chat (the result image after each reply, image generation result cards and images in reopened past chats) have an ③ Add as new layer button below them (next to Undo this step for reply images, next to View conversion settings on result cards). It adds the image as a new layer AI chat image on the current frame of the current document, centered on the canvas, in transform mode. Move or resize it and press Enter (one undo step; a floating selection or canvas size mode is applied first). With no document open, it opens as a new document in a new tab. The result shows in a bubble above the button and in the status bar; while the AI is working you're only told to try again when it's done.

Limiting edits to the current file

Turn on ① Current file only next to Attach canvas below the input box before sending, and the AI edits only the file that's open when you send.

  • Inside that file it can draw, add layers, frames, groups, parts, skins, and layer effects, and tidy up layers.
  • Opening or creating other files, such as new projects or opening files, is refused (③ red line; saving still works).
  • Tools that only write outside the document, such as adding or captioning references, saving a script effect and replacing the Effect Maker source, still work.
  • If you switch to another document tab while the AI is working, every tool except the reading tools is refused until that turn ends.
  • The chat shows the scope (②), like Edit scope: current file "slime.chickxel" only. Turn it off to lift the limit from the next message.

Protecting locked layers

The AI never touches locked layers (or the parts of a locked group), whether or not Current file only is on.

  • Changing their pixels, name, visibility, opacity, part position or size, adding, changing, removing, or permanently applying their layer effects, or changing the group's skin or outfit is refused with a "locked" reason (①).
  • Deleting a frame where a locked layer has pixels, and resizing the canvas (including enlarging it to fit effects) while any layer is locked, are refused too. Reading still works.
  • Lock layers the AI must not change with the ② lock icon in the timeline.

Troubleshooting

When something goes wrong, first press Check all again in the AI Status and Settings window. Each row shows whether installation, sign-in or the connection is the problem.

When the AI can't reach the editor

If the AI CLI doesn't receive the tool list from Chickxel's MCP server, the chat shows a ① red line. It tells you, from the requests the server received, at which stage the connection stopped, and ends with the path of the CLI that tried to connect; compare it with the path in the AI Status window. When the MCP connection row in the AI Status window fails, it shows the same explanation plus the end of the CLI's error output.

MessageMeaningWhat to check
No request arrivedThe CLI couldn't send a single request to Chickxel's internal server (the 127.0.0.1 address).Check whether proxy environment variables (HTTP_PROXY, HTTPS_PROXY, NO_PROXY), a VPN or Windows security software block loopback connections on this PC.
The auth token did not matchRequests arrived but the server rejected them because the token was wrong (HTTP 401).The authorization header in the temporary MCP config passed to the CLI wasn't applied. Update the CLI with Install or update and check again.
A request reached the server, but tools/list did notAuthentication passed, but the connection stopped during initialization.Look at the HTTP stages and response codes under the message. GET: HTTP 405 is the normal answer because SSE isn't used, and even POST initialize: HTTP 200 doesn't mean the CLI accepted the initialization response. Try updating the CLI.

If requests arrived, the line below the message lists the server's recent requests with their stage and response code, like POST initialize: HTTP 200, GET: HTTP 405 (up to the last 8). Stages are request names such as POST initialize, POST notifications/initialized, POST tools/list and POST tools/call.

The editor connection uses a temporary MCP config that the app passes straight to the CLI each time it runs; it's separate from MCP servers registered in your own Claude settings. It's normal for your settings to have no chickxel server.

When the CLI path changes

If the CLI location changes during a chat (install or update, changing the executable), "The CLI path changed. Reconnecting the session; send your message again after the check." is shown instead of sending, and it checks again. Send your message again once the check is done.

What looks different with Codex

Codex replies appear all at once after they're fully written, and show only the ② time, not the cost (Claude shows the cost too).

If you distribute the app

If you distribute an app with this feature to others, check Anthropic's and OpenAI's usage policies (restrictions on using subscription logins in third-party apps).

Shortcuts

Every menu command and every key outside the menus, grouped by menu. The keys in the tables are the defaults; None marks commands without a default key. You can assign any key to any command in Customizing shortcuts.

The in-app guide (F1 or Help > Guide…) shows the keys you currently use instead of the defaults in these tables. On macOS read Ctrl as ⌘ and Alt as ⌥.

Customizing shortcuts

All shortcuts are changed in the Shortcuts tab of Preferences. Besides menu commands you can change tools, drawing options and keys that aren't in any menu, and changed keys show up right away in menus, toolbar tooltips and the in-app guide. You can export the settings to a JSON file and use them on another computer.

Shortcuts tab

In the ① Shortcuts tab of Edit > Preferences… (Ctrl+,) you can change not only menu commands, tools and tool options but also keys that aren't in any menu (drop selection Enter, arrow-key nudging, peek previous frame H, selection slot M, etc.).

  • For each row choose ② Change (use the key you press), ③ Add (one more key), ④ None or ⑤ Default. After Change or Add, the row ⑥ waits for a key (Esc cancels). Pressing mouse button 4 or 5 (Back/Forward) then assigns that button together with any modifier keys (for example Ctrl+Mouse4). The left, right and middle buttons and the wheel can't be assigned.
  • ⑦ Search by command, category or key, and reset everything at once with ⑧ Reset all.
  • ⑨ Export (JSON)… saves the current shortcuts to a file, and ⑩ Import (JSON)… applies them again. The same file works on both Windows and macOS.

Keys already in use

If you assign a key used by another command, ⑪ you're asked whether to remove it from that command.

Checking changed keys

Changed keys appear right away in menus (see ⑫), toolbar tooltips and the in-app guide. ⑬ Help > Shortcut list shows a table of the default keys and mouse actions, not your changed keys.

Editing and selection

The undo, clipboard and selection commands at the top of the Edit menu, plus the drop-selection and arrow-key nudging keys that aren't in any menu.

Keys outside the menus

CommandDefault keyWhat it does
Drop selectionEnterDrops a floating selection (pasted or moved pixels) in place. While transforming, applies the transform.

Move 1pxCtrl+Shift+Left Ctrl+Shift+Right Up DownMoves the selection (the cel's pixels with the Move tool) by 1 pixel. Plain ←→ go to the previous and next frame, so for sideways moves add Ctrl+Shift. Keys in the menu

The Edit menu shows the keys from Undo to Deselect at ①.

Transform and image

The flip, rotate, transform, size and palette commands in the lower part of the Edit menu, plus the keys that finish transform and size modes.

Menu commands

CommandDefault keyWhat it does
Flip horizontal (selection/cel) / Flip vertical (selection/cel)Shift+H / Shift+VFlips the selection, or the current cel if nothing is selected.
Rotate 90° (selection/canvas) / Rotate -90° (selection/canvas)Ctrl+R / Ctrl+Shift+RRotates the selection, or the whole canvas if nothing is selected.
Transform (rotate/size)…Ctrl+TStarts Transform mode (blue box) for rotating, scaling and moving the current cel with handles. Ctrl+drag a handle for free distort, and right-click the box for a rotate/flip menu. Right-dragging moves only the image inside the box (the pivot stays), changes the margin on a handle, and rotates around the box center outside the border.
Add outline (primary color)Ctrl+Alt+ODraws a 1-pixel outline in the primary color around the art.
Add outline (with diagonals)NoneDraws an outline that also fills the diagonal corners.
Replace color…Ctrl+Alt+RReplaces one color with another.
Canvas size…Ctrl+Alt+CStarts Canvas size mode.
Transform whole layer…Alt+Shift+CStarts Transform whole layer, which rotates, scales and moves every frame of the current layer (every skin of a part) together with one orange box. The canvas size stays the same.
Flip entire canvas horizontally (all frames) / Flip entire canvas vertically (all frames)None / NoneFlips every layer and frame together.
Create palette from imageNoneMakes a palette from the colors used in the image (see Palette).
Convert to palette colors…NoneConverts the image's colors to the nearest colors of the current palette (see Recoloring with a palette).
Preferences…Ctrl+,Opens Preferences, where you also change shortcuts.

In transform and size modes

CommandDefault keyWhat it does
Apply transform / canvas size / whole-layer transformEnterApplies Transform, Canvas size or Transform whole layer as one undo step.
Cancel transform / canvas size / whole-layer transformEsc / Ctrl+ZLeaves everything as it was.

In Canvas size mode, dragging a handle stretches the image along with the canvas, and right-dragging changes only the margin and leaves the image as it is. In both, Shift keeps the ratio, Alt works from the center and Ctrl moves corners freely. In Transform and Transform whole layer, drag inside to move, drag a handle to resize (Shift keeps the ratio, Alt from the center), and Ctrl+drag a handle for free distort.

Keys in the menu

The same keys appear at ① in the Edit menu, from the flips to Canvas size.

Zoom in / Zoom outCtrl+= / Ctrl+-Zooms the canvas in or out one step. The numeric keypad +/- work too.
Fit to window / Actual size (1x)Ctrl+0 / Ctrl+1Fits the canvas to the window or shows it at 1×.
Pixel gridCtrl+GDraws a line around every pixel. Right-click the top bar button for its settings (see Menu and top bar).

Tiled mode (repeat view)Ctrl+Shift+TRepeats the image around itself so you can check the seams.

Onion skinCtrl+JOverlays neighboring frames translucently.

File browserF8Shows or hides the file browser on the left.
Movement testF5Opens the window where you move a character around with its tag animations.

When a tab row or an option group such as off/on has focus, you can also switch with ←/→ and Home/End.

Keys in the menu

Keys for the Frame·Layer menu, which handles frames, layers, groups, skins and tags, plus the mouse actions used in the timeline.

With the timeline grid focused they move the cel selection instead, and in a canvas moving mode they nudge by 1px.
First frame / Last frameHome / EndGoes to the first or last frame (when the canvas has focus).
New empty frameAlt+NAdds an empty frame.
Duplicate frameAlt+DAdds a copy of the current frame.
Delete frameNoneDeletes the current frame.
New layerShift+NAdds an empty layer.

Keys outside the menus and the mouse

CommandDefault keyWhat it does
Tap Space to play/pause previewSpaceA short tap of Space plays or stops the preview; holding it while dragging pans the view.

Scroll frames left/right (in the timeline)Ctrl+wheelScrolls the timeline's frames sideways.
Show only this layerAlt+click the eyeHides every other layer and keeps only this one (clicking a group keeps its parts; clicking a part keeps its group too). If that is already the state, all layers become visible again. One undo step.
Lock all other layersAlt+click the lockLocks every other layer and leaves only this one unlocked (groups and parts are kept together as with the eye). If that is already the state, all layers are unlocked. One undo step.

Skin switching applies to the current group (or the selected part's group) and isn't recorded in undo history. For groups, parts and skins, see the Parts and groups chapter.

Keys in the menu

Tools

Tool keys in the Tools menu, keys in its Select and Drawing options submenus, and modifier keys you hold while drawing. Click a tool name to open its page.

Language

Chickxel includes Korean and English. You can add other languages yourself with a translation CSV file, and edit the built-in text with a CSV too. The display language is changed in Preferences and takes effect after a restart.

PageRead it when
Changing the display languageYou switch the display to Korean, English or a language you added
Creating a new languageYou translate Chickxel into a language that isn't in the list
Editing and removing built-in textYou edit built-in text or remove a language you added

Changing the display language

Choose the display language at the top of the General tab in Edit > Preferences…. A new language takes effect after Chickxel restarts.

Choosing a language

  • Open ① Edit > Preferences… (Ctrl+,). ② Language is at the top.
  • Choose from the ③ Display language list and click ④ OK. Languages added from a CSV appear as Name (code), and built-in languages edited by a CSV as Name (edited by CSV).

Restarting to apply

  • Click ⑤ Restart now to close the app and reopen it in the new language. If there are unsaved documents, you're asked first, just like when closing. Choose ⑥ Later to apply it the next time you launch.

The language on first launch

On first launch Chickxel picks the language that matches your computer's UI language. It looks for an exact match including the region first (for example pt-BR), then the parent language (pt). If nothing matches, it starts in English. It picks again the same way if the file of the CSV language you chose has been removed.

What the language changes

ItemLanguage shown
Menus, windows and messagesThe chosen language; untranslated text uses the same built-in language, or English
Guide (F1)Korean when Korean is chosen, otherwise English
Changes in the update dialogNotes in the chosen language, or English (see Updates)
Help > Website and the Website button in the About windowThe Korean website for Korean, otherwise the English website
Number and date formatsUnchanged (they follow your computer's settings)

Creating a new language

To make a new language, export the translation CSV, add a language column, translate it and add the file back. Edit the CSV in a spreadsheet such as Excel or Google Sheets. You can add a partial translation; empty cells show English.

Exporting and translating

  1. Save a CSV file with ① Export translation CSV… in Preferences. You can open it in Excel, Google Sheets and so on.
  2. The first row is key,ko,en and the second row is #name,한국어,English. Add a new column at the far right with the language code in the first row (e.g. ja) and the language name in the second row (e.g. 日本語).
  3. Translate the text in that column. Braces such as {0} and {name} are placeholders for values, so keep them as they are (you may change their order).

CSV layout

RowContent
First rowkey and the language codes (key,ko,en,…). A language code uses letters, digits, - and _, up to 32 characters.
Second row#name and the name shown in the list for each language (#name,한국어,English,…)
Other rowsThe text key (<table>.<key>, e.g. UiStrings.Language_Label) and the text for each language. Don't change the keys.

The exported file is comma-separated UTF-8 (with BOM). Importing reads comma, semicolon and tab separators, so it's fine if Excel saves with semicolons because of your regional settings. One file can contain several language columns.

Adding and applying it

  • Choose the file with ② Add language from CSV… and it appears in the ③ language list. If you added just one new language, it is selected for you. Click OK and restart to apply it.
  • The number of strings added for each language appears below the list ("{name} ({code}): added {count} strings.").
  • Columns that aren't valid language codes are skipped and keys Chickxel doesn't have are ignored; the counts are reported.

Empty cells show the English text, so you can add your translation bit by bit. Adding the same language code again overwrites it. Added languages are saved as <settings folder>/Chickxel/Languages/<language code>.csv.

Editing and removing built-in text

You can override Korean or English text you don't like with a CSV, and undo added languages or edited text by deleting their file in the language folder.

Editing built-in text

Edit the ko or en column in an exported CSV and add it with Add language from CSV…: only the cells that differ from the built-in text are saved, and only those override it. If no cell differs, that language's CSV is removed.

Removing an added language

  • ① Open language folder opens the folder containing the CSV files of added languages. Deleting a file removes that language from the list, or, for a file that edited a built-in language, restores the original text.

Glossary

Chickxel terms used in this guide and what they mean. Click a term to open the page that explains it. In the guide text, the first mention of a term on each page links to the same place.

TermMeaning
9-sliceA Unity sprite border that splits an image into nine parts so its corners stay crisp when stretched.
account level (level chip)Your Chickxel account's level, which rises with the XP you earn by sharing and shows as an Lv chip next to your name.
active paletteThe palette you last clicked or edited; Edit-menu and AI palette tools use it.
Add rangeThe effect range given to effects newly added in the effects dialog.
AI chatThe window where you ask Claude or Codex to edit the drawing.
AI references (reference library)A library of images, notes and rules on your computer that the AI looks through before drawing.
AI Status window (AI Status and Settings)The window that checks CLI installation, version, sign-in and MCP connection, and installs or signs in.
AI tools (MCP tools)The editor functions Chickxel exposes over MCP for the AI to read and edit the image.
allowing scripts to run (allowed to run)Deciding that a script effect you haven't seen before may run on this computer.
animated effect (animated effects)An effect that changes over its effect range (flash, shake, explode and so on).
app trashChickxel's own trash that keeps deleted files and old export outputs for a while.
apply effect permanently (apply permanently, applied permanently)Bakes an effect into the cel pixels and removes it from the effect list.
Apply to frames (frame range)A brush tool setting that applies a stroke to the other frames in a chosen range at the same spot as on the current frame.
Asset ManagerThe window for installing, importing and publishing assets such as effects and gradients.
auto layoutA group setting that lines its parts up by size and re-lines them up whenever the parts change.
Auto optimizeSearches each checked preset for the highest-scoring values of the steps that have Optimize on.
autosaveSaves automatically after a short pause.
base positionThe part position shared by all frames; per-frame distances are added to it.
brush shape (brush size)The shape (square, circle, diamond) and size (1–32) that brush tools such as the pencil and eraser paint at once.
candidate tile (candidate row)A tile in the Pixelate window showing one preset's result and score; click to select it, double-click to apply it.
canvasThe central area where you view the current frame and draw.
canvas pivotThe canvas's own pivot used for sheet and Unity exports; with a representative layer it follows that layer's pivot.
canvas size modeA mode for setting the new canvas size and image position directly on the canvas.
cel (cels)The drawing of one layer in one frame — a single timeline cell.
cell name (cell names)The sprite name of each sheet cell (by frame number or by tag).
Chickxel accountThe chickxel.com account used for gallery and asset uploads (sign in with Google, GitHub or Discord).
Choose help on screen (help picker)Press F1, then click a button or panel on screen to open its guide page.
Claude CodeAnthropic's command-line AI tool. Signed in with a Claude subscription, it powers the Claude tab of AI chat.
clipping (clip to effect above)A setting that applies an effect only to the pixels made by the effect above it.
Codex CLIOpenAI's command-line AI tool. Signed in with a ChatGPT subscription, it powers the Codex tab and image generation.
color decisionThe row in the enlarged view that shows how one pixel's color was decided at each step.
color distanceHow closeness between two colors is measured (OKLCh hue-weighted, CIEDE2000, OKLab), used when cells pick palette colors.
color pickerThe panel that sets the color being edited with a square, honeycomb, wheel or sliders.
color spaceThe color model used to blend a smooth gradient (RGB, HSL, OKLab, OKLCH).
Contiguous onlyA setting that limits fill and the magic wand to the area connected to the clicked pixel.
convert to palette colorsReplaces the image's colors with the nearest palette colors.
copy images only (images-only copy, images-only copies)Copies timeline cels or frames as images only without their link groups.
Current file onlyA chat toggle that limits the AI to the image open when you send.
display languageThe language of menus, windows and messages; changing it takes effect after a restart.
ditheringMixing two colors in a dot pattern so they read as an in-between color; used by the dithering tool, gradients and palette conversion.
document tab (document tabs)A tab above the canvas for each open document.
Dodge/burn (Lighten/darken)A tool that rubs painted pixels lighter (left button) or darker (right button).
Draw pixels directlyA drawing mode where the AI places pixels one by one with the pixel tools.
Drawing settings (drawing settings)The chat panel that sets the drawing mode and generation options.
easingHow an animated effect speeds up and slows down over its range.
Effect MakerThe window for writing, previewing and publishing script effects.
effect paddingThe space an effect reserves outside the image to draw in (outline thickness, shadow offset and so on).
effect preset (effect presets)Several effects and their values saved together under a name.
effect range (effect ranges)The frames an effect applies to: all frames or a start–end frame.
effect row (effect rows, effect box)The row each effect gets under its layer in the timeline, with a box showing its range.
effects dialogThe dialog for adding effects to one layer, changing their order, settings and ranges, and previewing them.
end chickThe chick shown on the last frame of a transform effect's preview.
enlarged previewA resizable window that plays the preview enlarged.
eraserA tool that erases pixels to transparent (or by a strength and channels).
error log (error logs)Files describing unexpected errors; the latest 20 are kept in Logs in the settings folder.
excluded color (excluded colors)Removing one color from the selected result, replacing its pixels with the nearest remaining color.
Export as animated GIFThe dialog that makes a single GIF for sharing.
export cell name (export cell names, cell naming rule)The name of a cell (Unity sprite) in an exported sheet, set per frame or generated by the naming rule.
Export dialogThe dialog that sets up frame sheet and part atlas PNGs and Unity settings.
export on saveThe export dialog setting that creates the outputs (images, meta files) whenever you save; on for new projects.
export path encryptionWriting a project file's export folder and names locked with this install's own key (local-path-key.bin), so others can't see your folder paths.
export preset (export presets)Named export settings you can reuse in other projects.
Extend framesAn effects dialog setting that adds frames to an effect's range to create animation frames.
external AI connectionA setting that lets other AI apps use the running Chickxel's editor tools through Chickxel --mcp.
eyedropperA tool that picks a canvas color into the primary or secondary color.
fast pathThe execution path that converts and compiles a script's source to run it fast.
ffmpegAn external program used to open videos, AVIF and HEIC files; it isn't bundled with Chickxel and must be installed separately.
FidelityThe largest score term, measuring how closely the result matches the background-removed source cells.
file associationThe OS registration that makes double-clicking a .chickxel in Explorer or Finder open Chickxel.
file browserThe left panel that shows a folder's images as thumbnails and opens them.
first skin (base skin)The skin at the front of a group's skin list; it uses the plain cell names in the part atlas.
Fit outline to 1 pxHow a thick outer outline becomes 1 px: By shrinking, Thin outline only, Redraw as vector or Off.
fit to windowSwitches to the largest zoom that fits the whole image in the canvas area.
floating selectionSelected pixels that were lifted or pasted and not yet dropped.
frame (frames)One picture of the animation: a timeline column with its own duration (ms).
frame durationHow long one frame is shown (ms).
frame header (frame headers)The frame number cell in the timeline's top row; click it to select the frame.
frame sheetA PNG with every frame laid out in a grid.
frame splitThe step that splits a source with several frames into one frame each.
Generate image → pixelateA drawing mode that makes a picture with Codex image generation and converts it to pixel art.
gradient pencilA pencil that paints along the stroke in gradient colors.
gradient preset (gradient presets)A gradient saved under a name with its stops, interpolation, color space and dithering.
gradient stop (color stop, opacity stop)A point on the gradient bar that sets which color (or opacity) sits at which position.
gradient toolA tool that paints a linear gradient by dragging.
grid detectionFinding how many source pixels make one "pixel" and where the cell boundaries are.
group layer (group layers)A layer that bundles part layers into one character.
group viewThe canvas state with a group selected, where you see the frame composite and drag parts into place.
guide windowThe in-app window for searching and browsing this guide (Help > Guide…).
hue shiftA shading method that turns the hue toward yellow when lightening and toward blue/purple when darkening (the default lighten/darken mode).
image chip (image chips)The marker for an attached image inside the message text: a thumbnail and a #number.
Import SettingsThe Unity Texture Importer settings written to the .meta file.
Import videoThe window that extracts a video into frames after you set the range, frame rate and size.
keep the end lookKeeps an animated effect's final appearance on frames after its range.
keyboard shortcut (keyboard shortcuts)Keys that run a command directly; you can change them in Preferences.
language folderThe Languages folder in the settings folder where added languages and edited text are stored.
lasso select (lasso)A selection tool that selects the inside of an outline you draw.
layer (layers)One sheet you paint on. It is a timeline row; upper layers cover lower ones.
layer colorsThe colors used in every frame of the current layer, most used first.
layer effect (layer effects)Effects such as outline, shadow or flash that are layered on without changing the pixels.
layer pivot (layer pivots)The pivot a normal layer or group has, measured against the canvas; that layer's effects, transform rotation center and symmetry axis use it.
linked cels (cel link)A link that makes several cels share one drawing; editing one changes them all.
linked copy (linked copies)Copying timeline cels together with their link groups, so cels pasted into the same row join the original group.
local cell correctionRe-fits the image-wide cell lines band by band to the real color edges.
locked layer (locked layers)A layer locked with the timeline padlock so that drawing and AI edits can't change it.
magic wandA selection tool that selects the area of similar color around the click (same settings as Fill).
main presetThe preset used to pixelate images generated by AI drawing.
MCPModel Context Protocol, the connection the AI CLI uses to call Chickxel's editor tools.
merge filesCombines several images selected in the file browser into one .chickxel.
meta file (meta files)The Unity settings file (.meta) next to an exported image, holding sprite cells, pivots and the guid.
middle fillChooses whether the middle is stretched (Stretch) or its pattern repeated (Tile) when stretching with 9-slice.
move pivot alongMoves the center (pivot) of later effects by as much as a moving effect moved the image.
Move toolThe tool that moves the selection or the whole cel; Ctrl turns any tool into it while held.
my assetsAssets made on this computer or published with your account; you edit them in place.
nearest neighborA scaling and rotation method that uses the nearest original pixel without blending colors.
New project dialogThe dialog that creates a new document from a canvas size, start mode and background.
number field (number fields)A field you change by clicking to type, using the up/down buttons at its right end, or picking from its right-click list.
official effect (official effects)The 23 script effects the app installs by default (Outline, Shadow, Hit flash and so on).
onion skinA view option that overlays neighboring frames translucently.
Optimize criteriaThe per-preset budget (trials, seconds) and scoring rules for Clean up and Auto optimize.
outfit (outfit preset)The combination of skins chosen per group and part; it can be saved as a preset.
outfit rowThe row below the preview where you pick a skin for each group.
paletteA list of colors to paint with. You can stack several and switch between them.
palette web (Lospec)A window for browsing and importing palettes from lospec.com.
part atlasA PNG that packs part drawings one per cell.
part layer (part layers)A layer with its own size inside a group (head, body and so on).
part override (part overrides)A choice that dresses a single part in a different skin than its group.
part pivotThe pivot of a part's atlas sprite, measured against the part size.
part viewThe canvas state with a part selected, showing only that part at its own size with the rest dimmed.
part-only skin (part-only skins)A skin that exists for one part only (e.g. a hat for Head), worn only as that part's override.
peek previous frameShows the previous frame on the canvas while the key is held.
pencilThe basic drawing tool that paints pixels in the brush shape.
per-frame part position (per-frame part positions)A part placement that differs from the base position in a particular frame.
per-frame pivotA pivot (and for parts, position) kept separately from a given frame to the end with 'From current frame on'.
pick from screenA color popup button that picks a color anywhere on the screen, other apps included.
ping-pongA tag direction that plays to the end and back.
pinned color (pinned colors)A color picked from a source cell and added to the result palette; only pixels closer to it change.
pivotThe reference point for rotation and placement, exported as the Unity sprite pivot.
Pivot toolThe tool for setting the pivot by dragging on the canvas or with buttons and numbers.
pixel font (pixel fonts)A font drawn on a pixel grid; Galmuri, Unifont and others come with the app.
pixel gridA view option that outlines every pixel when zoomed in.
pixel perfectAn option that removes the doubled L-shaped pixels at stair corners when drawing at size 1, leaving a one-pixel line.
Pixel stepsA setting that joins the pixels of shape lines and curves, or of pencil, gradient pencil and eraser strokes, diagonally (Diagonal) or only sideways and up/down (Orthogonal); the Shape tool and the pencil remember it separately.
Pixelate AI image (Pixelate)The window that turns AI-made or large images into clean pixel art.
pixels outside the canvasPixels pushed past the canvas edge. They are kept and can be brought back.
Prefer shade familyPrefers a palette color of the cell's own hue family over a different hue of similar lightness.
preset parameter (preset parameters)A value in an effect preset that changes settings of several effects inside it together.
preview chickThe 20 numbered chick pictures that effect and preset previews apply the effect to.
preview position and pivotDeclaration values that move the end chick and each frame's pivot in effect previews.
preview sceneA small drawing used with the chick to preview effects that read other layers.
preview tabA green-tinted tab that plays the document being edited, enlarged, in the other pane.
primary color (Primary)The color painted with the left mouse button.
project file (project files)The .chickxel file that holds everything you edit: layers, frames, effects, export settings and more.
publish onlineThe window that publishes one of your assets to the Store and chickxel.com with your Chickxel account.
question cardThe card with options that appears above the input box when the AI asks you to decide.
received assets (received asset)Assets by others from the Store or files; copy one to My assets to change it.
recent colorsThe last 16 colors you painted with, newest first.
recent filesThe list of the last 10 opened or saved files, in the File menu and on the start screen.
recovery file (recovery files)A .chickxel saved separately for an unsaved document when an error forces the app to quit.
Replace color brush (color replace brush)A brush that, where you rub, changes only the color of the pixel where the stroke started.
representative layerThe layer whose pivot the canvas pivot follows in every frame (crown before its name).
resamplingHow rotated or scaled pixels take their colors from the original (RotSprite / Nearest).
Revert to savedA command that discards unsaved edits and rereads the file from disk.
Right button = eraserAn option that makes the right button of the pencil, gradient pencil, shape and fill tools erase instead of using the secondary color.
rotation centerThe point transform mode and Transform whole layer rotate around; it starts at the current layer's pivot.
RotSpriteA pixel-art rotation method that samples an 8× enlarged copy so rotated lines break less (no new colors).
score term (score breakdown)The terms, such as Fidelity and 1-px outline, and their weights that make up a candidate's 0–100 score.
script APIThe declaration, settings, img, ctx and other names and rules used in a script effect's source.
script effect (script effects)An effect written in JavaScript.
secondary color (Secondary)The color painted with the right mouse button (it erases when Right button = eraser is on).
seedA number that fixes the look of a random effect; the same seed always gives the same look.
selectionThe area chosen with a selection tool; painting, erasing and moving stay inside it.
settings folder (app settings folder)The Chickxel folder holding preferences, undo history, the app trash and logs (%AppData%\Chickxel on Windows).
shape pen (shape set)A drawing tool that stamps small pictures (leaves, flowers, sparkles and more) from the chosen set. You can add shapes to a set or share it as a file.
Shape toolA tool that drags out 11 shapes such as lines, curves, rectangles, ellipses and stars.
show effects on canvasA view option that decides whether layer effects are shown on the editing canvas.
skin (skins)One set of drawings for the same part structure (outfit, body type and so on).
skin chip (skin chips)The small label after a group or part name in the timeline showing the skin worn; click it to open the skin manager.
skin managerThe window that shows every group's skins and the outfit presets as cards for editing.
skin slot (skin slots)The place skins are swapped in, i.e. a group. Skins in one slot replace each other; slots stack.
split viewThe canvas divided into left and right panes showing two documents.
Sprite LibraryThe .spriteLib asset Unity uses to swap skins.
start screenThe screen shown with no document open, where you choose a folder, recent file or Unity Hub project.
status barThe bottom line showing coordinates, color, size and save state.
style rulesThe pixel-art rules the AI follows when drawing: the built-in rules plus your own rules.md.
symmetry drawing (horizontal symmetry, vertical symmetry)An option that also draws on the opposite side of the canvas center (or pivot) axis.
tag (tags)A named frame range that groups an animation (for example idle or run).
target colorsA color count above which (or, when Strict, below which) the score is reduced.
temporary MCP configThe MCP server address and token Chickxel passes straight to the CLI on each run, separate from your CLI settings.
text boxA text area dragged out with the Text tool; lines wrap within its width.
the Store (Store tab)The Asset Manager tab for finding and installing effects and gradients published on chickxel.com.
tile gridA view option that draws a line every tile of the chosen size.
tiled modeA view option that repeats the image around itself to check seamless patterns.
timelineThe bottom panel that shows frames (columns) and layers (rows) as cels.
toleranceHow different a color may be and still count as the same for fill and magic wand.
tool line (tool lines)A chat line showing an editor tool the AI called, its result and how long it took.
Tool options (tool options)The right-dock panel where you change the current tool's settings.
toolbarThe vertical bar on the far left for picking tools.
top barThe bar below the menus with the tool name, view options and zoom.
transform modeA mode for rotating, scaling and moving the selected pixels with handles.
transform whole layerA mode that rotates, scales, moves and distorts every frame of one layer (every skin of a part) together with one orange box.
translation CSVA spreadsheet file with every UI string as a key and one column per language; add a column to make a new language.
undo historyUndo steps that survive closing and reopening a file.
Undo this stepThe button under an AI reply that undoes everything that reply changed.
update dialog (automatic update)The dialog that shows a new version and its changes, then downloads, installs and restarts.
Upload to galleryThe window that uploads a picture to the chickxel.com gallery.
Use palette colors onlyA drawing setting that snaps the colors the AI paints to the nearest color in the current palette.
wrap aroundA Move tool option that brings pixels pushed off one side back on the other side.
XPPoints from gallery pictures, shared assets, likes, daily check-ins and app time that raise your account level.
zoom levelHow much the canvas is magnified (1–64×, whole steps).
